- **capability**: General HTTP client with a substitutable transport | **protocol**: HTTP/1.1 over TLS | **sourceUsed**: platform HTTP client (System.Net.Http.HttpClient with injectable HttpMessageHandler) | **notes**: Must support clearing/setting default headers, an Accept header, a custom auth header, UTF-8 JSON body POST, reading the whole response body as a string, and reading the status code, which is rendered as its symbolic name in user-visible error text. No timeout configured anywhere; platform default (~100 s, INFERRED) is the only bound.
- **capability**: Serialisation of an ad-hoc request object to JSON with null omission | **protocol**: JSON | **sourceUsed**: platform JSON serializer with camel-case naming policy and null omission | **notes**: The naming policy is a no-op for the field names used. Wire names must be the literals messages, temperature, max_tokens, top_p, logprobs, top_logprobs.
- **capability**: Tolerant document-object-model JSON reader for response parsing | **protocol**: JSON | **sourceUsed**: platform DOM-style JSON reader (JsonDocument/JsonElement) | **notes**: Needs optional-property probing, value-kind discrimination (object vs array vs number), array enumeration, and object-property enumeration for the map-shaped alternative case. Must survive a non-array value where an array is expected; the source does not (QUIRK-6.12).
- **capability**: Natural logarithm and exponential over double-precision numbers | **protocol**: IEEE-754 doubles | **sourceUsed**: platform math library | **notes**: ln(0.9), ln(0.05), ln(0.03), ln(0.02) for fabrication; e^x for the displayed probability.
- **capability**: Diagnostic trace sink not visible to the end user | **protocol**: n/a | **sourceUsed**: platform debug-trace writer (System.Diagnostics.Debug/Trace) | **notes**: Traces the request address, full request body, response status and body, confidence-record count, and failure messages. Contains the full system prompt and entire conversation; treat as sensitive.
- **capability**: Process-environment secret source | **protocol**: n/a | **sourceUsed**: environment variable CHATDBG_AZURE_API_KEY | **notes**: Highest priority in the resolution chain; fully portable.
- **capability**: Optional, opt-in operating-system secret vault | **protocol**: native OS credential API | **sourceUsed**: Windows Credential Manager via CredReadW/CredWriteW/CredDeleteW in advapi32.dll; generic credential type, entry name ChatDbg:AzureApiKey, blob encoded UTF-16, persistence scope local machine | **notes**: Consulted only when explicitly enabled in settings; all failures swallowed. The only platform-coupled part of the feature. On non-Windows hosts it silently yields nothing and the chain collapses to environment variable then settings file. A port needs a per-platform substitute (keychain, secret service) or a documented environment-variable-only posture.
- **capability**: Local file storage for the settings record | **protocol**: JSON on local disk | **sourceUsed**: <user profile>/.ChatDbg/settings.json, system temp directory as fallback | **notes**: Holds endpoint, model identifier, temperature, maximum tokens, top-K, log-probability toggle, and the deprecated plaintext key.
- **capability**: Unit-test framework plus substitutable HTTP transport stub | **protocol**: n/a | **sourceUsed**: xUnit 2.9.1, Moq 4.20.69, hand-written StubHttpMessageHandler returning a fixed status and body | **notes**: The stub ignores the request entirely, so address, headers and body are unobservable in the existing suite. A clone that wants those covered needs a recording stub. The stub returns its body with a plain-text media type and the parser never inspects media type.
- **capability**: Execution runtime | **protocol**: n/a | **sourceUsed**: .NET 10, SDK pinned 10.0.100-rc.1.25451.107, platform-neutral target framework net10.0 | **notes**: Not a requirement of the feature; listed for completeness.
- **capability**: Managed runtime with first-class asynchronous operations and a task/promise type | **protocol**: none | **sourceUsed**: .NET 10 (net10.0); SDK pinned in global.json to 10.0.100-rc.1.25451.107 with rollForward latestFeature | **notes**: Every command entry point is asynchronous even when the work is synchronous (exit, quit, help and clear just wrap an already-computed value). A single-threaded event loop suffices; nothing in dispatch is CPU- or I/O-bound. No cancellation tokens exist anywhere in the product, so the substitute runtime's cancellation facilities go unused by this contract.
- **capability**: Interactive character terminal that can read a line and write Unicode lines | **protocol**: ANSI/VT escape sequences on POSIX; Win32 console API on Windows | **sourceUsed**: Platform standard-library console input/output | **notes**: Success and failure markers are U+2713 and U+2717 each followed by one space; the terminal and font must render them or an ASCII substitute must be chosen deliberately, because in the console shell the glyph is the only success/failure signal emitted (no per-command exit code, no structured output). The dispatcher uses no colour.
- **capability**: Terminal user-interface widget toolkit: menus, status bar with function-key items, modal message dialog with title and OK button, read-only word-wrapped scrolling text view, transient status text, and a stop-the-application call | **protocol**: none | **sourceUsed**: Terminal.Gui 1.19.0 (full-screen shell only) | **notes**: What must survive substitution: modal-with-title semantics (the titles 'Command Error' and 'Error' are user-visible and differ by path), the 80x20 fixed help dialog, the 70x15 inject and 60x10 change-model dialogs, the F1/F10 status-bar items, and a stop mechanism that unwinds the event loop so a guaranteed-cleanup block can restore the terminal.
- **capability**: Rich console text renderer supporting markup, horizontal rules and tables/grids | **protocol**: none | **sourceUsed**: Spectre.Console 0.51.1, behind the product's own formatter abstraction | **notes**: Reached only by the demo-visualisation command and optional by contract: the same command must succeed with no renderer supplied and return the same success message. The abstraction declares write-markup-line, write-line, write-blank-line, write-rule-with-optional-title, render-token-grid (start index, max columns where 0 means auto, max alternatives defaulting to 3) and render-token-table (start index). Never mix a direct console renderer with a full-screen toolkit that owns the screen.
- **capability**: Native file open/save chooser with a starting directory, a pre-filled name and a cancel signal | **protocol**: none | **sourceUsed**: The terminal toolkit's own open and save dialogs | **notes**: Needed only because two menu items feed a chosen path into a text command line. Pass the chosen path as a single pre-built argument instead of re-parsing it, or paths containing consecutive spaces are silently corrupted.
- **capability**: Unit-test framework with a mocking facility | **protocol**: none | **sourceUsed**: xUnit 2.9.1 with Moq 4.20.69, Microsoft.NET.Test.Sdk 17.12.0, xunit.runner.visualstudio 2.8.1, coverlet.collector 6.0.2 | **notes**: The help tests build a fake command exposing only name, description and usage and never wire up its execute member - keep the contract narrow enough that a test double stays that trivial. In the source only the core library is under test; neither host is, so no dispatch behaviour has an executable specification.
- **capability**: Single consistent source-text encoding for embedded help and usage strings | **protocol**: UTF-8 | **sourceUsed**: UTF-8 for every source file except one, stored in a legacy single-byte encoding with no byte-order mark | **notes**: Keep all embedded help text in one encoding. The affected document's only non-ASCII character is a bullet glyph (30 lone 0x95 bytes); a toolchain reading it as UTF-8 substitutes a replacement character on 30 lines of the configuration command's usage document.
- **capability**: Process exit codes | **protocol**: POSIX/Win32 convention | **sourceUsed**: 0 on normal shutdown, 1 when a fault escapes the session | **notes**: The only machine-readable signal the whole application emits; individual commands have no exit codes.
- **capability**: Size-optimised packaging producing ahead-of-time-compiled and single-file binaries (optional) | **protocol**: none | **sourceUsed**: Two extra build configurations, both defaulting to a win-x64 runtime identifier and both enabling invariant-globalisation mode | **notes**: Invariant-globalisation mode changes the culture-sensitive comparisons this capability performs - the '/'-prefix test and the alphabetical ordering in the command listing dialog. Nothing in dispatch requires Windows; only the shipped packaging presets assume it. Make the prefix test and the ordering ordinal so packaging cannot alter behaviour.
- **capability**: Managed, region-scoped, account-billed marketplace for hosted foundation models, invoked once per turn with no streaming | **protocol**: HTTPS with per-request cryptographic request signing (AWS Signature Version 4); opaque per-model structured request/response bodies; region-scoped endpoint; content type and accept type both application/json | **sourceUsed**: Amazon Bedrock Runtime via AWSSDK.BedrockRuntime 4.0.7.3 (src/Xcaciv.ChatDbg.Core/Xcaciv.ChatDbg.Core.csproj:11) | **notes**: Only the single-shot invoke-model operation is used — no streaming, no unified converse-style API, no tool use, no model listing. The body is opaque and per-model; the service performs no cross-family normalisation, so the two body shapes the product implements are the entire contract.
- **capability**: Cloud credential resolution and request signing, with a documented fallback discovery chain | **protocol**: AWS Signature Version 4; the vendor's default credential provider chain (shared profile file, container/instance role, single sign-on, environment variables) | **sourceUsed**: The vendor SDK's static-credential constructor when both keys resolve, otherwise its region-only constructor (DefaultBedrockRuntimeClientFactory.cs:11-20) | **notes**: Both branches must exist and be selected by exactly the both-keys-non-empty rule. There is no session-token support anywhere, so temporary credentials in the environment are hoisted into a static key pair without their token and authentication fails.
- **capability**: Resolution of a service endpoint from a short region code | **protocol**: Vendor region naming (us-east-1, eu-west-1, …) | **sourceUsed**: RegionEndpoint.GetBySystemName(<string>) (DefaultBedrockRuntimeClientFactory.cs:16, :20) | **notes**: Unknown names must not fail fast; the source fabricates an endpoint and lets the failure surface at connection time. Any validation is a new requirement, not a port.
- **capability**: Claude-family message contract in its marketplace flavour | **protocol**: Structured body with anthropic_version, system, messages[{role,content}], max_tokens, temperature; reply read as content[0].text | **sourceUsed**: Hand-built body with the contract version pinned to bedrock-2023-05-31 (BedrockService.cs:68-78) | **notes**: Reproduce member names verbatim, snake_case included. The source additionally sends non-contract logprobs/top_logprobs members — do not copy that unless the target service is verified to accept them.
- **capability**: Legacy single-string completion reply contract | **protocol**: Structured body with a top-level completion string | **sourceUsed**: Parsed as the second-priority response branch (BedrockService.cs:190-192) | **notes**: This is the only reply shape the source's test suite exercises — best-understood behaviour, least real-world relevance.
- **capability**: Per-family request and response contracts for non-Claude model families (Titan, Llama, Mistral, Cohere, AI21) | **protocol**: Each family has its own native structured contract | **sourceUsed**: Not implemented — a single invented messages shape is sent to every non-Claude identifier (BedrockService.cs:95-103) | **notes**: Treat the generic branch as a stub. A faithful clone must either implement real per-family shapes or explicitly reject non-Claude identifiers.
- **capability**: Structured-document serialisation and read-only document traversal with try-get semantics | **protocol**: JSON (RFC 8259) | **sourceUsed**: Platform JSON library; request serialised from anonymous objects with default member naming; reply walked as a document object model (BedrockService.cs:107, :115, :126) | **notes**: Member names must be reproduced verbatim including snake_case. The sibling cloud back end applies camel-casing to its body and this one does not — do not unify them.
- **capability**: Diagnostic trace channel for developer builds | **protocol**: — | **sourceUsed**: Platform conditional debug-trace writes of the full request body, full reply body, full failure detail and probability-parse failures (BedrockService.cs:108, :124, :217, :321) | **notes**: INFERRED that these are compiled out of release builds. If your platform's equivalent is always on, every user prompt and model answer is written to it unredacted. No credential value appears there.
- **capability**: Optional operating-system secret vault, capability-detected, degrading silently to absent | **protocol**: Native platform credential store API | **sourceUsed**: Windows Credential Manager via advapi32.dll entry points CredReadW, CredWriteW, CredDeleteW, CredFree, under target names ChatDbg:AwsAccessKey and ChatDbg:AwsSecretKey (WindowsCredentialManager.cs:11-21; ChatSettings.cs:127-128) | **notes**: The feature's only operating-system-specific dependency, reached indirectly. Map to your platform's keychain or drop it and keep environment variables; remediation text must hide options the running platform cannot offer.
- **capability**: Substitutable client-construction seam plus test doubles for zero-network testing | **protocol**: — | **sourceUsed**: Interface-based factory with Moq 4.20.69 and xUnit 2.9.1 (Xcaciv.ChatDbg.Core.Tests.csproj:13, :18) | **notes**: Keep an equivalent seam; two of the source's three assertions concern the seam's call count and the identity of the settings record passed to it.
- **capability**: HTTP transport policy — timeout, retry count, back-off, connection pooling, proxy | **protocol**: HTTP/1.1 over TLS | **sourceUsed**: Nothing configured; whatever the vendor SDK defaults to is what runs (DefaultBedrockRuntimeClientFactory.cs:13-20) | **notes**: The clone will not match the source's timing behaviour unless it matches those defaults. Decide explicitly — the source made no decision — and note that per-turn client construction defeats pooling regardless.
- **capability**: Exponential function for converting log probabilities to probabilities | **protocol**: — | **sourceUsed**: Standard-library exp applied at parse time to alternatives (BedrockService.cs:178, :268) and again at display time to every entry (TokenLogProbabilities.cs:26) | **notes**: Any language's exponential will do; what matters is WHERE it is applied — the double application to alternatives is the source's defect.
- **capability**: Managed runtime | **protocol**: — | **sourceUsed**: net10.0 for all four projects | **notes**: Nothing in this feature depends on the runtime version.
- **capability**: Operating-system-managed, encrypted-at-rest, per-user secret store addressable by an opaque entry name | **protocol**: Native OS credential-store API; entries created as generic credentials with an explicit persistence scope | **sourceUsed**: Microsoft Windows Credential Manager via direct native calls to advapi32.dll (CredReadW, CredWriteW, CredDeleteW, CredFree) with a manually marshalled credential structure | **notes**: The only encrypted-at-rest channel. Blob is UTF-16 little-endian and its declared size is in BYTES (2x character count). Persistence scope 2 = local machine, explicitly non-roaming. Entry names contain a colon (ChatDbg:AzureApiKey). Natural equivalents elsewhere are the macOS keychain and a Secret-Service/keyring daemon on Linux, but the source deliberately reports 'unavailable' off-Windows and a test pins write-returns-false there. No specific encryption algorithm is a requirement; 'the OS encrypts it' is.
- **capability**: Native-library interoperation with manual memory management | **protocol**: C ABI, structure marshalling, UTF-16 string allocation | **sourceUsed**: Platform invoke with AllocHGlobal/FreeHGlobal, Marshal.Copy, PtrToStructure; unsafe blocks enabled in the core library | **notes**: Disappears entirely if the target language has a first-class keyring binding. What must survive is the adapter's public shape: read -> optional string, write -> bool, delete -> bool, available -> bool, and none of the four ever raises. Every handle and buffer must be released on every path including failures.
- **capability**: Read access to the process environment | **protocol**: POSIX / Windows environment variables | **sourceUsed**: Standard runtime environment API | **notes**: Read-only; the product never sets a variable. A variable that exists but holds the empty string must be treated as absent. Lookups repeat on every read because nothing is cached.
- **capability**: Local document persistence for non-secret configuration, human-readable and hand-editable, backward-compatible with files written by earlier versions | **protocol**: JSON, pretty-printed, camelCase keys | **sourceUsed**: System.Text.Json with pretty-printing, a camelCase naming policy, per-field JsonPropertyName overrides, and JsonIgnore on computed/resolved members | **notes**: The file format is a compatibility requirement. A clone must read {"useWindowsCredentialManager": false, "azureApiKey": "", "awsAccessKey": "", "awsSecretKey": ""} and files where those three string fields are non-empty. Preserve exactly the split between the three serialized legacy fields and the three excluded resolved values, or you break backward compatibility or leak resolved secrets to disk.
- **capability**: User home / profile directory discovery with a temp-directory fallback | **protocol**: filesystem | **sourceUsed**: User-profile special-folder lookup, falling back to the platform temp path when the result is blank or the lookup throws | **notes**: Default path <home>/.ChatDbg/settings.json. The fallback silently relocates the file, plaintext credential slots included, into a directory that is world-readable on most systems, with no message to the user.
- **capability**: Line-oriented interactive console input and output for consent prompts and wizard menus | **protocol**: - | **sourceUsed**: Direct stdout writes and stdin line reads performed inside the service layer itself | **notes**: Hard coupling in the source and the direct cause of the graphical shell's unusable wizard. Reads block with no timeout and no cancellation. A clone should inject an interaction port so both shells and the tests can supply their own implementation, but that changes observable behavior and belongs in an explicit decision, not a silent refactor.
- **capability**: Full-screen terminal user-interface toolkit with tabbed dialogs, checkboxes, modal sub-dialogs, radio groups, and a masked text field | **protocol**: - | **sourceUsed**: Terminal.Gui (Secret = true text field for the credential value) | **notes**: Needed only for the graphical credential surface. Masked entry is the one capability the console path lacks, so console-typed secrets are echoed and land in shell history. The toolkit owns the terminal, which is why the service layer's own stdout prompts are invisible and unanswerable there.
- **capability**: Command-line tokenizer | **protocol**: - | **sourceUsed**: Split on the single space character, discarding empty tokens | **notes**: Determines exactly which secrets are typeable: single internal spaces survive, runs of spaces collapse to one, tabs and newlines are impossible. Pin this or change it deliberately.
- **capability**: Hosted OpenAI-compatible chat service (consumer of this feature only) | **protocol**: HTTPS, API-key header authentication | **sourceUsed**: Azure OpenAI via its SDK plus a raw HTTP path that sets an api-key header | **notes**: Listed only because it is the sink for one resolved secret. Treats itself as configured when endpoint, key and model are all non-empty.
- **capability**: Cloud model-inference service (consumer of this feature only) | **protocol**: HTTPS, request signing with an access-key / secret-key pair | **sourceUsed**: Amazon Bedrock Runtime SDK, constructed either with explicit static credentials or with the SDK's own credential chain | **notes**: Its 'am I configured' test ignores the secret half of the pair entirely, and its fallback to the SDK's ambient credential chain is a fourth, undocumented credential channel a clone inherits unless it closes it.
- **capability**: Randomized identifier generation (test-only) | **protocol**: - | **sourceUsed**: GUID generation, used to build never-before-written vault entry names | **notes**: Keep an equivalent so the 'unknown entry reads as absent' test stays honest.
- **capability**: Mocking / test-double framework (test-only) | **protocol**: - | **sourceUsed**: Moq, asserting the store-into-vault operation is invoked exactly zero times when the enable flag is false | **notes**: The credential-store boundary must remain an injectable interface or the zero-invocation gate becomes unassertable.
- **capability**: Whole-file text read and whole-file text overwrite on a local filesystem | **protocol**: POSIX / Win32 file I/O | **sourceUsed**: .NET 10 File.ReadAllTextAsync / File.WriteAllTextAsync | **notes**: Whole-file semantics only: no streaming, no locking, no temp-file-then-rename, therefore no atomicity. Default encoding UTF-8 with no byte-order mark. An interrupted write must leave the target truncated, matching observed behavior.
- **capability**: Recursive create-directory-if-missing | **protocol**: — | **sourceUsed**: .NET Directory.CreateDirectory | **notes**: Called before every export write; a no-op when the directory exists. A failure here must be indistinguishable to the user from a write failure (same swallowed-exception path).
- **capability**: File existence check | **protocol**: — | **sourceUsed**: .NET File.Exists | **notes**: Import's pre-flight. A negative result is a normal, non-exceptional failure path that prints the distinct diagnostic 'File not found: {path}' to standard output.
- **capability**: Path manipulation: extension detection, join, directory-of | **protocol**: — | **sourceUsed**: .NET Path.HasExtension / Path.Combine / Path.GetDirectoryName | **notes**: 'Has an extension' must mean a dot after the last separator that is not the final character, so a dot-prefixed name such as .history counts as having one. Separator rules follow the host platform, so a backslash is a separator on Windows and an ordinary filename character elsewhere.
- **capability**: Current user's home/profile directory lookup | **protocol**: — | **sourceUsed**: .NET Environment.GetFolderPath(UserProfile) | **notes**: Resolves to the user profile folder on Windows and $HOME on Unix-like systems. Used for '~/' expansion in import and export and as the starting directory for both file dialogs.
- **capability**: Object serialization/deserialization with explicit per-field wire names and indented output | **protocol**: JSON (RFC 8259) | **sourceUsed**: .NET System.Text.Json, indented, camelCase policy, explicit [JsonPropertyName] per field | **notes**: Explicit per-field names win over the naming policy, so wire keys are exactly as listed in FR-4.40 — including the snake-case logprob and top_alternatives among otherwise camelCase keys. Deserialization is case-sensitive, ignores unknown keys, and rejects comments and trailing commas.
- **capability**: A JSON string-escaping profile that escapes more than the standard minimum | **protocol**: JSON (RFC 8259) | **sourceUsed**: .NET System.Text.Json default JavaScriptEncoder | **notes**: Apostrophe is written as the escape ', backtick as `, plus as +; less-than, greater-than and ampersand are also escaped; all non-ASCII becomes \uXXXX; newlines become \n. A minimally-escaping clone produces semantically identical but byte-different files.
- **capability**: Shortest-round-trip floating-point formatting | **protocol**: IEEE 754 / JSON number | **sourceUsed**: .NET default double JSON writer | **notes**: A log probability of -5 is written -5, not -5.0; no exponent notation for ordinary log-probability magnitudes.
- **capability**: Wall-clock UTC timestamp source | **protocol**: ISO-8601 | **sourceUsed**: .NET DateTime.UtcNow | **notes**: Used for every message timestamp and the record's created-at. Persisted as round-trip ISO-8601 with exactly seven fractional-second digits and a Z suffix. Nothing depends on uniqueness or monotonicity — and monotonicity is broken by inject.
- **capability**: Random unique identifier generation | **protocol**: UUID / GUID | **sourceUsed**: .NET Guid.NewGuid().ToString() | **notes**: Session identifier only, canonical lowercase hyphenated form. Not security-sensitive, not used as a key, never read by anything else.
- **capability**: Culture-sensitive signed 32-bit integer parsing | **protocol**: — | **sourceUsed**: .NET int.TryParse(string, out int) | **notes**: Used only for the inject position. Accepts a leading sign per the current culture; rejects decimal points, group separators and out-of-range values. Compact/SingleFile build flavors force invariant globalization, so sign handling can differ between build flavors.
- **capability**: Standard output stream | **protocol**: — | **sourceUsed**: .NET Console.WriteLine | **notes**: The persistence layer writes its own diagnostics here unconditionally, including while the full-screen shell owns the terminal — reproducing this reproduces the display corruption in QUIRK-4.23. No logger abstraction, no suppression switch.
- **capability**: Full-screen terminal user interface with menus and native file open/save dialogs | **protocol**: ANSI terminal | **sourceUsed**: Terminal.Gui 1.19.0 (full-screen shell only) | **notes**: Open dialog is single-selection and starts in the home directory; save dialog is pre-seeded with chat_history.json in the home directory. Both route the chosen path back through the same string command line as a typed command, inheriting space collapsing.
- **capability**: Delayed user-interface callback marshalled onto the interface main loop | **protocol**: — | **sourceUsed**: .NET Task.Delay plus a Terminal.Gui main-loop invoke | **notes**: Drives the 3000 ms status-bar revert. The timer is started unconditionally for every status message, so two commands within 3 seconds truncate the second message's display.
- **capability**: Managed application runtime | **protocol**: — | **sourceUsed**: .NET 10 (net10.0), SDK pinned to 10.0.100-rc.1.25451.107, rollForward latestFeature | **notes**: Nothing in this feature depends on a runtime-specific capability; every operation here is fully cross-platform (Windows, Linux, macOS).
- **capability**: Structured-document serialisation for a small flat configuration record (text-based, hand-editable, indentation preserved) | **protocol**: JSON | **sourceUsed**: Platform standard-library JSON serializer with explicit lower-camel wire names on every persisted member, opt-out markers on computed/secret members, indented output; an additional camel-case naming policy that is inert | **notes**: Any equivalent library works, but the exact 21 wire names must survive so existing documents keep loading. Indented output is a user-visible requirement (the document is an intended hand-edit surface). The three deprecated secret keys must still be emitted as empty strings.
- **capability**: Local filesystem read/write of a single small text file with directory auto-creation | **protocol**: POSIX / Win32 file APIs | **sourceUsed**: Whole-file asynchronous read and whole-file overwrite; parent directory created on first save | **notes**: No locking, no atomic replace, no temp-file-and-rename. 'Last writer wins' is the observed contract; two concurrent shells clobber each other. A reimplementation may improve this but must not depend on the improvement.
- **capability**: User home / profile directory discovery | **protocol**: OS convention | **sourceUsed**: Platform 'user profile' special-folder lookup with an OS temp-directory fallback | **notes**: The product's directory name is the literal '.ChatDbg', a dotted directory used verbatim inside a Windows user profile as well as on Unix-likes, so the convention ports unchanged.
- **capability**: Operating-system temporary-directory discovery | **protocol**: OS convention | **sourceUsed**: Platform temp-path lookup | **notes**: Used only as a degraded fallback when the profile directory is null, empty, whitespace, or throws. The user learns about it only from the startup banner's settings-file path line.
- **capability**: Process environment variable reading | **protocol**: none | **sourceUsed**: Environment lookups by fixed name | **notes**: Contract names: CHATDBG_AZURE_API_KEY; CHATDBG_AWS_ACCESS_KEY then AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID; CHATDBG_AWS_SECRET_KEY then AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY. Read on every access, so a variable set after launch takes effect immediately without restart.
- **capability**: Operating-system platform detection (is the native credential store present) | **protocol**: none | **sourceUsed**: Runtime 'is Windows' check | **notes**: Gates the keystore toggle, help text shaping, and startup notices. On other platforms substitute the local secret store and preserve the three-tier resolution ordering.
- **capability**: Operating-system secret store keyed by named entries | **protocol**: Win32 Credential Manager (DPAPI-backed) | **sourceUsed**: Native credential API via platform invocation, owned by Credential Management; entries named ChatDbg:AzureApiKey, ChatDbg:AwsAccessKey, ChatDbg:AwsSecretKey | **notes**: This feature owns only the toggle. Substitute the platform keychain or secret service; keep or one-for-one map the three entry names, and keep short aliases azure / awsaccess / awssecret as accepted logical types.
- **capability**: Interactive terminal input for yes/no and numbered-choice confirmations | **protocol**: none | **sourceUsed**: Blocking line reads on standard input performed inside the settings service itself | **notes**: Must be refactored into an injectable prompt/confirm port. As written, these prompts are invisible and unanswerable from a windowed or headless host, which is why the windowed Migrate button appears to hang or no-op.
- **capability**: Windowed terminal UI toolkit (tabs, radio groups, text fields, checkboxes, modal dialogs, message boxes) | **protocol**: none | **sourceUsed**: A terminal-UI widget library; the settings dialog is a fixed 80x25 modal with four tabs and 60x15 / 70x18 sub-dialogs | **notes**: Only the windowed shell depends on this; the plain-console shell needs nothing beyond stdin/stdout. Avoid the source's untyped per-tab property bag read back by reflection at save time - hold typed field references instead.
- **capability**: Rich console rendering (rules, tables, colour markup) | **protocol**: ANSI escape sequences | **sourceUsed**: A console-styling library used by the token-log-probability display these settings drive | **notes**: Not required by the settings feature itself; listed because these settings configure it.
- **capability**: Locale-aware number parsing and formatting | **protocol**: none | **sourceUsed**: Ambient-culture parse and format for temperature and every integer; no invariant-culture overload anywhere | **notes**: Choose invariant culture for the persisted document and all command input and record it as an intentional deviation; otherwise a document written under one locale can fail to load under another.
- **capability**: Build-time globalization mode configuration | **protocol**: none | **sourceUsed**: Invariant globalization forced on only in the Compact and SingleFile build configurations, not in ordinary builds | **notes**: This is why the same command accepts 0.7 in one binary and rejects it in another. Packaging choices must never change input validation.
- **capability**: Trimmed / ahead-of-time packaging with framework resource strings stripped | **protocol**: none | **sourceUsed**: The Compact and SingleFile configurations replace platform exception text with bare resource keys | **notes**: Every error message that interpolates a platform exception degrades in those builds. Carry your own diagnostic text rather than surfacing the runtime's.
- **capability**: Substitutable persistence port for automated testing | **protocol**: none | **sourceUsed**: The persistence contract is an interface; command tests substitute a recording double and assert save counts without touching disk | **notes**: Keep persistence behind the narrow six-operation port so 'saved exactly once' assertions remain expressible; also keep the optional base-directory and file-name construction parameters as a test seam.
- **capability**: Local file system: directory creation, non-recursive listing filtered by extension, whole-file read/write/delete of UTF-8 text | **protocol**: POSIX / Win32 file APIs | **sourceUsed**: .NET System.IO (Directory.CreateDirectory, Directory.GetFiles(dir,"*.json"), File.ReadAllTextAsync/WriteAllTextAsync/Delete/Exists) | **notes**: Whole-file overwrite semantics, no locking, no temp-file-and-rename, no version token. UTF-8 without BOM. No recursion into subdirectories. Per-file read failures are caught and reported; directory-enumeration failures are not caught anywhere.
- **capability**: Per-user well-known directory resolution (local application data, Documents, user home) | **protocol**: XDG user directories on Linux / Known Folders on Windows | **sourceUsed**: .NET Environment.GetFolderPath for LocalApplicationData, MyDocuments, UserProfile | **notes**: Measured: Windows returns C:\Users\<user>\AppData\Local, C:\Users\<user>\Documents, C:\Users\<user>. Linux with no XDG user-dirs file returns /home/<user>/.local/share, the EMPTY STRING for Documents, and /home/<user>. The empty Documents result is not an error path; it silently degrades the default export destination to a bare relative file name. A clone must pick an explicit, non-empty default export directory and document it. The store root remains indirectly sensitive to $XDG_DATA_HOME.
- **capability**: Host OS set of characters forbidden in file names | **protocol**: none | **sourceUsed**: .NET Path.GetInvalidFileNameChars() | **notes**: Platform-dependent and measured: 41 characters on Windows (code points 0-31 plus " < > | : * ? \ /), 2 on Linux (code point 0 and /). Delegating to the host makes prompt libraries non-portable between operating systems and changes which names silently collide. Pick one explicit documented set; do not delegate.
- **capability**: Object serialization/deserialization to a text document with explicitly named fields and optional pretty-printing | **protocol**: JSON (RFC 8259) | **sourceUsed**: .NET System.Text.Json with per-field JsonPropertyName attributes and a WriteIndented option | **notes**: Field names fixed as name, content, description, createdAt, lastUsedAt and matched CASE-SENSITIVELY (a hand-edited file using 'Name' loses that field to its default). Timestamps ISO-8601 round-trip UTC with Z suffix. Unset last-used is written as explicit null, never omitted. Missing fields tolerated (defaults apply); malformed documents raise an error caught per file. The settings file of another feature uses a camel-case naming policy instead of per-field attributes - do not conflate the two formats.
- **capability**: Character-cell terminal UI toolkit: modal windows, list view with integer selection index, single-line text fields, multi-line word-wrapped text views, buttons, message/confirmation boxes returning the chosen button index, main-loop marshaling | **protocol**: ANSI/VT terminal rendering | **sourceUsed**: Terminal.Gui 1.19.0 with NStack.Core 1.1.1 (confirmed from committed build output; in-code comments claiming v2 and docs claiming 1.17.1 are stale) | **notes**: Needed only for the graphical management screen. The confirmation box returns the INDEX of the chosen button and the source treats index 0 as delete. A fresh list view reports selected index 0 and still reports 0 after its source is set - which makes the 'select a prompt first' guard nearly dead. All sizes are in character cells (80x25, 80x20, 70x15, 70x8, 60x8). No native file-open dialog is used.
- **capability**: Console line input / line output | **protocol**: none | **sourceUsed**: .NET Console.ReadLine / Console.WriteLine | **notes**: Used for the interactive content editor and for library load-error notices. Requires a real, line-buffered standard input; the source's read loop has no end-of-input check and hangs forever when input is exhausted.
- **capability**: Wall-clock time in UTC, UTC-to-local conversion, culture-aware short date-and-time formatting | **protocol**: none | **sourceUsed**: .NET DateTime.UtcNow, ToLocalTime(), format specifier 'g' | **notes**: Storage is always UTC; only display is localized. Display is date plus hours and minutes with NO seconds - measured under the invariant culture as '08/28/2026 22:21'.
- **capability**: Culture-aware string ordering | **protocol**: Unicode collation | **sourceUsed**: .NET default OrderBy on strings (current-culture comparison) | **notes**: Measured: a-b, ab, a_b, B, a orders as a, a_b, a-b, ab, B culture-sensitively but as B, a, a-b, a_b, ab by bytes. A byte-ordering clone renders /prompt list in a visibly different order.
- **capability**: Globalization data (collation tables and culture date formats) | **protocol**: CLDR / ICU | **sourceUsed**: .NET ICU-backed globalization, DISABLED via InvariantGlobalization in the product's Compact and SingleFile publish profiles | **notes**: Turning globalization off changes BOTH list ordering and timestamp rendering for the same library, so the same prompt library renders differently depending on which build of the product opened it. A clone shipping a size-optimized variant must reproduce or explicitly eliminate this divergence.
- **capability**: Unit-test framework with mocking (only if the reference tests are ported) | **protocol**: none | **sourceUsed**: xUnit 2.9.1 with Moq 4.20.69 | **notes**: Six assertions total across three test classes; they pin far less than the feature's behavior. Porting them is optional and insufficient on its own.
- **capability**: In-process local large-language-model inference engine that emits native diagnostic lines and exposes a hook to install a diagnostic-sink callback | **protocol**: Native (unmanaged) library callback carrying a severity-level token plus a message string per line | **sourceUsed**: LLamaSharp 0.25.0 managed bindings over llama.cpp (NativeLogConfig.llama_log_set), with CPU and CUDA-12 backend packages, pinned in src/Xcaciv.ChatDbg.Core/Xcaciv.ChatDbg.Core.csproj | **notes**: The sink is process-global, set-only (never unregistered) and last-writer-wins. The callback fires on engine-owned threads, making the recorder genuinely multi-threaded. The severity level is an engine-internal enumeration rendered by name - treat it as an opaque token; the resulting file mixes two label vocabularies. INFERRED: the engine may deliver partial lines; the source assumes whole lines (appends a line terminator per invocation) and only strips trailing whitespace. Repo docs cite the stale version 0.11.2 - ignore.
- **capability**: Local filesystem with recursive directory creation, append-to-file, overwrite-file, file-exists and directory-exists primitives | **protocol**: POSIX / Win32 file I/O | **sourceUsed**: Platform file APIs (Directory.CreateDirectory, File.AppendAllText, File.WriteAllText, File.Exists, Directory.Exists) | **notes**: No file handles are held open - every flush opens, appends and closes. No file locking, no forced synchronization to storage, no cross-process coordination, so two instances writing the same daily file interleave unpredictably. All failures must be swallowed with no propagation to the caller.
- **capability**: Per-user 'roaming application data' folder resolution | **protocol**: OS convention | **sourceUsed**: Platform special-folder lookup for roaming application data, yielding %APPDATA% (C:\Users\<user>\AppData\Roaming) on Windows | **notes**: Two fixed sub-segments follow: ChatDbg then Logs. On Unix-like hosts the same lookup yields $XDG_CONFIG_HOME or $HOME/.config, giving ~/.config/ChatDbg/Logs - documented nowhere in the source, which only ever states the Windows form. HAZARD: with no home directory set the lookup returns the empty string and the segments compose into the relative path ChatDbg/Logs, so logs land under the process working directory; nothing guards this (INFERRED from platform behaviour). The product uses three different per-user roots for three kinds of state.
- **capability**: Attachable debug/trace output stream visible to an attached debugger | **protocol**: none | **sourceUsed**: Platform debug-write primitive (System.Diagnostics.Debug.WriteLine), conditionally compiled on the DEBUG symbol | **notes**: On by default AND the only channel for this feature's own internal failures. In the source it is removed at compile time in any build lacking the DEBUG symbol, and no project or shared property file defines that symbol outside stock Debug - so in a shipped binary the channel does not exist and a dead logging subsystem is indistinguishable from a healthy one. A clone should make this a runtime switch, or route internal failures somewhere that survives, and must keep it cheap or a no-op when nothing is listening.
- **capability**: Standard console output | **protocol**: none | **sourceUsed**: Platform console write-line | **notes**: Off by default; when enabled, log lines interleave into the user's chat transcript with no visual separation. No code path in the source ever enables it.
- **capability**: Local wall clock with millisecond resolution | **protocol**: none | **sourceUsed**: Platform local (not UTC) date-time | **notes**: Used for both entry timestamps (yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm:ss.fff) and the daily file-name date (yyyyMMdd). No timezone or offset is recorded, so entries are ambiguous across DST transitions and across machines, and a process crossing local midnight writes pre-midnight entries into the next day's file. Timestamps render through the CURRENT culture, so a locale with a non-':' time separator changes the shape; the shipped publish configurations force invariant globalization while development configurations do not.
- **capability**: Thread mutual-exclusion primitive that is re-entrant on the same thread | **protocol**: none | **sourceUsed**: Platform monitor lock over a private object | **notes**: Re-entrancy is load-bearing: the size-triggered flush is invoked from inside the already-locked append and re-acquires the same lock. A non-re-entrant primitive deadlocks unless the flush is restructured. Debug-stream and console echoes are emitted outside the lock, so only buffer and file ordering is guaranteed.
- **capability**: Elapsed-time measurement | **protocol**: none | **sourceUsed**: Platform stopwatch | **notes**: Used only to produce the 'Generation complete in {N}ms' entry.
- **capability**: Text file encoding | **protocol**: UTF-8 | **sourceUsed**: Platform default for unspecified-encoding text writes: UTF-8 with no byte-order mark | **notes**: Applies to both the daily append and the snapshot overwrite. A clone that emits a byte-order mark, or a 16-bit encoding, breaks the 'attach the file to a bug report' workflow for line-oriented readers. INFERRED - no test inspects bytes.
- **capability**: Unit-test runner with fact-style tests and system temporary-directory access | **protocol**: none | **sourceUsed**: xUnit 2.9.1, with Moq 4.20.69 present but unused by these tests and coverlet 6.0.2, pinned in src/Xcaciv.ChatDbg.Core.Tests/Xcaciv.ChatDbg.Core.Tests.csproj:12-19 | **notes**: The source's tests touch the real filesystem with no abstraction over file I/O, and two of the seven recorder tests contain zero assertions. A clone should place the clock, the file store and the debug sink behind seams so the engine-callback path, the auto-flush threshold, the daily file-name derivation and all four failure branches - none reached by the source tests - become testable.
- **capability**: Managed runtime and build toolchain capable of ahead-of-time compilation, trimming and single-file packaging | **protocol**: none | **sourceUsed**: .NET 10 (net10.0) across library, both shells and tests; LangVersion=latest pinned in Directory.Build.props:3 | **notes**: Publish configurations matter more to this feature than usual: Compact and SingleFile force invariant globalization, substitute bare framework resource keys for exception messages, strip symbols, disable stack-trace metadata, and default the runtime identifier to win-x64. Every ERROR entry embeds a whole exception object, so in exactly the configurations a user is most likely to run, the most valuable log lines degrade to an opaque key. INFERRED: aggressive trimming plus a marshalled native callback is a known hazard and trim-analysis warnings are explicitly suppressed, so such a break would present as silence.
- **capability**: Managed application runtime providing line-oriented terminal input/output, file I/O, environment-variable access, a UTC clock and random unique-identifier generation | **protocol**: none | **sourceUsed**: .NET 10 (net10.0), SDK pinned by global.json to 10.0.100-rc.1.25451.107 | **notes**: Only console, file, environment and clock primitives are load-bearing for this feature. Two size-optimised publish profiles enable invariant globalization and trimming; both change observable behaviour (instruction dispatch and percentage spacing), so the chosen build configuration is part of the specification.
- **capability**: Rich terminal rendering: titled horizontal rules, auto-expanding bordered tables with fixed per-column widths and centring, grids of bordered/headed panels, inline colour and dim markup | **protocol**: ANSI escape sequences | **sourceUsed**: Spectre.Console 0.51.1, called directly by the session and used only for the token-probability visualization | **notes**: Required palette names: green, lime, yellow, orange3, red, blue, grey, dim. Escaping convention is bracket doubling. Borders are the rounded style; tables expand to terminal width. All other output is plain text.
- **capability**: Terminal width query | **protocol**: none | **sourceUsed**: Runtime console window-width property | **notes**: Used once, to compute grid columns as max(1, width / 40). Measured on Linux with redirected output it returns 80 and does not fail; on Windows with no console attached the equivalent query raises and the visualization is lost to the loop's blanket guard. The source guards only against a zero width, never against failure.
- **capability**: Line-oriented standard input | **protocol**: none | **sourceUsed**: Runtime console read-line | **notes**: Measured: returns nothing at end of stream, which the blank-input guard reports as blank, producing an unbounded busy loop. A reimplementer should treat end of stream as an exit condition and flag that as a deliberate deviation.
- **capability**: Unicode-capable terminal font and code page | **protocol**: Unicode | **sourceUsed**: none configured | **notes**: Banner frame U+2554/2550/2557/2551/255A/255D, markers U+2713 and U+2717, numero header U+2116 are all outside ASCII and Latin-1. Encoding is never set, so a legacy code page shows replacement characters.
- **capability**: Cloud language-model chat completion with optional per-token log probabilities | **protocol**: HTTPS/REST + JSON via a vendor client | **sourceUsed**: Azure.AI.OpenAI 2.1.0 over the runtime HTTP client | **notes**: This session needs only reply text plus an ordered per-token probability list with alternatives. The adapter owns request shaping and system-prompt injection. Merely constructing this adapter opens an outbound connection pool even when unused.
- **capability**: Managed-service language-model chat completion | **protocol**: HTTPS + request signing + JSON | **sourceUsed**: AWSSDK.BedrockRuntime 4.0.7.3 | **notes**: Same abstraction; default region us-east-1. Considered configured on presence of an access key alone, so a session with only a secret key still reports configured and the reported source is always the access key's.
- **capability**: Local language-model inference from an on-disk model file with token-level introspection | **protocol**: native library binding | **sourceUsed**: LLamaSharp 0.25.0 with CPU and CUDA-12 backends, bundling native runtimes for win-x64, linux-x64 (avx/avx2/avx512/cuda12/noavx), linux-musl-x64, linux-arm64, osx-x64, osx-arm64 | **notes**: This feature only constructs and disposes it. Construction installs a native diagnostic sink; disposal appends to a daily log file - both happen even in a session that never selects this provider. Configuration check is purely that the model path exists as a file.
- **capability**: Optional operating-system secret vault | **protocol**: platform-native credential API (generic credential entries) | **sourceUsed**: Direct native interop with the Windows credential API (CredReadW/CredWriteW/CredDeleteW) | **notes**: Availability is a plain platform check; all vault guidance is hidden when unavailable. Target names ChatDbg:AzureApiKey, ChatDbg:AwsAccessKey, ChatDbg:AwsSecretKey. Treat as a pluggable secret source with a working unavailable branch - it is a genuine capability gap off-platform.
- **capability**: Structured document persistence for settings, prompts and transcripts | **protocol**: JSON | **sourceUsed**: Runtime JSON serializer, indented output, explicit per-property names, computed and secret members excluded | **notes**: Reached only through the stores, but the exact key names are a compatibility contract for existing user settings files and for exported transcripts that must remain re-importable (note the snake_case outlier top_alternatives).
- **capability**: Locale-aware text comparison and number formatting | **protocol**: Unicode collation (ICU) | **sourceUsed**: Runtime default culture-sensitive string prefix comparison and ambient-culture percentage format | **notes**: Load-bearing in exactly two places: the leading-slash prefix test, and percentage rendering, which produces 25.00000%% under en-US and 25.00000 %% under invariant globalization. Use an ordinal prefix test and pin the number format, recording both as deliberate deviations.
- **capability**: Chat-completion service returning per-token log-probabilities and top-K alternatives | **protocol**: HTTPS + JSON, OpenAI-style chat completions | **sourceUsed**: Azure OpenAI via hand-rolled REST POST to {endpoint}/openai/deployments/{model}/chat/completions?api-version=2023-12-01-preview with an api-key header; Azure.AI.OpenAI 2.1.0 referenced by all three projects but bypassed on the probability path | **notes**: Request body carries messages, temperature, max_tokens, top_p: 1.0, logprobs: true, top_logprobs: {topK}. Three response shapes must be tolerated: top-level logprobs.content[], top-level logprobs[], and choices[0].logprobs as either an object with content or a bare array. Each entry needs token + logprob, with optional top_logprobs/top_alternatives. The api-version the code sends is newer than the one the product's own diagnostics text advises (2023-05-15).
- **capability**: Managed model-invocation API that can be asked for token log-probabilities | **protocol**: HTTPS + JSON over a cloud SDK | **sourceUsed**: Amazon Bedrock runtime via AWSSDK.BedrockRuntime 4.0.7.3 | **notes**: Requests carry logprobs (boolean) and top_logprobs (topK when enabled, else 0); the Claude-shaped body also carries the fixed anthropic_version value bedrock-2023-05-31. The parser accepts logprobs as an array of token objects or an object with a tokens member, and accepts either a logprob or a log_prob key. This adapter exponentiates alternative log-probabilities at parse time, so a single response mixes two encodings.
- **capability**: Local in-process language-model inference with access to next-token candidate scores | **protocol**: native library binding | **sourceUsed**: LLamaSharp 0.25.0 plus CPU and CUDA-12 backend packages, with runtime reflection / late binding for the candidate-score accessor and token-to-text lookup | **notes**: Alternatives are a softmax over only the top-K candidate scores, so they sum to 1 among themselves rather than over the vocabulary; they are attached one generation step late, so the first token never gets alternatives and keeps a placeholder log-probability of 0. When no text match is found the token's own value falls back to the maximum alternative. Late binding is incompatible with the project's own ahead-of-time publish configuration and fails silently there.
- **capability**: Rich terminal rendering: rules, tables, grids, bordered panels, 16/256-colour markup | **protocol**: ANSI terminal escape sequences | **sourceUsed**: Spectre.Console 0.51.1, referenced by all three projects including the core library | **notes**: Markup brackets inside token text must be escaped by doubling them. A dependency-free ASCII fallback renderer ships but no runtime path selects it, and its presence does not remove the package dependency.
- **capability**: Full-screen terminal UI toolkit: menus, tabbed dialogs, scroll views, colour schemes, modal message boxes | **protocol**: terminal (curses-like) | **sourceUsed**: Terminal.Gui 1.19.0 | **notes**: It owns and repaints the whole screen, which is why a renderer that writes ANSI escapes straight to the console produces output invisible beneath it. Used for the Log Probs settings tab, the View menu, the per-message indicators and the side panel.
- **capability**: Local file storage for a single application settings document | **protocol**: UTF-8 JSON text file | **sourceUsed**: <user profile>/.ChatDbg/settings.json, indented, camel-cased member names | **notes**: Directory created on demand; falls back to the OS temp directory with the same file name when the profile directory is unavailable or resolves to an empty string. Save failures are printed and swallowed; load failures silently yield an all-defaults document.
- **capability**: Local file storage for exported conversations that must be re-importable | **protocol**: UTF-8 JSON text file | **sourceUsed**: user-chosen path via the export/import commands, indented and camel-cased | **notes**: Confidence records ride inside exported messages under logProbabilities; the alternatives array is persisted under the snake-cased name top_alternatives, overriding the camel-case convention; the derived probability is omitted. This is a compatibility requirement - exports must re-import field-for-field.
- **capability**: Terminal width query | **protocol**: none | **sourceUsed**: console window width, used to size the responsive grid | **notes**: About 40 columns are assumed per token card; column count is max(1, width / 40). A reported width of 0 is guarded, but a query that fails outright when output is redirected or no terminal is attached is not guarded at the point of use.
- **capability**: Pseudo-random number generation with a fixed seed | **protocol**: none | **sourceUsed**: seeded generator with seed 42 for demonstration data; a second unseeded generator is constructed but never used in the cloud fallback | **notes**: Only reproducibility across runs is contractual - the exact number sequence is an artefact of the source runtime's generator and will not port.
- **capability**: Natural exponential and natural logarithm | **protocol**: none | **sourceUsed**: platform standard math library | **notes**: Exponentiation runs on every read of a derived probability (hot path when rendering thousands of tokens); logarithm is used when normalising local candidate scores.
- **capability**: Diagnostic trace channel invisible in release runs | **protocol**: none | **sourceUsed**: platform debug output stream | **notes**: Exception detail from the configuration command and the provider parsers is written here and nowhere else; it never reaches the operator.
- **capability**: Unit-test and mocking stack (to port the pinned behaviours) | **protocol**: none | **sourceUsed**: xUnit 2.9.1, Moq 4.20.69, Microsoft.NET.Test.Sdk 17.12.0, coverlet.collector 6.0.2 | **notes**: The output-formatter abstraction is mocked to assert which render operation was called and with which start index. The source's suite cannot be built as checked out (malformed SDK pin), so its assertions are unverified - notably one impossible probability assertion.
- **capability**: Managed runtime plus pinned SDK | **protocol**: none | **sourceUsed**: net10.0 for all four projects; SDK pinned in global.json to 10.0.100-rc.1.25451.107 with rollForward: latestFeature | **notes**: The pinned global.json at this commit is not valid JSON (one extra closing brace), which makes the toolchain refuse to start anywhere inside the tree.
- **capability**: Styled terminal output: inline markup tags, named colour palette, rounded-box tables with per-column fixed widths and centring, multi-column grids, bordered panels with headers, captioned horizontal rules with left/centre justification, auto-expanding widths | **protocol**: ANSI/VT escape sequences | **sourceUsed**: Spectre.Console 0.51.1 (referenced by the core project, the plain shell and the full-screen shell) | **notes**: Markup uses square-bracket tags closed by a [/] sentinel; literal brackets must be doubled to escape them. Colour names must exist in the chosen library's palette — the shared band map emits a bare 'orange' that is very likely invalid (QUIRK-12.6). Any equivalent rich-terminal library works provided it supplies: a styled span, a table with per-column fixed widths and centring, a grid of fixed columns, a bordered panel with a header, and a captioned rule with left/centre justification.
- **capability**: Full-screen terminal user-interface toolkit: windows, framed views, menu bar, status bar, modal dialogs and message boxes, scrollable views with content-size and offset control, aligned labels, buttons, radio groups, check boxes, tab views, file open/save dialogs, named global colour-scheme slots, custom views with a draw hook giving direct cell painting | **protocol**: terminfo / ANSI | **sourceUsed**: Terminal.Gui 1.19.0 (full-screen shell only) | **notes**: Needs per-widget foreground/background attribute pairs; four global scheme slots (base, dialog, menu, error) each with normal, focus, hot-normal, hot-focus and disabled attributes; a custom-draw hook exposing move-cursor, set-attribute and write-string; and a main-loop marshalling primitive so timer callbacks can touch the interface thread.
- **capability**: Terminal width query | **protocol**: — | **sourceUsed**: Standard-library console metrics | **notes**: Used only for the responsive grid column count (max(1, width/40)). Must degrade safely when output is redirected or no console is attached — the source guards only against a zero result, not against a failing query. (Failure mode is INFERRED; not tested per platform.)
- **capability**: Redirectable standard-output stream | **protocol**: — | **sourceUsed**: Standard-library console writer | **notes**: The plain output surface must write through a replaceable stream, not a raw terminal handle, or the existing test approach (swap writer, render, restore in a finally block) cannot be reproduced.
- **capability**: Culture-sensitive number formatting for percent and fixed-point values | **protocol**: Unicode CLDR number patterns | **sourceUsed**: Standard-library formatting against the ambient culture | **notes**: The percent pattern differs by culture — invariant inserts a space before the sign (50.00000 %), US English does not (50.00000%). Both shell binaries force invariant globalization in their compact and single-file build configurations, so packaged builds show the spaced form. Pin a culture explicitly if exact output matters. Verified experimentally in the dossier, not assumed.
- **capability**: Deterministic pseudo-random generator | **protocol**: — | **sourceUsed**: Standard-library generator seeded with 42 | **notes**: Only used to build demonstration data so demo output is stable across runs in a process. Reimplementations will not reproduce the same numbers unless the same algorithm is used; treat 'deterministic given a fixed seed' as the requirement, not the specific values.
- **capability**: Exponential function | **protocol**: — | **sourceUsed**: Standard-library exp | **notes**: The only arithmetic this feature performs on model data: displayed probability = e^(log probability). Yields a 0…1 fraction for genuine log-probabilities and unbounded values for the demonstration fixture.
- **capability**: Unicode glyph rendering | **protocol**: Unicode | **sourceUsed**: Literal characters in source: ◊ (U+25CA probability indicator), № (U+2116 numero sign), ✓/✗ (U+2713/U+2717 status marks), and the box-drawing set ═ ║ ╔ ╗ ╚ ╝ for the plain welcome banner | **notes**: Terminal font must carry these; supply ASCII fallbacks. Note the numero sign has already been corrupted to '?' in two of three copies (QUIRK-12.8); a reimplementation should pick one intentional caption ('#' recommended).
- **capability**: Single consistent source and document text encoding | **protocol**: UTF-8 | **sourceUsed**: UTF-8 source files | **notes**: Two files in this feature's blast radius are already broken: the project README is pure ASCII so its rendered examples are '?'-mojibake and unusable as a visual specification, and Shell.Gui/UI/SettingsDialog.cs carries a lone 0x95 byte at line 368 that is not valid UTF-8. Fix the encoding of literals rather than copying them.
- **capability**: Structured document persistence for display settings | **protocol**: JSON | **sourceUsed**: Standard-library JSON serializer with explicit wire names | **notes**: Consumed, not owned by this feature, but the wire names are load-bearing: showAllTokens (false), gridViewForTokens (false), gridViewMaxAlternatives (5), enableLogProbabilities (false), logProbabilitiesTopK (5). The computed probability field is explicitly excluded from serialization.
- **capability**: Per-user configuration directory resolution | **protocol**: — | **sourceUsed**: Standard-library special-folder lookup | **notes**: Settings live at «user profile»/.ChatDbg/settings.json, with a temporary directory as the fallback. Any per-user configuration location works provided the directory name '.ChatDbg' and file name are preserved for compatibility with existing installs.
- **capability**: Test doubles for the output contract plus standard-output redirection | **protocol**: — | **sourceUsed**: A mocking library for the contract; process-wide output writer replacement for the plain surface | **notes**: The output contract must be substitutable and the plain surface must write through a replaceable stream, or the existing test approach cannot be reproduced. No dependency-injection container exists — the concrete surface is constructed by the host and hand-passed to callers.
- **capability**: Managed-language compiler and build toolchain, pinnable to an exact version with a controlled roll-forward policy | **protocol**: none (structured-text pin document) | **sourceUsed**: .NET SDK pinned in global.json to 10.0.100-rc.1.25451.107 with rollForward latestFeature; target framework net10.0; LangVersion latest | **notes**: The pinned build is a pre-release. The pin document must be validated in CI — in the source it is malformed structured text and gates every build. A port needs an equivalent exact-version-plus-bounded-roll-forward mechanism or it loses the guarantee that every machine builds identically.
- **capability**: Declarative project and build description system with directory-inherited defaults and named, condition-selectable configurations | **protocol**: none | **sourceUsed**: MSBuild .csproj project files, Directory.Build.props, and an .sln workspace descriptor (format 12.00) | **notes**: Configuration-conditioned property blocks are how all four profiles are expressed. The workspace descriptor enumerates only two of the four configurations, so the two distribution profiles are invisible to an IDE and reachable only from a command line.
- **capability**: Ahead-of-time native compilation of a managed program, with size-preference code generation, identical-body folding, stack-trace-metadata suppression and symbol stripping | **protocol**: none | **sourceUsed**: .NET Native AOT (PublishAot, IlcOptimizationPreference=Size, IlcFoldVTables, IlcGenerateStackTraceData=false, StripSymbols) | **notes**: Needed for the native profile only, which is never actually released. It additionally requires a host C/C++ compiler and linker that nothing in the source installs; budget for a second toolchain on every machine and agent that builds it.
- **capability**: Whole-program dead-code elimination (tree shaking) with at least two selectable levels of aggressiveness | **protocol**: none | **sourceUsed**: .NET IL trimmer (PublishTrimmed, TrimMode=full for the native profile and partial for the bundled profile, SuppressTrimAnalysisWarnings) | **notes**: Two granularities are required, not one — the shipping profile is deliberately the gentler one so reflection-heavy dependencies survive. Suppressing analysis warnings is what makes every trimming defect invisible until run time; a port should reconsider that.
- **capability**: Single-file self-extracting bundler with in-bundle compression and native-payload inclusion | **protocol**: none | **sourceUsed**: .NET single-file publish (PublishSingleFile, EnableCompressionInSingleFile, IncludeNativeLibrariesForSelfExtract, IncludeAllContentForSelfExtract) | **notes**: This is the profile that actually ships. Compression plus both self-extraction switches means the single file unpacks to a per-user cache before running, so a writable temporary area becomes a hard runtime requirement and first start pays a decompression cost.
- **capability**: Self-contained runtime embedding so the target machine needs no pre-installed runtime, plus a native launcher stub | **protocol**: none | **sourceUsed**: .NET self-contained deployment (SelfContained, UseAppHost) | **notes**: This is the whole product promise; do not substitute anything that reintroduces a runtime prerequisite on the target machine.
- **capability**: Culture-data-free (invariant) runtime mode and symbolic framework message keys | **protocol**: none | **sourceUsed**: InvariantGlobalization and UseSystemResourceKeys | **notes**: Both materially change user-visible behaviour in shipped builds — string comparison and formatting semantics, and error message text. Carry them only with that trade-off understood.
- **capability**: Platform-triple targeting so one source tree produces per-platform binaries | **protocol**: none | **sourceUsed**: .NET runtime identifiers win-x64 and linux-x64; documented but never built: osx-x64, osx-arm64 | **notes**: The triple strings appear literally inside the public release asset names, so changing the triple vocabulary changes the download contract.
- **capability**: Dependency manager supporting exact-version pins declared per project | **protocol**: none | **sourceUsed**: NuGet PackageReference. Pinned set: AWSSDK.BedrockRuntime 4.0.7.3; Azure.AI.OpenAI 2.1.0; Spectre.Console 0.51.1; Terminal.Gui 1.19.0 (graphical shell only); LLamaSharp 0.25.0; LLamaSharp.Backend.Cpu 0.25.0; LLamaSharp.Backend.Cuda12 0.25.0; Microsoft.NET.Test.Sdk 17.12.0; xunit 2.9.1; xunit.runner.visualstudio 2.8.1; Moq 4.20.69; coverlet.collector 6.0.2 | **notes**: No central version file and no committed lock file exist, so three pins are duplicated between the shared library and the console executable and must be synchronised by hand. An internal convention document claims central package management is in use; it is not.
- **capability**: Shipping large per-platform native machine-learning inference payloads inside the distributable | **protocol**: none | **sourceUsed**: LLamaSharp backend packages (CPU and CUDA-12 variants, both referenced simultaneously), extracted to <platform triple>/native/ with per-platform library file names | **notes**: This is the dominant size and packaging constraint, and it is why the native profile cannot produce a single file. Decide explicitly whether to ship one backend, both, or make them optional side-loads.
- **capability**: Reflection-driven structured serialization for every persisted document | **protocol**: JSON | **sourceUsed**: System.Text.Json in reflection mode, with no source-generated serializer context anywhere | **notes**: This is the single biggest incompatibility with the chosen dead-code-removal strategy. Either pick a serialization approach that is statically analysable, or do not trim.
- **capability**: Permission to compile raw pointer and unmanaged-memory access, scoped to one component | **protocol**: none | **sourceUsed**: AllowUnsafeBlocks on the shared library project only | **notes**: Required by the local-inference integration. Decide early whether the target language permits this and whether it survives an ahead-of-time or trimmed build.
- **capability**: Operating-system credential vault reached through a native platform API, guarded at run time | **protocol**: none | **sourceUsed**: Windows credential API entry points CredReadW, CredWriteW, CredDeleteW, CredFree in advapi32.dll | **notes**: A port needs an equivalent per-platform secret store plus the same run-time operating-system guard, or the one-binary-two-platforms property is lost.
- **capability**: Well-known per-user directory resolution (user profile, local application data, roaming application data) | **protocol**: none | **sourceUsed**: Platform special-folder lookups (Environment.SpecialFolder) | **notes**: Three different roots are used for one product. A port must pick per-platform conventions deliberately; the source did not, and there is no uninstall story.
- **capability**: Hosted continuous integration with manual dispatch, typed inputs, matrix fan-out onto per-platform agents, artefact upload/download between jobs with retention control, and a fan-in job dependency | **protocol**: none | **sourceUsed**: GitHub Actions on windows-latest and ubuntu-latest agents with actions/checkout@v4, actions/setup-dotnet@v4, actions/upload-artifact@v4, actions/download-artifact@v4 | **notes**: Both agent images are floating latest tags, so nothing about the build environment is reproducible. There is no dependency or build cache; every run restores from scratch.
- **capability**: Release publication service supporting tagging, a notes body, a pre-release flag, a draft flag and binary asset attachment | **protocol**: none | **sourceUsed**: GitHub Releases via softprops/action-gh-release@v1 (a floating major tag) | **notes**: The source publishes non-draft, so there is no review gate and no rollback. Pin the publishing component by revision rather than a floating tag.
- **capability**: Credential authorising release publication | **protocol**: none | **sourceUsed**: A repository secret named GH_PATT holding a long-lived personal access token, supplied to the publication step; the pipeline declares no permissions block | **notes**: Prefer a short-lived, scoped, ambient job credential. The source's own documentation claims no external secret is required, which is false.
- **capability**: POSIX-style shell available on both agent operating systems for scripting steps | **protocol**: POSIX sh | **sourceUsed**: shell: bash steps, including on the Windows agent, using grep with Perl-compatible regular expressions, mv, ls, stat, wc and heredocs | **notes**: The rename and version-scrape steps assume this. A port that cannot guarantee such a shell on every agent must rewrite those steps per platform.
- **capability**: Local developer build scripting on the maintainer's workstation platform | **protocol**: none | **sourceUsed**: Three Windows command-interpreter scripts (.bat) and one PowerShell script (.ps1) | **notes**: All four are Windows-only and all four block on a keypress, so there is no non-interactive path and no POSIX developer script at all, despite a POSIX binary being released.
- **capability**: Code-coverage collection integrated with the test runner | **protocol**: none | **sourceUsed**: coverlet.collector 6.0.2 | **notes**: Present but never invoked by any script or pipeline step; there is no coverage gate.
- **capability**: Copyleft source licence governing binary redistribution | **protocol**: none | **sourceUsed**: GNU General Public License version 3 (LICENSE) | **notes**: The published release attaches only executables — no licence text, no notice, no source offer. A port must decide and document how the equivalent obligations are met.
- **capability**: Full-screen text-mode user-interface toolkit: windows, menu bar, shortcut/status bar, z-ordered modal dialogs with their own event loop, tab strip, list view, radio group, checkbox, single-line and masked text fields, multi-line text area, buttons, scrollable canvas with programmable content extent, per-widget colour attributes, file open/save dialogs, message boxes, UI-thread marshalling | **protocol**: ANSI/VT terminal escape sequences; Windows console API on Windows | **sourceUsed**: Terminal.Gui 1.19.0 (pulls in NStack for its string type) | **notes**: Any curses-like toolkit works (ncurses, blessed, Textual, tview, ratatui). Required primitives: absolute plus anchored positioning, percentage widths, fill-minus-N sizing, per-widget colour schemes, a scrollable canvas whose content extent is set programmatically, and marshalling a timer callback back onto the UI thread. Menu hot-keys, focus traversal, dialog dismissal and scroll keys are toolkit defaults and are INFERRED. Docs claim 1.17.1; the project pins 1.19.0.
- **capability**: Rich console (non-full-screen) text renderer: coloured markup, panels, grids, tables, horizontal rules | **protocol**: ANSI SGR | **sourceUsed**: Spectre.Console 0.51.1 | **notes**: Used only by the demo-visualisation path and by dead code in the same folder. It writes straight to standard output and fights the full-screen interface for the screen. A reimplementation should drop it and render the demo in-window.
- **capability**: Terminal size query | **protocol**: none | **sourceUsed**: Console window width, read when laying out the demo grid | **notes**: Column count divided by 40, minimum 1, gives the number of demo grid columns - roughly 40 columns per token card.
- **capability**: Managed cross-platform runtime host for a terminal executable | **protocol**: none | **sourceUsed**: .NET 10 (net10.0), SDK pinned in global.json to 10.0.100-rc.1.25451.107 | **notes**: No ahead-of-time or single-file publish profile in CI; only Compact and SingleFile build configurations exist in the project file, both defaulting to a 64-bit Windows runtime identifier, and the release pipeline never publishes this executable at all.
- **capability**: Per-user home directory and per-user local-application-data directory | **protocol**: none | **sourceUsed**: Operating-system user-profile and local-application-data special folders | **notes**: Needed for the import/export picker start paths and the prompt-export default path. Settings live at <home>/.ChatDbg/settings.json, prompt records under <local application data>/ChatDbg/system_prompts.
- **capability**: Arbitrary local file read and write | **protocol**: none | **sourceUsed**: Whole-file text read of an imported prompt file and whole-file text write of an exported prompt | **notes**: Plain UTF-8 text, whole-file semantics, no size limit, no path validation.
- **capability**: Operating-system credential store: write and read one named secret | **protocol**: none (platform-specific vault API) | **sourceUsed**: Windows Credential Manager via direct native calls into the Windows credential API | **notes**: Platform-specific and it fails soft: off the supported platform every entry point returns 'not available' rather than raising, and the calling dialog discards the result, so the user is told the credential was saved when nothing was stored. Target names ChatDbg:AzureApiKey, ChatDbg:AwsAccessKey, ChatDbg:AwsSecretKey. A port needs a per-platform keychain abstraction and must propagate the real success flag into the message.
- **capability**: Read-only process environment variables as a credential channel | **protocol**: POSIX/Windows environment | **sourceUsed**: CHATDBG_AZURE_API_KEY; CHATDBG_AWS_ACCESS_KEY then AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID; CHATDBG_AWS_SECRET_KEY then AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY | **notes**: The only credential path that works on every platform; takes priority over both the credential store and the settings file; the interface never names any of these variables to the user.
- **capability**: Delayed callback marshalled onto the interface thread | **protocol**: none | **sourceUsed**: A 3-second fire-and-forget timer whose continuation is posted back to the main loop | **notes**: Needed for the transient status line. Each call creates an independent timer and does not cancel the previous one.
- **capability**: Structured document format for persisted state, plus a plain-text format for one export path | **protocol**: JSON | **sourceUsed**: JSON for settings, conversation exports and prompt records; plain text for prompt export | **notes**: This shell never parses these formats, it only chooses paths - but the conversation export is an interchange artefact that must stay re-importable (.json appended when the chosen path has no extension). The asymmetry is load-bearing: prompt export writes only the body as plain text while prompt import reads any file's whole text as the body.
- **capability**: Process exit codes | **protocol**: POSIX/Windows convention | **sourceUsed**: 0 on clean shutdown, 1 after a fatal startup or teardown error | **notes**: The only machine-readable output this feature produces; the fatal path also writes the single line 'Fatal error: <message>' to the plain console.
- **capability**: Remote and local model inference (indirect) | **protocol**: HTTPS/REST for hosted providers; in-process inference for the local one | **sourceUsed**: Azure OpenAI (Azure.AI.OpenAI 2.1.0), Amazon Bedrock (AWSSDK.BedrockRuntime 4.0.7.3), local GGUF models via LLamaSharp | **notes**: This feature never speaks these protocols; it names the three provider keys azure, bedrock, llama and surfaces their error text verbatim in a modal box.
- **capability**: Mouse input reporting in a terminal | **protocol**: xterm mouse reporting | **sourceUsed**: Handled by the interface toolkit - probability markers and every button are clickable | **notes**: Keyboard-only operation must remain possible through hot-keys and focus traversal.
