- **capability**: Local transformer inference engine exposing a tokenizer and a streaming text generator | **protocol**: Native shared library invoked through a language binding; model weights in a single-file quantized container format (GGUF) | **sourceUsed**: LLamaSharp 0.25.0 with LLamaSharp.Backend.Cpu and LLamaSharp.Backend.Cuda12, over llama.cpp | **notes**: Only three capabilities are exercised: load-model-with-explicit-context-size, tokenize-string-with-beginning-of-sequence-marker to integer vocabulary IDs, and stream-generate-with-max-token-budget and an (empty) stop-sequence list. A detokenize (ID to string) call is required by the intended behaviour but was never wired up - the clone should implement it rather than reproduce the <token_N> placeholder. The backend is loaded and unloaded once per command, twice for the inspection command. GPU offload is never enabled by this feature despite a CUDA-12 backend being referenced unconditionally.
- **capability**: Managed runtime with asynchronous streaming enumeration and background-thread offload of blocking native calls | **protocol**: - | **sourceUsed**: .NET 10 (net10.0); SDK pinned to 10.0.100-rc.1.25451.107 in global.json with rollForward latestFeature | **notes**: Model loading and context creation are pushed onto worker threads; tokenization and the streaming loop run on the caller. There is no cancellation token anywhere in the feature - a long load or generation cannot be aborted.
- **capability**: Structured-document serialization with explicit field naming | **protocol**: JSON | **sourceUsed**: System.Text.Json with per-member name attributes; indented output | **notes**: Only the per-step analysis record, its candidate entries and its state snapshot are annotated; the six inspection result types are never serialized. The exported document is a JSON array and its field names (step, tokenText, tokenId, probability, logprob, promptOffset, topCandidates, systemDebugInfo, modelState) are a compatibility surface.
- **capability**: Rich terminal rendering library | **protocol**: ANSI terminal escape sequences | **sourceUsed**: Spectre.Console 0.51.1 | **notes**: Imported by the two stub commands but never called. The commands that actually render use plain fixed-width text with pipe separators and dashed rules - reproduce that, not a styled table.
- **capability**: Full-screen terminal UI toolkit | **protocol**: ANSI terminal | **sourceUsed**: Terminal.Gui 1.19.0 (full-screen shell only) | **notes**: Relevant only because it breaks this feature: raw standard-output writes and a blocking standard-input read cannot work under a toolkit that owns the screen and keyboard.
- **capability**: Per-user configuration storage on local disk | **protocol**: JSON file | **sourceUsed**: ~/.ChatDbg/settings.json, falling back to the platform temp directory when the user-profile folder resolves empty | **notes**: Read-only from this feature's perspective; four keys are consumed (provider, modelId, maxTokens, logProbabilitiesTopK) and are never re-validated at read time.
- **capability**: Per-user diagnostic log storage with daily rollover | **protocol**: Plain text file | **sourceUsed**: Platform application-data folder + ChatDbg/Logs, files named llamasharp_{yyyyMMdd}.log | **notes**: Append mode for threshold flushes, overwrite mode for an explicit save-to-path; directory created on demand; all failures swallowed. On platforms where the application-data folder resolves empty the path becomes relative.
- **capability**: Native-library log interception | **protocol**: A log callback registered with the native inference library, receiving a severity level and a message string | **sourceUsed**: NativeLogConfig.llama_log_set from the inference binding | **notes**: Registered once and never unregistered. The clone needs an equivalent sink that timestamps each line, tags it with a level, buffers it in memory under a lock, and flushes past a 10000-character threshold.
- **capability**: Console standard input and standard output | **protocol**: Plain text, no escape sequences | **sourceUsed**: Direct process standard output and a blocking standard-input line read | **notes**: The entire report bypasses the command result contract; only the one-line completion message travels back through it. The consent gate requires an interactive terminal - there is no non-interactive or scripted mode and no flag to pre-answer it.
- **capability**: Unit test framework with mocking | **protocol**: - | **sourceUsed**: xUnit 2.9.1 with Moq 4.20.69 | **notes**: No mocks are used for this feature because the inspection operations are process-wide static utilities with no substitutable seam. A reimplementer should introduce an injectable tokenizer/generator abstraction.
- **capability**: In-process large-language-model inference engine that loads weights from a single local file, creates a sized inference context, and streams decoded text pieces through an interactive-executor plus chat-session abstraction | **protocol**: none — native library loaded in-process via a foreign-function interface | **sourceUsed**: LLamaSharp 0.25.0 (managed binding over llama.cpp), pinned in src/Xcaciv.ChatDbg.Core/Xcaciv.ChatDbg.Core.csproj:13 | **notes**: Substitute must support inference parameters carrying maximum-new-tokens, a list of stop strings, and a sampling policy configured with temperature and a top-K selection limit; must stream text pieces (not token ids), since every counter and record counts pieces; and must own conversation continuity in a session object, because only one message is sent per turn. Ideally exposes the current per-step score vector and a detokenise entry point — the source obtained neither cleanly and fell back to run-time name resolution, which is why introspection produces nothing.
- **capability**: Host-processor inference backend supplying platform-native shared libraries | **protocol**: none | **sourceUsed**: LLamaSharp.Backend.Cpu 0.25.0 (csproj:14), binaries under runtimes/<platform-id>/native/ | **notes**: Documented artefacts to verify are win-x64/native/llama.dll and linux-x64/native/libllama.so. On Windows the vendor C++ runtime redistributables are required; a missing or mismatched binary is the leading cause of the uncatchable native crash during load.
- **capability**: Optional graphics-accelerated inference backend | **protocol**: vendor compute toolkit, version 12 series (CUDA 12.x) | **sourceUsed**: LLamaSharp.Backend.Cuda12 0.25.0 (csproj:15) | **notes**: The only accelerator backend anywhere in the source — no Metal, Vulkan, ROCm/HIP, OpenCL or processor-variant package exists — so graphics acceleration means one vendor or nothing. On any other accelerator a non-zero layer count is accepted, persisted, displayed, then silently ignored or fatal in the native loader. Both backends are referenced unconditionally in the same project, which the source's own troubleshooting document names as a cause of native load failures.
- **capability**: Quantised model weight file format | **protocol**: GGUF | **sourceUsed**: user-supplied .gguf files; docs name a 7B chat model at 4-bit medium quantisation as known-good and rank Q3_K_M / Q4_K_M / Q5_K_M by size and quality | **notes**: The file format is a user-facing compatibility requirement — users already hold GGUF files. Pre-GGUF formats are explicitly out of scope. Nothing validates header, extension or size; existence is the only check, so a zero-byte file passes.
- **capability**: Hook redirecting the inference engine's own native log output into the host process | **protocol**: none | **sourceUsed**: the binding's process-global native-log callback registration | **notes**: Must accept a (level, message) pair arriving on arbitrary threads, which is why the buffer is lock-guarded. The source registers it globally on first use and never unregisters it, so a second instance silently steals it.
- **capability**: Local filesystem access | **protocol**: none | **sourceUsed**: platform file APIs | **notes**: Reads the model file (existence and size only). Writes an append-only daily log file under a per-user roaming application-data directory created on demand, plus optional exports to caller-chosen paths.
- **capability**: Structured document serialisation with pinned property names | **protocol**: JSON | **sourceUsed**: the platform's built-in JSON serialiser with explicit per-property names, indented output for the analysis export | **notes**: The analysis-record, candidate and state-snapshot key names are the export contract and must be preserved verbatim. Note the display probability record uses snake_case 'top_alternatives' while every other record uses camelCase.
- **capability**: Managed runtime supporting run-time member lookup by name and late binding | **protocol**: none | **sourceUsed**: .NET 10 (net10.0), SDK pinned to 10.0.100-rc.1.25451.107 in global.json, AllowUnsafeBlocks enabled in the core library | **notes**: The score-vector read resolves an operation by name and the detokenise call is late-bound; both are hostile to ahead-of-time compilation and trimming, which the source's compact publish profile enables — under which both silently degrade to 'no alternatives'. The source pins a release-candidate runtime while its own troubleshooting document names an older runtime plus engine version 0.17.0 as the proven combination.
- **capability**: Process-wide mutual exclusion primitives | **protocol**: none | **sourceUsed**: two process-wide binary semaphores, one for loading and one for generating | **notes**: Required because the native engine and the cached context are not safe for concurrent use. Neither has a timeout, queue bound or cancellation path; only one of the two is ever disposed.
- **capability**: Rich terminal rendering of the resulting probability tables | **protocol**: ANSI | **sourceUsed**: Spectre.Console 0.51.1 (csproj:16) | **notes**: Owned by the output-rendering feature; listed because the probability records exist to feed it.
- **capability**: Per-user structured settings store | **protocol**: JSON | **sourceUsed**: a camelCase-keyed indented JSON document at <user profile>/.ChatDbg/settings.json, falling back to the system temporary directory when the user-profile folder is unavailable or blank | **notes**: Owned by the settings feature; listed because every knob this feature reads arrives through it.

---

## Data entities

- **entity**: Provider response | **fields**: reply text (nullable string, defaults to empty string); ordered token-confidence list (nullable, null when absent, never an empty list from either construction shape); elapsed-time value in seconds (number, default 0, never populated by the hosted OpenAI backend); error message (nullable string, default null, never populated by the hosted OpenAI backend). Exactly two construction shapes: text alone (confidence list stays null) and text plus list (the caller's list is stored by reference, not copied, cloned or sorted). | **lifecycle**: Created per send call; never persisted as a whole. Its confidence list is persisted indirectly when the shell copies it onto the assistant message it appends to history. Discarded when the turn completes.
- **entity**: Token confidence record | **fields**: token text (string, defaults empty, wire name 'token'); natural-log probability (number, wire name 'logprob'; at most 0 for measured data; fabricated data uses ln(0.9); map-shaped alternatives can land on 0); derived probability (number, computed as e^(log probability), not persisted); alternatives (nullable list of the same record type, wire name 'top_alternatives'; only one level of nesting — alternatives are always created without their own list). | **lifecycle**: Created by strict extraction, tolerant extraction, or fabrication; mutated only during construction; deleted with its owning chat message.
- **entity**: Command (the contract) | **fields**: Name (short text, read-only, lower-case ASCII, the registry key and the token typed after the slash, unique within a registry); Description (one-sentence text, read-only, shown by both help renderers); Usage (text that may contain newlines, read-only, shown only by detailed help, conventionally begins with slash+name, may be recomputed from live state on each read); Execute (asynchronous operation: ordered list of text -> Command Result) | **lifecycle**: Created once during host start-up in a fixed literal order with all collaborators passed explicitly; never mutated, never re-created, never disposed (the contract has no disposal member); held for the process lifetime.
- **entity**: Command Result | **fields**: Success (boolean, set by factory); Message (optional text, defaults to absent, free-form, may be multi-line and arbitrarily long, rendered verbatim with no length limit, truncation, escaping or sanitisation); ExitRequested (boolean, defaults to false). Built by exactly three factories: success(message?) -> {true, message, false}; error(message) -> {false, message, false} with no validation of the message; exit() -> {true, absent, true}. All three fields are individually settable so any combination is constructible. | **lifecycle**: Created inside a command's execute, returned up one level, consumed immediately by the dispatcher, then discarded. Never stored, never persisted, never compared.
- **entity**: Command Registry | **fields**: Mutable map from lower-case name text to command instance, with ordinal key equality. Live contents in both shipped hosts are 15 entries: clear, demologprobs, exit, export, help, import, inject, inspect, logprobs, model, pop, prompt, quit, set, tokenize. | **lifecycle**: One per process, owned by the host. Populated once at start-up; duplicate names silently overwrite; the help capability is inserted last and given a live reference to the same map (not a copy) so it lists itself. Read-only for the rest of the process - no runtime registration, unregistration or aliasing. Never persisted.
- **entity**: Marketplace request body (Claude-family shape) — wire format, owned by this feature | **fields**: anthropic_version (constant string 'bedrock-2023-05-31'); max_tokens (integer, from the max-output-tokens setting); temperature (number); system (string, the current system-prompt text); messages (ordered array of {role: string, content: string}); logprobs (boolean, always present); top_logprobs (integer, top-K when enabled else 0). Member order is as listed; member names are snake_case verbatim. | **lifecycle**: Constructed per chat turn from the settings record and the conversation history, serialised once to a string for the diagnostic trace and once to UTF-8 bytes for the request, then discarded. Never persisted.
- **entity**: Marketplace request body (generic shape) — wire format, owned by this feature | **fields**: max_tokens (integer); temperature (number); messages (ordered array whose index 0 is always a synthetic {role: 'system', content: <system-prompt text>} followed by the flattened conversation); logprobs (boolean); top_logprobs (integer). No anthropic_version and no top-level system member. | **lifecycle**: Same as the Claude-family shape: built per turn, traced, sent, discarded.
- **entity**: Marketplace reply body — wire format, consumed by this feature | **fields**: One of three recognised shapes, evaluated in fixed order: (1) content: non-empty array whose first element may carry text (string or null); (2) completion: string or null; (3) anything else. Optionally a top-level logprobs member: an array of {token: string, logprob: number, top_logprobs|alternatives|top_alternatives: array of {token, logprob}}, or an object carrying tokens: array of {token, logprob|log_prob}. | **lifecycle**: Read in full into memory as a single string, traced verbatim, parsed to a document, then discarded. No streaming, no size cap.
- **entity**: Response envelope (produced; shape owned by the provider abstraction) | **fields**: text (optional string — always set by this feature); token-probability list (optional ordered list — set only when at least one entry parsed, never an empty list); total elapsed time (decimal seconds — never populated, stays 0); error message (optional string — never populated; failures are raised). | **lifecycle**: Created per turn, handed to the calling host, which appends its text (and any probability list) to the conversation history.
- **entity**: Token-probability entry (produced; shape owned by Token Probability Analysis) | **fields**: token (string, defaults to empty when the source value is null); log probability (decimal — raw for primary tokens, ALREADY EXPONENTIATED for alternatives); derived probability (computed as e^(stored value), never serialised); alternatives (optional ordered list of the same entry type — always empty in the object-shaped parse path). | **lifecycle**: Created per turn during reply parsing, attached to the response envelope, then lives as long as the conversation history holds the assistant turn.
- **entity**: Settings record (consumed; owned by Settings & Configuration) | **fields**: Fields read here: provider (azure|bedrock|llama, default azure); model identifier (free text, default 'gpt-4'); AWS region (free text, default 'us-east-1', unvalidated); resolved access key and resolved secret key (computed secret strings, never serialised); temperature (0.0-2.0, default 0.7); max output tokens (1-8192, default 1000); system-prompt text (computed, never persisted); probabilities-enabled (default false); top-K (1-20, default 5). | **lifecycle**: Read-only here. Persisted by the settings feature at <user profile>/.ChatDbg/settings.json under the member names provider, modelId, temperature, maxTokens, awsRegion, enableLogProbabilities, logProbabilitiesTopK, plus deprecated awsAccessKey/awsSecretKey that no supported user action can populate.
- **entity**: Conversation history (consumed; owned by the hosts) | **fields**: Ordered list of turns, each with role (free text; only 'assistant' is special-cased here), content text, a UTC timestamp, an is-command flag, and an optional per-token probability list. Only role, content and the is-command flag are read by this feature. | **lifecycle**: Read in full on every turn; never truncated, reordered, deduplicated or windowed by this feature. The user's turn is appended before the readiness check, so a failed turn leaves an orphan user message.
- **entity**: Back end instance | **fields**: An already-released boolean flag and an optional injected client-construction seam. No other mutable state. | **lifecycle**: Created once per host process at start-up; released at shutdown by the console host only. Release is a no-op beyond setting the flag and is idempotent.
- **entity**: Credential settings (the credential-relevant members of the shared settings record) | **fields**: vault-enabled flag (boolean, persisted, default false); deprecated Azure/hosted-provider key (string, persisted PLAINTEXT, default ""); deprecated cloud-inference access key (string, persisted PLAINTEXT, default ""); deprecated cloud-inference secret key (string, persisted PLAINTEXT, default ""); resolved hosted-provider key, resolved access key, resolved secret key (strings, computed per read, never persisted, empty string when unavailable) | **lifecycle**: Created with defaults when the settings file is absent and immediately written to disk (the new file contains the flag as false and all three plaintext keys as ""). Mutated by the settings command, the graphical dialog, and the enable/store/bulk-migrate operations; every mutation rewrites the whole file. The plaintext fields are only ever cleared (never written non-empty) by the product; they become non-empty only via hand-editing or an older build. Never deleted. A corrupt file silently reverts everything to defaults for the session, and the next save overwrites it.
- **entity**: Vault credential entry (external entity owned by the operating system) | **fields**: entry name/target (one of the three fixed ChatDbg:* literals); kind (generic, constant 1); flags (0); blob (secret encoded UTF-16LE); blob size (byte length, 2x character count); persistence (2 = local machine); account label (literal "ChatDbg"); comment (literal "ChatDbg API Credential"); last-written timestamp (set by the OS, never read); attributes (count 0, null) | **lifecycle**: Created or overwritten by the store operation and by bulk migration. Read by credential resolution and by the source lookup, matching on entry name only and ignoring the account label and comment. NEVER deleted by the product: a delete primitive exists with zero call sites. Entries survive process exit and reboot and are scoped to the operating-system user account, so one OS user cannot keep two product profiles apart.
- **entity**: Credential environment variables (external, process-scoped, read-only) | **fields**: CHATDBG_AZURE_API_KEY; CHATDBG_AWS_ACCESS_KEY; AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID; CHATDBG_AWS_SECRET_KEY; AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY | **lifecycle**: Never written by the product. Read fresh on every credential resolution and again on every source lookup, so a change takes effect on the very next read with no restart. An existing-but-empty variable is treated as absent and resolution continues to the next name.
- **entity**: Conversation record | **fields**: messages (ordered list of Message; persisted key 'messages'; default empty; order is authoritative; no size limit) | session identifier (string; key 'sessionId'; default a freshly generated canonical lowercase hyphenated UUID; overwritten wholesale on import; NOT reset by clear; never read by anything else) | created-at (date-time; key 'createdAt'; default current UTC instant at construction; written on export, read but DISCARDED on import; never mutated after construction) | **lifecycle**: Created once, empty, when a shell process starts. Mutated by append, inject, pop, clear and import. Read by export and by the provider layer on every request. Import mutates it in place rather than replacing it, so object identity is stable for the whole process lifetime and both shells plus every command see changes immediately. Destroyed when the process exits — there is no autosave and no autoload.
- **entity**: Message | **fields**: role (string; key 'role'; default empty; inject enforces user|assistant|system after lower-casing, append and import enforce nothing) | content (string; key 'content'; default empty; no length limit, no emptiness check, may contain newlines) | timestamp (date-time; key 'timestamp'; current UTC instant at append/inject time; NOT re-sequenced when injected mid-list) | hidden-from-model flag (boolean; key 'isCommand'; default false; excludes the message from hosted-provider requests; never set true by shipping code) | token log probabilities (optional list; key 'logProbabilities'; default null and written literally; attached only on the log-probability reply path) | has-token-log-probabilities (derived boolean, NOT persisted; true only when the list exists and has at least one element) | **lifecycle**: Constructed by append or inject with a fresh UTC timestamp; placed at the end (append) or at an index in [0,count) (inject, degrading to append when out of range). Removed only by pop (last only) or clear (all). Replaced wholesale by import. Serialized on export and reconstructed on import with no validation.
- **entity**: Conversation history file | **fields**: A single JSON object, 2-space indented, UTF-8 without BOM, no schema-version field. Top-level keys in declaration order: messages, sessionId, createdAt. Each message in order: role, content, timestamp, isCommand, logProbabilities. Each token entry in order: token, logprob, top_alternatives (note the snake-case inconsistency). null is written literally for absent lists; timestamps are ISO-8601 UTC with 7 fractional digits and a Z suffix; numbers use shortest round-trip form; strings use an aggressive escaping profile (' for apostrophe, ` for backtick, + for plus, \uXXXX for all non-ASCII). | **lifecycle**: Created or overwritten on demand by export, at a user-supplied path with '~/' expansion and '.json' appended only when the name has no extension at all. Overwritten in place with no temp file, no rename and no warning, so an interrupted write destroys the previous contents. Read whole into memory by import with no size cap. No versioning, no negotiation, no cleanup — the file outlives the process.
- **entity**: Settings record | **fields**: provider (azure|bedrock|llama, default azure, stored lowercase); modelId (default gpt-4); temperature (decimal, 0..2 inclusive, default 0.7); maxTokens (int, 1..8192 inclusive, default 1000); azureEndpoint (nullable text, default null, unvalidated); awsRegion (text, default us-east-1, unvalidated); systemPromptName (text, default 'default'); systemPromptBody (long text, NOT persisted, re-resolved each launch, built-in fallback body); enableLogProbabilities (bool, default false); logProbabilitiesTopK (int, 1..20 inclusive, default 5); showAllTokens (bool, default false); gridViewForTokens (bool, default false); gridViewMaxAlternatives (int, 1..20 inclusive, default 5); useWindowsCredentialManager (bool, default false); llamaContextSize (int, 512..32768 inclusive, default 4096); llamaGpuLayerCount (int, 0..100 inclusive, default 0); llamaGpuDevice (nullable text, default null, unvalidated); llamaThreads (int, 0..64 inclusive, default 0); llamaBatchSize (int, 1..2048 inclusive, default 512); azureApiKey/awsAccessKey/awsSecretKey (deprecated slots, default empty string, write-blocked via commands, always emitted); plus three non-persisted resolved credential values and two derived predicates ('does the document hold any secret', 'where did secret X come from') | **lifecycle**: Created at process start with built-in defaults, and again by the store when the document is absent. One instance per process, shared by reference, mutated in place by /set, /model, /logprobs, /prompt use|edit, the windowed Settings dialog, the System Prompts dialog and the toggle-log-probs menu action; every successful mutation persists the whole record immediately. Read from disk exactly once per process; no re-read, no file watching, no cache invalidation. Never deleted: there is no reset, unset, clear or restore-defaults path anywhere - the only route back to defaults is deleting the document out of band.
- **entity**: Settings document (persisted file) | **fields**: Exactly 21 keys: provider, modelId, temperature, maxTokens, azureEndpoint, awsRegion, systemPromptName, enableLogProbabilities, logProbabilitiesTopK, showAllTokens, gridViewForTokens, gridViewMaxAlternatives, useWindowsCredentialManager, llamaContextSize, llamaGpuLayerCount, llamaGpuDevice, llamaThreads, llamaBatchSize, azureApiKey, awsAccessKey, awsSecretKey. Indented, human-editable. The system-prompt body and resolved credentials never appear. | **lifecycle**: Path resolved as caller-supplied base directory, else <user profile>/.ChatDbg, else OS temp directory on null/empty/throw; file name defaults to settings.json. Created with all defaults on first load when absent (directory created too). Overwritten in full on every save; never partially patched, merged or backed up. A corrupt document is left untouched on load failure and only replaced by the next successful save. Deleted only out of band by the user.
- **entity**: Prompt record (system prompt) | **fields**: name (short text, primary key, also the basis of the file name after lossy sanitization, never validated); content (long free multi-line text, the instruction string, no length limit); description (short text, display-only, changeable only via the graphical edit form); createdAt (UTC timestamp, defaults to construction/deserialization instant, never updated after creation); lastUsedAt (nullable UTC timestamp, written as explicit null when unset) | **lifecycle**: Created by seeding (library empty at launch), by /prompt create, by /prompt import, or by the graphical Create/Import forms. Mutated by /prompt edit (content only), the graphical Edit form (description plus content), and last-used stamping (full read-modify-write of the record). Overwritten silently by same-name import or graphical create. Deleted by /prompt delete (refused while active) or the graphical Delete button (no active guard). Emptying the library causes the four built-ins to reappear at the next launch.
- **entity**: Prompt library store (directory) | **fields**: path = <per-user local app data>/ChatDbg/system_prompts; contents = files matching *.json, non-recursive; optional base-directory override (tests only; whitespace or empty falls back to the default) | **lifecycle**: Created at store construction if absent (an uncaught failure aborts application startup). Never deleted by this feature. Seeded with four built-ins whenever it loads zero records - checked at every launch. Re-read from disk on every operation; no cache, no index, no locking, no concurrency control.
- **entity**: Exported prompt file | **fields**: plain text containing ONLY the prompt's content - no name, no description, no timestamps, no wrapper format | **lifecycle**: Written by /prompt export (default: <Documents>/chatdbg_prompt_<sanitized name>.txt; on a Linux host with no XDG user-dirs the Documents part is the empty string and the file lands in the working directory) or by the graphical Export dialog (default: <user home>/<name>.txt, unsanitized). Overwrites any existing file without asking. Never deleted by this feature. Re-importing one loses description, created-at and last-used.
- **entity**: Foreign fields this feature reads/writes on the settings entity (owned by Settings & Configuration) | **fields**: active prompt NAME (persisted key systemPromptName, default literal "default"); active prompt TEXT (runtime-only, explicitly excluded from the settings file) | **lifecycle**: The name is read at startup, for the '(current)' marker and for the delete guard; it is written by every select operation (/prompt use, /set systemPrompt, graphical Use), each of which persists settings immediately. The text is written by every select operation and by startup resolution, and read by every provider on every model call. No referential integrity: settings may name a prompt that does not exist.
- **entity**: Diagnostic recorder configuration and state | **fields**: Log directory (path string; default = per-user roaming app-data root + 'ChatDbg' + 'Logs'; never null); File capture enabled (boolean, default true); Debug-stream echo enabled (boolean, default true); Console echo enabled (boolean, default false); Maximum buffer size (integer characters, default 10000, no validation, no clamp, no range); Capture buffer (mutable text accumulator of whole formatted lines); Armed latch (private boolean, one-way false->true); Shutdown latch (private boolean, one-way false->true); Buffer lock (mutual-exclusion primitive, must be re-entrant on the same thread) | **lifecycle**: Created with the owning local-inference subsystem; all five settings mutable at any time but with NO runtime configuration surface anywhere in the product (rebuild required). Buffer created empty, appended by both producer paths, drained by flush, emptied by clear, snapshotted non-destructively by export, flushed once at shutdown. Armed latch set on first successful arm and never reset, not even by shutdown. Unbounded growth when file capture is off.
- **entity**: Rolling daily log file | **fields**: Path = <log directory>/llamasharp_<yyyyMMdd>.log (flush-time local date, no separators); Content = append-only plain text, UTF-8 with no byte-order mark, zero or more entry lines, no header, no footer, no schema marker | **lifecycle**: Created lazily on the first NON-EMPTY flush of a given local calendar day; appended to thereafter; never truncated, never rotated by size, never compressed, never deleted or pruned by the product; never read back by the product. Two processes on the same machine on the same day interleave into one file with no coordination and no distinguishing marker.
- **entity**: Exported log snapshot | **fields**: Path = caller-supplied file path (parent directory auto-created when non-empty; bare file name writes relative to the process working directory; no extension convention, no validation of any kind); Content = whole-file OVERWRITE of the current buffer, UTF-8 with no byte-order mark | **lifecycle**: Created or overwritten on demand with no prompt and no backup; buffer is NOT cleared (non-destructive); no emptiness guard, so an empty buffer yields a 0-byte file; the 'Logs saved to <path>' confirmation entry is appended AFTER the write and is therefore never present in the file it describes; failures are swallowed and the caller cannot distinguish success from failure; never read back by the product.
- **entity**: Log entry (value, not persisted as a record) | **fields**: Timestamp (local date-time, rendered yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm:ss.fff, exactly three fractional-second digits, NO timezone or offset); Level (free-text token - application vocabulary INFO/WARN/ERROR/DEBUG all upper-case; engine vocabulary is whatever the engine's severity type renders as, INFERRED mixed-case such as Info/Warn); Message (text - engine-sourced messages have trailing whitespace stripped, application-sourced messages do not). Rendered as '[timestamp] [level] message' plus a platform line terminator. | **lifecycle**: Formatted, appended to the buffer under the lock in strict production order, optionally echoed outside the lock to the debug stream and standard output, then flushed to the rolling file or exported. Never parsed back, never indexed, never queried. No structured (key-value or document) log format exists anywhere in this feature.
- **entity**: Session state (not persisted) | **fields**: transcript reference; live settings reference; command registry (map from lowercase name to command, 15 entries); provider table (map from lowercase key to adapter, exactly 3 entries: azure, bedrock, llama); live system-prompt content string; disposed flag | **lifecycle**: Created eagerly in one constructor pass; the registry and provider table are never mutated after construction; the settings record is overwritten field-by-field at startup and then mutated in place by commands; the whole state is released at shutdown and the transcript is discarded unless explicitly exported.
- **entity**: Prompt string | **fields**: literal form 'ChatDbg ({provider}/{modelId})> ', written without a trailing line break | **lifecycle**: Rebuilt from the live settings on every loop iteration, so provider/model changes appear on the very next prompt.
- **entity**: Command result (consumed contract) | **fields**: success boolean; optional message string (may be multi-line); exitRequested boolean, default false | **lifecycle**: Produced by each command invocation, consumed once by the loop: exit flag checked first, then a non-empty message rendered as one write prefixed with the success or failure marker. Never stored.
- **entity**: Transcript message (written by this feature) | **fields**: role (only 'user' and 'assistant' are written here); content (raw untrimmed input line, or reply text); timestamp (UTC); isCommand (always false from this feature); logProbabilities (populated only on the probability path) | **lifecycle**: Appended only - the user message before any validation, the assistant message after a successful call. Never removed, never rolled back on error. Discarded at exit unless exported.
- **entity**: Rendered probability block (transient view model) | **fields**: selection mode (show-all vs sample); layout (grid vs list); per token: absolute 1-based index, escaped token text, coloured formatted probability, capped alternatives list | **lifecycle**: Computed per reply immediately after the reply text is printed, written to the terminal, and discarded. Never persisted.
- **entity**: Local-model diagnostic log file | **fields**: path <roaming app data>/ChatDbg/Logs/llamasharp_{yyyyMMdd}.log; date component uses the LOCAL date; append mode | **lifecycle**: Created or appended at shutdown when the local-model adapter is disposed, which happens in every session regardless of provider.
- **entity**: Token confidence record | **fields**: token text (defaults to empty, never null by construction, may contain control characters; persisted as `token`); stored log-probability (double-precision, no range validation; persisted as `logprob`); derived probability (double-precision, computed as e^logprob, NEVER persisted); alternatives (optional ordered list of records of the same shape; persisted as `top_alternatives`, snake-cased against the camel-case convention; nested alternatives normally absent) | **lifecycle**: Created by a provider adapter while parsing a completion, or fabricated by the demonstration command or by the cloud adapter's silent fallback. Attached to a response record, then copied by reference onto the assistant message in conversation history. Never mutated after attachment except by the local in-process adapter, which back-fills the previous token's log-probability and alternatives one generation step late during generation. Destroyed with the conversation history (clear, pop, process exit) or persisted verbatim inside an exported conversation file and restored on import.
- **entity**: Response record (transport) | **fields**: text (optional, defaults to empty; `text`); confidence list (optional list of token confidence records; `logProbabilities`; absent - not empty - when not requested or unavailable); total time in seconds (`totalTime`; demonstration sets 0.5, local provider sets measured elapsed, error responses set 0); error message (optional; `errorMessage`, set by the local provider on failure) | **lifecycle**: Constructed by a provider adapter per conversation turn, or by the demonstration command. Two construction forms: text-only (confidence list left absent) and text-plus-list (the SAME list instance is stored by reference, not copied). Consumed by the shell within the turn, then discarded; only its text and confidence list survive, on the history message.
- **entity**: Token-probability configuration section of the settings document | **fields**: enableLogProbabilities (boolean, default false); logProbabilitiesTopK (integer, default 5, range 1-20); showAllTokens (boolean, default false); gridViewForTokens (boolean, default false); gridViewMaxAlternatives (integer, default 5, range 1-20) | **lifecycle**: Loaded at startup from <user profile>/.ChatDbg/settings.json (created with defaults if absent; OS temp directory as fallback location). Mutated by the probability configuration command, the generic settings command, the full-screen settings dialog and one View-menu item, each of which writes the WHOLE settings document back immediately. Save failures are swallowed (change survives only in memory); load failures silently yield all defaults. The plain console shell restores only the first two values at startup, so the three display values revert to defaults on every restart of that shell.
- **entity**: Conversation message extension (owned by chat history, extended by this feature) | **fields**: logProbabilities (optional list of token confidence records) plus a derived, never-persisted has-confidence-data flag defined as list non-null AND count > 0 | **lifecycle**: Set once when the assistant message is appended to history at the end of a capture-enabled turn; read by every renderer and by the per-message indicator; serialized into and restored from exported conversation files; destroyed with the history.
- **entity**: Panel visibility state (full-screen host) | **fields**: panel-visible flag (boolean); currently displayed message (nullable reference to a chat message); transcript width fraction (100% when hidden, 60% when the panel is shown) | **lifecycle**: Computed at start-up from the enable-log-probabilities setting AND the presence of at least one assistant message carrying data; flipped by the menu toggle, by activating a probability indicator, and by auto-open when a new reply arrives with data. The displayed-message reference is set by those three paths and is never cleared. Purely in-memory; nothing is persisted.
- **entity**: Role colour schemes | **fields**: five named schemes — user (white on dark grey), assistant (bright yellow on blue), system (green on black), divider (grey on black, never used), lozenge/indicator (bright green on black; bright green on dark grey when focused) | **lifecycle**: Created once in the full-screen window's constructor; immutable thereafter; discarded when the window closes.
- **entity**: Confidence heat buckets | **fields**: a fixed array of 10 colour schemes indexed by clamp(floor(probability × 10), 0, 9): bright red, red, bright magenta, magenta, bright blue, blue, cyan, bright cyan, bright yellow, bright green — all over a black background | **lifecycle**: Created once in the full-screen window's constructor; immutable thereafter.
- **entity**: Status label state | **fields**: current status text (string); pending revert timer (never cancelled); idle text template 'Provider: X | Model: Y | Prompt: Z' | **lifecycle**: Overwritten by any transient message and refreshed immediately; a 3000 ms background timer marshals back to the interface thread and restores the idle text. Overlapping timers can clear a newer message early.
- **entity**: Scroll geometry (transcript and panel) | **fields**: content width, content height, scroll offset — per view; initial placeholder canvases of 80×1000 (transcript) and 50×1000 (panel) | **lifecycle**: Recomputed on every transcript or panel re-render: transcript height becomes max(used height, viewport height) and auto-scrolls to the bottom; panel width becomes max(longest rendered line + 5, 50) and scrolls to the top. The panel's empty-data path skips this update entirely, leaving stale geometry.
- **entity**: Per-call render buffers | **fields**: grid row buffer (list of cards up to the column count), card body builders, panel longest-line tracker | **lifecycle**: Allocated per rendering call, flushed when a row fills or the last token is processed, and discarded when the call returns. Nothing is cached between calls; the transcript and panel are torn down and rebuilt widget-by-widget on every refresh.
- **entity**: Build profile (configuration) | **fields**: profile name (one of four named identifiers; only two discoverable from the workspace descriptor); native ahead-of-time compilation (bool); single-file bundling (bool); in-bundle compression (bool); include native payloads in self-extraction (bool); include all content in self-extraction (bool); dead-code trimming enabled (bool); trimming granularity (full|partial); suppress trim diagnostics (bool); self-contained (bool); target platform triple (string, defaults to 64-bit Windows only when not supplied); emit native launcher stub (bool); debug information (none); emit runtime-configuration side-car (bool, false in both); retained localisation languages (English only); optimise (bool); synthesise identity metadata (false in native, true in bundled); emit target-framework marker (false in native, true in bundled); invariant globalisation (bool); symbolic resource keys (bool); native code-generator preference (size); fold identical method bodies (bool); emit stack-trace metadata (false); strip native symbols (bool) | **lifecycle**: Declared statically and byte-identically in the two executable project descriptors; never generated, mutated or deleted at build time. The shared library and test project declare no configuration-conditioned settings at all.
- **entity**: Product identity metadata | **fields**: product version (3-part, 1.0.0); assembly version (4-part, 1.0.0.0); file version (4-part, 1.0.0.0); title (ChatDbg Shell, used by both executables); description (Cross-platform chat debugging tool with AWS Bedrock and Azure OpenAI support); company (Xcaciv); product (ChatDbg); copyright (Copyright © Xcaciv 2024); about-box version string (free literal ChatDbg v1.0, not derived from the above) | **lifecycle**: Hand-edited in three independent places that no build step reconciles; stripped entirely from native-profile artefacts; present in bundled-profile artefacts.
- **entity**: Distribution artefact | **fields**: built base name (fixed Xcaciv.ChatDbg.Shell); platform key (windows|linux); platform triple (win-x64|linux-x64); executable suffix (.exe|empty); release file name (chatdbg-<platform key>-<platform triple><suffix>); byte size (measured, echoed, stored in an unused step output); retention (7 days) | **lifecycle**: Created by the publish step, renamed in place, uploaded as an intermediate, expires after 7 days as an intermediate, copied into the release where it persists indefinitely.
- **entity**: Release | **fields**: release version / tag (operator input, required, pre-filled v1.0.0; else 'v' + scraped project version); display title (ChatDbg <release version>); body (fixed template, only the version interpolated, written to release_notes.md); pre-release flag (true only when the input string is exactly 'true'); draft flag (always false); attached assets (exactly 2 hard-coded file paths); authentication (repository secret GH_PATT) | **lifecycle**: Created once per manual dispatch; never updated or deleted by this feature; re-dispatching with an existing tag fails. No draft state, no staging state, no rollback path.
- **entity**: Toolchain pin | **fields**: exact toolchain version (10.0.100-rc.1.25451.107, a pre-release); roll-forward policy (newest patch of newest feature band within the same major/minor); document validity (malformed — one closing brace too many; 96 bytes) | **lifecycle**: Declared once at the repository root; read by every build, local and automated; never generated.
- **entity**: Dependency pin set | **fields**: One flat list per project of package identifier plus exact version; no central version file; no committed lock file; three pins duplicated between the shared library and the console executable | **lifecycle**: Hand-edited per project; resolved at restore time; never generated or locked.
- **entity**: Intermediate build artefact | **fields**: artefact name (chatdbg-<platform key>-binary); exactly one member file; retention window (7 days); download root (./artifacts/) | **lifecycle**: Uploaded by a platform build row, downloaded by the publication job, expires 7 days after the run.
- **entity**: Committed pre-built artefact (accidental) | **fields**: path test-publish/Xcaciv.ChatDbg.Shell; size 15,677,171 bytes; 64-bit Linux, stripped; embedded manifest naming 65 bundled assemblies and a runtime version the source no longer targets; no runtime-options block present | **lifecycle**: Committed 2025-09-29 and never removed; produced and consumed by nothing; not covered by any ignore rule, so every clone downloads it.
- **entity**: Window view-state | **fields**: panel-visible flag (boolean); selected probability message (reference or none); transcript-frame width mode (full versus 60 percent) | **lifecycle**: Created when the window is built; the panel flag always starts false because the conversation is empty at startup; the selection is set by response arrival, a probability-marker click, or a panel toggle; discarded at process exit. Never persisted.
- **entity**: Role palette map | **fields**: role name (text) mapped to a colour pair (foreground, background) | **lifecycle**: Fixed at construction with entries for user, assistant and system, plus a fallback that reuses the system pair for any unrecognised role. Never mutated.
- **entity**: Heat-map palette array | **fields**: ordered list of exactly 10 colour pairs, all on a black background | **lifecycle**: Fixed at construction; indexed by floor(probability x 10) clamped to 0-9. Never mutated.
- **entity**: Global theme palettes | **fields**: Base, Dialog, Menu and Error, each with normal, focus, hot-normal and hot-focus colour pairs, plus disabled for Menu only | **lifecycle**: Assigned once at startup from a single hard-coded dark theme; never changed. Base, Dialog and Error leave their disabled entry at the framework default because each palette is created empty.
- **entity**: Transient status text | **fields**: message text; implicit 3000 ms expiry | **lifecycle**: Replaces the resting context string on each status event; restored unconditionally by an independent timer, even if a newer message was posted since.
- **entity**: Command registry | **fields**: map of lowercase command name to a command exposing name, description, usage and an execute operation | **lifecycle**: Built once at startup with exactly 15 entries in a fixed construction order; never mutated.
- **entity**: Provider registry | **fields**: map of lowercase provider key (azure, bedrock, llama) to a provider adapter | **lifecycle**: Built once at startup; never mutated.
- **entity**: Token record | **fields**: Index (integer, 0-based, contiguous); TokenId (integer vocabulary index; real for tokenization, synthetic 1000+i for generation); Text (string, non-null, defaults empty; in practice the placeholder <token_{id}> for tokenization and the word itself for generation); CharStartPosition (integer, estimated inclusive start, -1 for the beginning-of-sequence token); CharEndPosition (integer, estimated exclusive end, -1 for BOS); LogProb (floating point, 0 unless set) | **lifecycle**: Created inside the two inspection operations, never mutated after the enclosing result is returned, discarded after display. Not persisted, no serialization names.
- **entity**: Tokenization result | **fields**: OriginalPrompt (string, the post-normalisation text); Tokens (ordered list of token records); TokenCount (integer, includes the beginning-of-sequence token); Visualization (string, nullable - null when not requested, otherwise the fixed five-line block) | **lifecycle**: Created per invocation of the analyze-prompt operation; printed; discarded. Not persisted.
- **entity**: Alternative record | **fields**: TokenId (integer, synthetic 2000 + i*100 + j); TokenText (string, '{word}_alt{rank}' with rank 1-based); LogProb (floating point, -3.0 - j, strictly decreasing with rank) | **lifecycle**: Created per generation step per rank; printed; discarded.
- **entity**: Step-probability record | **fields**: TokenPosition (integer, 0-based, equals list index); SelectedTokenId (integer); SelectedTokenText (string); SelectedLogProb (floating point, always -2.5 in practice); Alternatives (list of alternative records, exactly top-K entries, descending probability) | **lifecycle**: Created per generated word; printed; discarded.
- **entity**: Attribution record | **fields**: TokenIndex (integer, 0-based, equals list index); TokenId (integer, copy of the generated token's ID); TokenText (string, copy of the generated token's text); InfluencingText (string, prompt tail of at most 50 characters or the concatenation of the previous at most 5 generated token texts; may be empty); InfluenceScore (single-precision floating point, always 0.8, unconstrained by any range) | **lifecycle**: Created one per generated token in ascending index order; printed; discarded.
- **entity**: Probability-mapping result | **fields**: OriginalPrompt (string); GeneratedTokens (list of token records, one per split word); TokenProbabilityMaps (list of step-probability records, same length and order); AttributionMap (list of attribution records, same length and order) | **lifecycle**: Created per consented inspection invocation; printed; discarded.
- **entity**: Per-step analysis record (the only persisted entity) | **fields**: step (integer, 0-based); tokenText (string); tokenId (integer, always -1 in practice); probability (number, documented 0.0-1.0, in practice a temperature-derived estimate); logprob (number, natural log of probability); promptOffset (integer, equals step in practice); topCandidates (array of candidate records, defaults empty); systemDebugInfo (string, nullable); modelState (optional model-state snapshot) | **lifecycle**: Created one per emitted token during chat generation; the whole list is cleared at the start of every generation so only the most recent generation survives; retrievable as a defensive copy and writable as an indented JSON array; there is no user-reachable clear or export path.
- **entity**: Candidate record | **fields**: text (string, decoded alternative text or the literal 'id:{tokenId}' when decoding fails); tokenId (integer, always 0 in practice); probability (number, always 0 in practice); logprob (number); logit (single-precision, raw pre-softmax score, always 0 in practice) | **lifecycle**: Created per generation step from the current logits (attached one step late), nested inside a per-step analysis record, persisted with it.
- **entity**: Model-state snapshot | **fields**: totalTokensProcessed (integer); contextTokenCount (integer); contextSize (integer, configured context window with a fallback of 4096 when at or below 0); remainingContext (integer, contextSize minus step); timestamp (date-time, captured from the local clock); debugInfo (map of string to string, defaults empty; producer fills Temperature, TopK and Mode='SamplingPipeline') | **lifecycle**: Created per generated token, attached optionally to a per-step analysis record, persisted with it.
- **entity**: Diagnostic log sink state (consumed here, owned by the diagnostic-logging feature) | **fields**: LogDirectory (path, defaults to the platform application-data folder + ChatDbg/Logs); EnableFileLogging (boolean, default true); EnableDebugOutput (boolean, default true); EnableConsoleOutput (boolean, default false); MaxBufferSize (integer characters, default 10000); an in-memory lock-guarded text buffer | **lifecycle**: Constructed with defaults inside the inference service; buffer flushes to a daily file past the threshold (which clears it); explicit save overwrites a target path without clearing; disposal flushes once and is idempotent; the native callback is never unregistered.
- **entity**: Per-token analysis record | **fields**: step (integer, 0-based generation index); tokenText (string, the emitted text piece); tokenId (integer, always the sentinel -1 on this path); probability (floating point, the temperature-derived estimate in {0.95, 0.85, 0.75, 0.60, 0.50, 0.40}); logprob (floating point, natural log of that estimate); promptOffset (integer, equal to step — no attribution computed); topCandidates (list of candidate records, empty in practice); systemDebugInfo (nullable string, the fixed sentence 'Generated via sampling pipeline at step <n>, temperature=<t to 2dp>.'); modelState (nullable model-state snapshot) | **lifecycle**: Created once per streamed text piece on the introspection path only; accumulated in an in-memory list on the back end; the list is cleared at the start of every introspected generation and never cleared otherwise; never persisted automatically; exportable as indented JSON through an operation that no user-reachable path invokes; nothing bounds its growth and no clearing operation is exposed.
- **entity**: Candidate record | **fields**: text (string, candidate token text or the 'id:<n>' placeholder); tokenId (integer, never populated on this path — 0); probability (floating point, never populated on this path — 0); logprob (floating point, natural log of the within-selection renormalised probability); logit (single-precision float, never populated on this path — 0) | **lifecycle**: Value object created inside the candidate routine per selected vocabulary index; projected into per-token analysis records as text and logprob only; lives and dies with its parent record; candidates nested inside a display probability record always carry an absent alternatives list, so the structure never recurses past two levels.
- **entity**: Model-state snapshot | **fields**: totalTokensProcessed (integer, generated-piece count only); contextTokenCount (integer, same value — prompt tokens not counted); contextSize (integer, configured value else fallback 4096); remainingContext (integer, contextSize minus generated-piece count); timestamp (date-time, local wall clock, no zone offset); debugInfo (string→string map with exactly three keys: Temperature at two decimals, TopK, and Mode = 'SamplingPipeline') | **lifecycle**: One per per-token analysis record; created synthetically at each generation step; never reconciled against the engine's real context usage; gates nothing.
- **entity**: Display probability record | **fields**: token (string, default empty); logprob (floating point, default 0); top_alternatives (nullable list of the same shape, snake_case key unlike every other record here); a derived probability equal to the exponential of logprob, computed on read and excluded from serialisation | **lifecycle**: Produced by the back end's introspection path, one per streamed piece in emission order, returned to the shell for the current turn only; not persisted by this feature. (The record type itself is owned by Token Probability Analysis; this feature is one of its producers.)
- **entity**: Engine log buffer and daily log file | **fields**: in-memory text buffer of lines formatted '[yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm:ss.fff] [LEVEL] message' (engine-origin lines right-trimmed, application-origin lines not); an append-only file named 'llamasharp_<yyyyMMdd>.log' in the log directory; component settings for file logging (default on), platform debug output (default on), console output (default off) and a 10 000-character flush threshold | **lifecycle**: Buffer created when capture is armed on the first generation of an instance's life; appended on every application log call and every engine callback; cleared after each successful flush; flushed on the 10 000-character threshold (engine-origin lines only), at the end of every introspected generation, on any absorbed failure, on load failure, and on shutdown; written (without clearing) to a caller-chosen path on request. The daily file is appended to with no size rotation, retention limit or pruning.
- **entity**: Cached native resource set (weights, inference context, executor, engine-side chat session) | **fields**: model weights handle; sized inference context; interactive executor over the context; engine-side chat session seeded with one system-role message; the remembered model path | **lifecycle**: Created lazily on the first generation, on a worker thread, under the process-wide model-load lock; keyed on the model path; reused for every later generation on the same path; torn down strictly in the order session → executor → context → weights when the path changes or the back end shuts down; leaked entirely if the object is reclaimed without an explicit shutdown; not synchronised against an in-flight generation.

---

## Open questions

- **question**: Should the clone ship the fabricated confidence-data fallback at all, and if so must it be marked as invented? | **whyItMatters**: The backend silently returns invented 90-percent-confidence tokens whenever the service returns none, and no consumer can tell them from measured data. Shipping invented confidence numbers to developers analysing model uncertainty is a product-level decision, not an implementation detail. A separate explicit demonstration command already exists, which argues the fallback is vestigial scaffolding. | **whereWeLooked**: README.md, IMPLEMENTATION_SUMMARY.md, all of docs/ (grepped for Azure), and the in-code comment at AzureOpenAIService.cs:201-202 which says only 'generate some simulated ones so the user can see the feature works'.
- **question**: Should the confidence-source precedence be reordered so that per-choice data is reachable even when a malformed top-level block exists (QUIRK-6.1)? | **whyItMatters**: The hosted service's documented format puts confidence data under choices[0].logprobs — the location checked last and reachable only when no top-level block exists at all. As written, a top-level block of JSON null destroys all real data and substitutes fabricated data. This is likely the single highest-impact behavioural defect in the feature. | **whereWeLooked**: AzureOpenAIService.cs:245-270; no captured fixture, test, or document in the repository shows a real response shape.
- **question**: Was the API version 2023-12-01-preview chosen deliberately or is it stale, and should it become user-configurable? | **whyItMatters**: It is hard-coded, is a preview version, and the in-code comment misdescribes it as the latest stable version — while the troubleshooting text tells the user to ensure a recent version as though it were controllable (QUIRK-6.6). A clone pinning a dead preview version will fail against the live service. | **whereWeLooked**: AzureOpenAIService.cs:119-120, LogProbsCommand.cs:185; no changelog or architecture decision record exists in the repository.
- **question**: What timeout and retry policy should the clone adopt? | **whyItMatters**: Nothing is configured anywhere — no timeout, no retry, no back-off, no rate-limit or retry-after handling. The user simply waits on 'Thinking...' until the transport's platform default expires, with no way to abort because no cancellation signal exists in the contract. | **whereWeLooked**: All of AzureOpenAIService.cs, both shells, and all of docs/; nothing sets or documents a timeout or retry.
- **question**: Should the maximum-response-tokens setting be sent on the library-mediated path (QUIRK-6.3)? | **whyItMatters**: The product documentation describes it as 'Maximum response length' for all providers, but it is only sent when token log probabilities are enabled. Switching that flag on silently starts enforcing a cap that was not enforced before, changing reply lengths for reasons the user cannot connect to the setting they changed. | **whereWeLooked**: AzureOpenAIService.cs:98-101 vs :155; README.md:50,100; no comment, test, or document explains the omission.
- **question**: Should unknown message roles be dropped or forwarded (QUIRK-6.5)? | **whyItMatters**: The two request paths disagree, so the same conversation produces two different upstream payloads depending only on the token-log-probability flag. A clone must pick one behaviour, and the choice affects any future use of tool or function roles. | **whereWeLooked**: AzureOpenAIService.cs:84-95 vs :143-147; nothing explains which is correct.
- **question**: Should provider lookup be case-sensitive or case-insensitive, and should the full-screen interface pre-check configuration (QUIRK-6.17, QUIRK-6.18)? | **whyItMatters**: The two shells behave differently on the same settings file: one refuses a capitalised provider name that the other accepts, and one shows friendly remediation guidance while the other exposes internal settings identifiers in a modal. A clone with one shell must choose; a clone with two must decide whether parity is required. | **whereWeLooked**: ChatDbg/ChatShell.cs:251,349,356-361 vs ChatDbg.Shell.Gui/UI/ChatWindow.cs:425-437,494-497.
- **question**: Should extraction preserve partial results instead of discarding everything on the first malformed entry (QUIRK-6.10)? | **whyItMatters**: One bad token entry silently loses every good token already extracted and triggers fabrication in their place, so a mostly-valid response is replaced wholesale by invented data. INFERRED from control flow; no test exercises it. | **whereWeLooked**: AzureOpenAIService.cs:298,330-334,403-407,273-276; no test covers a malformed confidence block.
- **question**: Should the map-shaped alternative handling in tolerant extraction be kept at all? | **whyItMatters**: It appears written against a hypothetical format that no fixture, test, or document in the repository uses, and it is the direct source of the 100-percent-confidence unchosen alternative (QUIRK-6.11). Dropping it removes the defect and no known behaviour. | **whereWeLooked**: AzureOpenAIService.cs:362-393; grepped the repository for any fixture using top_alternatives.
- **question**: Should the full upstream error body be echoed into the user's terminal (QUIRK-6.7)? | **whyItMatters**: An upstream error page can push arbitrary remote content — quotas, request identifiers, echoed prompt fragments — into the terminal unfiltered, and the status is shown as a symbolic name rather than a numeric code. This is both a usability and a content-injection concern. | **whereWeLooked**: AzureOpenAIService.cs:184-187; no test captures an error message, so the symbolic rendering is INFERRED.
- **question**: Should range validation move from the interactive command handlers to the settings load path (QUIRK-6.16)? | **whyItMatters**: A hand-edited or migrated settings file bypasses the 1-20 top-K and 1-8192 maximum-tokens rules entirely and puts arbitrary values on the wire; a top-K below 1 also silently produces zero fabricated alternatives. | **whereWeLooked**: SettingsService.cs:43-58 vs SetCommand.cs:79-85,173-181.
- **question**: Is the unused second full-screen shell registry abandoned or in progress (QUIRK-6.19)? | **whyItMatters**: It duplicates the live wiring minus the local-model backend and is unreachable. A clone must decide whether the three-backend registry is canonical or whether a two-backend variant is intended for some configuration. | **whereWeLooked**: ChatDbg.Shell.Gui/ChatShell.cs:33-37 vs ChatDbg.Shell.Gui/Program.cs:22-27; searched the whole tree for instantiations — only the console shell is ever constructed. No comment, commit message, or document explains it.
- **question**: Is the split between a plain endpoint field in the settings dialog and a separate masked credential dialog a deliberate 'secrets never in a plain settings tab' policy? | **whyItMatters**: If it is policy, a clone must preserve it; if it is accident, a clone may consolidate. It also determines whether the deprecated plaintext key in the settings file should be removed outright. | **whereWeLooked**: ChatDbg.Shell.Gui/UI/SettingsDialog.cs:92-98 and :509-545; the split is implied but stated nowhere.
- **question**: Should the four (five including the shells) divergent 'text was null' fallback strings be unified (QUIRK-6.20)? | **whyItMatters**: The same condition produces five different user-visible outcomes across the product, one of them containing a spelling error and one of them an empty string. A clone building from this specification will otherwise reproduce the inconsistency deliberately. | **whereWeLooked**: AzureOpenAIService.cs:50, BedrockService.cs:33, LLamaSharpService.cs:58, ChatDbg/ChatShell.cs:377, ChatDbg.Shell.Gui/UI/ChatWindow.cs:444.
- **question**: Should the three implemented-but-unregistered commands (export-logs, export-analysis, show-analysis) be registered, or dropped? | **whyItMatters**: They implement the contract and have unit tests but are unreachable from every surface, and one of them is the only command using flag-style arguments (--top N, --state, --range START END, default top 3) - a style the space-only parser offers no support for. A clone must either register them (and then decide whether to add flag parsing) or drop them, not ship them unreachable. | **whereWeLooked**: Core/Commands/ExportLogsCommand.cs, ExportTokenAnalysisCommand.cs, ShowTokenAnalysisCommand.cs vs. both hosts' registry initialisers; IMPLEMENTATION_SUMMARY.md:49-54 and :221-222 state they are unfinished integration work, not a rejected design.
- **question**: Which of the two help renderers is canonical - the curated grouped document or the alphabetical listing dialog? | **whyItMatters**: They differ in content (usage strings present vs. absent), grouping and ordering, and they can disagree because one is hand-scripted and the other is registry-derived. A clone should drive both surfaces from one renderer, but which behaviour is the intended product experience is unstated. | **whereWeLooked**: HelpCommand.cs:37-98 vs. ChatWindow.cs:1087-1126; no code comment, README section or design document states an intent.
- **question**: Was the console shell's non-trimming of input deliberate, making a line with a leading space a chat turn there but a command in the full-screen shell? | **whyItMatters**: The same keystrokes produce a paid model call in one host and a local control action in the other. A clone must pick one rule; trimming in both is the safer default but changes shipped behaviour. | **whereWeLooked**: ChatShell.cs:83-92 vs. ChatWindow.cs:334; no test, no comment, no documentation.
- **question**: Was 'Invalid command' versus total silence for a lone slash deliberate? | **whyItMatters**: Same divergence between hosts, same class of user confusion; in the full-screen shell the input box is already cleared so the typed text vanishes without a trace. | **whereWeLooked**: ChatShell.cs:327-330 vs. ChatWindow.cs:385-388; no evidence either way.
- **question**: Is the full-screen shell's abandoned configuration object (QUIRK-1.13) known to the authors, and should the clone fix it? | **whyItMatters**: It is the single defect most likely to be mistaken for intended behaviour: /set reports built-in defaults instead of the user's saved configuration, /model persists a change the window never uses, and /set provider does not switch the provider chat turns actually use. The documentation describes these commands as working. | **whereWeLooked**: Program.cs:14, :37-46, :58, :81; ChatWindow.cs:55; contrast ChatShell.cs:127-140 in the console host. No comment, issue reference or test mentions it.
- **question**: Should the dialog-driven direct-invocation paths be folded back into the standard dispatch pipeline? | **whyItMatters**: As shipped they skip the exit-flag check, skip the empty-message-means-silence rule (substituting canned text), use a different failure-modal title, guard neither faults nor a missing registry entry - four behavioural divergences on two paths. Keeping them means specifying all four divergences; folding them in changes user-visible feedback. | **whereWeLooked**: ChatWindow.cs:960-995 and :1061-1079 vs. the typed path at :344-420.
- **question**: Should an entire second, dead dispatcher be reproduced or dropped, and which of its two divergent registries is authoritative? | **whyItMatters**: The dead copy registers 13 commands (omitting tokenize and inspect) and 2 providers, while the live path registers 15 and 3. A reimplementer reading the wrong file would build the wrong command surface. | **whereWeLooked**: src/ChatDbg.Shell.Gui/ChatShell.cs:43-68, :81-121, :276-294, never instantiated; src/ChatDbg.Shell.Gui/Program.cs:30-90 builds the registry itself.
- **question**: Should commands be allowed to write directly to the terminal at all? | **whyItMatters**: Direct console writes from several commands corrupt the full-screen shell's layout, and the system-prompt editor reads console lines until a line equal to END, which cannot work under a full-screen host on any platform. Deciding this determines whether the result message is the only output channel or merely the primary one. | **whereWeLooked**: InspectCommand.cs (37 direct writes), TokenizeCommand.cs (10), PromptCommand.cs:258-267; Program.cs:41 injects the rich renderer in exactly the host that cannot tolerate direct writes. (The visual corruption was reasoned from source, not observed at run time.)
- **question**: Should there be any dispatch-level automated tests, and what is the intended behaviour they would pin down? | **whyItMatters**: Parsing, lookup, unknown-command handling, exit handling and result rendering all live in the two host projects, which have no test project, so none of the dispatch rules in this section has an executable specification in the source. Several rules here were derived by reading three dispatchers rather than from tests. | **whereWeLooked**: Xcaciv.ChatDbg.sln (four projects) and Xcaciv.ChatDbg.Core.Tests.csproj, which references only the core library.
- **question**: Should the mis-encoded usage document be repaired, and does the fault reproduce on every toolchain? | **whyItMatters**: Detailed help for the configuration command renders 30 lines beginning with a replacement character. The byte-level fault is certain; what a given toolchain substitutes was not verified by building, and a toolchain configured with a matching legacy code page would render the intended bullet. | **whereWeLooked**: Core/Commands/SetCommand.cs verified by byte scan: no byte-order mark, 30 lone 0x95 bytes, the only non-ASCII bytes in the file; the document is assembled at :297 and reached through :24.
- **question**: Was the marketplace token-log-probability path ever exercised against the real service, and is it required at all in the clone? | **whyItMatters**: Both probability request members are outside the marketplace's published contract, so enabling the feature either draws a validation rejection or is ignored, and the entire response-side parser (roughly 200 lines of behaviour specified in FR-7.43 through FR-7.56) is dead code against genuine replies. If it is not required, a large slice of the specification can be dropped; if it is required, a real contract must be sourced first. | **whereWeLooked**: All tests, README.md, every file under docs/, IMPLEMENTATION_SUMMARY.md, .github/*.prompt.md, and the git log for BedrockService.cs (5 commits, all generic refactor messages). No test, doc, changelog entry or captured real reply exists. Recommendation from the dossier: specify it as explicitly out of scope unless the product owner confirms otherwise.
- **question**: Which non-Claude model families, if any, must the clone support? | **whyItMatters**: The generic request shape is an invented placeholder that matches no real family's native contract. If Titan/Llama/Mistral/Cohere/AI21 support is wanted, per-family request and response schemas must be specified from scratch; if not, the clone should reject non-Claude identifiers outright rather than send a body that will be refused. | **whereWeLooked**: BedrockService.cs:82-103 and the author's hedging comment there; nothing in the repository describes any other family's contract.
- **question**: Should the alternatives' double exponentiation be preserved for bug-compatibility or fixed? | **whyItMatters**: As written, every alternative's displayed confidence is at or above 100 % and can reach about 271 %. Fixing it changes visible output; preserving it ships an obviously wrong percentage. No test pins the derived percentage either way. | **whereWeLooked**: BedrockService.cs:162, :178, :245, :268 versus TokenLogProbabilities.cs:26; the source test asserts only that the probability list is non-null.
- **question**: Should an unrecognised reply remain a successful turn whose answer text is 'Unable to parse model response', or become an error? | **whyItMatters**: Today the literal is appended to the transcript as the assistant's words and the real body survives only in the diagnostic trace — silent data loss that a user cannot distinguish from a real model answer. | **whereWeLooked**: BedrockService.cs:202-204, :213. No test, doc or issue addresses it.
- **question**: Is temporary-credential (session-token / single-sign-on / assumed-role) support required? | **whyItMatters**: The source cannot support it: the settings layer hoists the standard environment variables into a static key pair and the construction seam re-injects them without the session token, so a user relying on assumed roles will fail authentication with a confusing message. If the clone's users assume single sign-on, this is a new requirement, not a port. | **whereWeLooked**: Exhaustive grep for AWS_SESSION_TOKEN across the repository returns no hits; ChatSettings.cs:73, :76 and DefaultBedrockRuntimeClientFactory.cs:11-18.
- **question**: Is a context-window or history-truncation policy required? | **whyItMatters**: The entire conversation is resent every turn with no windowing, so cost and latency grow quadratically and a long enough session will exceed the model's context window with no local warning. | **whereWeLooked**: BedrockService.cs:48-58; no truncation, windowing or token-budget code exists anywhere in the feature.
- **question**: Is a 'system'-role message injected into the history meant to reach the marketplace as a system directive? | **whyItMatters**: Today it is silently downgraded to a user turn, unlike the sibling cloud back end which preserves all three roles distinctly. The clone must pick one behaviour, and the two back ends currently disagree. | **whereWeLooked**: BedrockService.cs:55 versus AzureOpenAIService.cs:84-95; the message-injection command and both hosts — no evidence of intent either way.
- **question**: What is the intended behaviour when a user configures an access key without a secret key? | **whyItMatters**: Readiness reports 'configured' and the turn then fails with a raw credential error from the underlying library. Readiness and the client-construction precondition disagree, which produces a confusing first-run experience. | **whereWeLooked**: BedrockService.cs:25-27 versus DefaultBedrockRuntimeClientFactory.cs:11. No test, no documentation.
- **question**: Should a malformed probability entry be allowed to destroy an otherwise-good answer? | **whyItMatters**: It does today in the structured-content branch and does not in the completion branch — identical malformed data, opposite outcomes. The clone must pick one behaviour for both branches, and picking 'fatal' means users lose real answers over cosmetic data. | **whereWeLooked**: BedrockService.cs:151-182 (unguarded) versus :227 and :319-323 (guarded).
- **question**: Should the windowed host gain the readiness check the console host has? | **whyItMatters**: Without it, an unconfigured user sees the internal, developer-worded guard message in a modal instead of the console host's actionable remediation text — two different experiences for the same misconfiguration. | **whereWeLooked**: ChatWindow.cs:420-441 (no readiness call) versus ChatShell.cs:356-359. No test, doc or issue references it.
- **question**: Is the windowed project's second, never-reached host-wiring file meant to be deleted or meant to be the real entry point? | **whyItMatters**: It disagrees with the live wiring on which back ends exist (it registers only two of three) and carries its own readiness checks and teardown. A reimplementer copying the wrong file would ship a two-provider product with different start-up behaviour. | **whereWeLooked**: src/ChatDbg.Shell.Gui/ChatShell.cs:33-37, :223, :309, :651-666 versus the live path at src/ChatDbg.Shell.Gui/Program.cs:22-26, :79-87; grep finds no construction of that type anywhere (a reflection-based construction would not show up in a grep).
- **question**: Should the user documentation name the cloud credential environment variables? | **whyItMatters**: As written, a user who follows only the README cannot reach a configured state: the README names no variable and the settings command refuses to accept keys. This is the single most likely first-run failure for the clone as well. | **whereWeLooked**: README.md:123-129; grep for CHATDBG_AWS over README.md returns nothing; the names appear only in docs/SECURITY-IMPLEMENTATION.md:32-33, two stale specification copies, and the runtime remediation text.
- **question**: Should the top-K setting truncate the response as well as shape the request? | **whyItMatters**: It is accepted and ignored on the response side today, so a user asking for 5 alternatives may be shown 10. - **question**: Should the diagnostic trace be redacted or truncated? | **whyItMatters**: It records every user prompt and every model answer verbatim.
