- No internationalization: every user-visible string is a hard-coded English literal, while numeric formatting uses the ambient culture and timestamps use the local clock - so numbers and English labels can disagree on locale.
- Windows-first packaging default (product-wide): both shell projects default their target runtime to 64-bit Windows in their size-optimised publish configurations whenever one is not supplied on the command line.
- Provider abstraction: every model back end implements one shared contract — a display name, a boolean readiness check taking the settings record, a text-only generation entry point, and a generation entry point that also returns per-token probability records — plus a disposal contract. Shells hold exactly one instance of each and may swap between them without knowing which they hold. The three accepted provider keys product-wide are 'azure', 'bedrock' and 'llama', validated with the message "Provider must be 'azure', 'bedrock', or 'llama'".
- Settings persistence: all configuration lives in one camelCase-keyed, indented JSON document at <user profile>/.ChatDbg/settings.json, falling back to the system temporary directory when the user-profile folder is unavailable or blank. Every accepted change is written immediately, after each individual successful assignment.
- Settings validation ranges shared across back ends: temperature 0.0–2.0 inclusive (default 0.7); maximum output tokens 1–8192 (default 1000); introspection alternatives count 1–20 (default 5); introspection enabled defaults to false. The command surface rejects out-of-range values with a fixed message while the windowed dialog silently clamps instead — a product-wide inconsistency between the two user interfaces.
- Error convention: a back-end failure detected before any work is raised out of the back end and caught at turn level by the shell; every failure raised after that point is absorbed and returned as a success-shaped response whose text carries the error, whose elapsed time is 0, and whose dedicated error field is populated but read by no shell. Shells therefore append error strings to the transcript as assistant turns. This convention is shared by all three back ends.
- Turn-level error presentation: the console shell prints 'Error getting AI response: <message>'; the windowed shell shows a dialog reading 'Failed to get AI response: <message>'. Both shells substitute the misspelled 'Error: Response text expected, none recieved.' when a reply's text is absent on the introspection path, while the back ends use the correctly spelled 'Error: Response text expected, none received' — one product-wide message with two spellings.
- Pre-turn readiness message: when the selected back end reports itself not configured, the shell prints 'Error: <provider display name> service is not configured. Use environment variables or Windows Credential Manager to configure credentials securely.' followed by a two-space-indented "Type '/set' to see current configuration and setup instructions." — the credential wording is emitted for every back end, including ones that read no credential.
- Async and cancellation convention: every service and command entry point is asynchronous, and there are no cancellation tokens anywhere in the product. No timeout, retry, back-off, queue bound or progress reporting exists on any back end.
- Serialisation convention: persisted and exported records carry explicit per-property names and exclude computed or secret members. Names are camelCase everywhere except the display probability record's 'top_alternatives'. Exported documents are indented JSON.
- Localisation and formatting convention: all messages, log lines and help text are hard-coded English; timestamps use local time with a fixed 'yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm:ss.fff' pattern and no zone offset; numbers are formatted under the ambient locale with fixed decimal patterns, so a comma decimal separator can appear inside exported documents and logs.
- File path convention: settings under the user-profile folder; system prompts under local application data; diagnostic logs under the roaming application-data folder plus 'ChatDbg' plus a subfolder, created on demand.
- Diagnostic logging failures are never surfaced to the user: an unwritable directory, a full disk or a failed hook registration reaches only the platform debug channel, and chat continues unaffected.
- Command surface convention: input beginning with '/' is a command, everything else is a chat turn; each command reports success with an optional message; a command's failure never ends the session. A command class can exist, be documented in help text and guides, and still be registered in neither shell — registration is manual and per shell, so 'implemented' does not imply 'reachable'.

---

## Quirks

- QUIRK-6.1: Per-choice confidence data is unreachable whenever a top-level confidence block exists in an unexpected shape (e.g. JSON null), so all real data is lost and fabricated data substituted. Evidence: src/Xcaciv.ChatDbg.Core/Services/AzureOpenAIService.cs:245-270. INFERRED from branch structure; untested.
- QUIRK-6.2: The direct request path clears and rewrites the shared transport's default headers on every call, so concurrent sends can race on headers and credentials and a caller-supplied transport has its headers wiped. Evidence: AzureOpenAIService.cs:126-128. INFERRED hazard; latent in the single-turn shells.
- QUIRK-6.3: The maximum-response-tokens setting is never sent on the default path, so enabling token log probabilities silently starts enforcing a length cap. Evidence: AzureOpenAIService.cs:98-101 vs :155; README.md:50,100.
- QUIRK-6.4: The two send paths normalise the endpoint differently — the direct path trims trailing separators, the library-mediated path does not. Evidence: AzureOpenAIService.cs:70 vs :121; README.md:120.
- QUIRK-6.5: The two send paths filter message roles differently — the library-mediated path drops unknown roles, the direct path forwards every role lower-cased. Evidence: AzureOpenAIService.cs:84-95 vs :143-147.
- QUIRK-6.6: Troubleshooting text tells the user to ensure a recent API version although the version is a hard-coded preview constant that the in-code comment misdescribes as stable. Evidence: LogProbsCommand.cs:185 vs AzureOpenAIService.cs:119-120.
- QUIRK-6.7: The entire upstream error body is echoed unfiltered into the terminal on a non-success status, with the status rendered as a symbolic name rather than a number. Evidence: AzureOpenAIService.cs:184-187. Symbolic rendering INFERRED.
- QUIRK-6.8: Parse failures are double-wrapped, producing nested prefixes in the user-visible message. Evidence: AzureOpenAIService.cs:284 then :216.
- QUIRK-6.9: Fabricated confidence data is returned unmarked, so invented numbers are indistinguishable from measured ones and the 'none were returned' notice becomes nearly unreachable. Evidence: AzureOpenAIService.cs:197-208; src/ChatDbg/ChatShell.cs:387-391.
- QUIRK-6.10: One malformed token entry discards every confidence record already extracted, and fabrication then replaces the lot. Evidence: AzureOpenAIService.cs:298,330-334,403-407,273-276. INFERRED; untested.
- QUIRK-6.11: A map-shaped alternative with no numeric property is emitted with log-probability 0, displaying as 100 percent confidence for an unchosen alternative, and its token is the last property name scanned. Evidence: AzureOpenAIService.cs:362-393. INFERRED; untested.
- QUIRK-6.12: An alternatives field that is present but not an array aborts extraction entirely and the whole response is reported as carrying no confidence data. Evidence: AzureOpenAIService.cs:308-310,330-334. INFERRED; untested.
- QUIRK-6.13: A message-less choice is a hard parse failure while a null-content choice yields a silent empty assistant turn that also suppresses fabrication. Evidence: AzureOpenAIService.cs:237-238,203; src/ChatDbg/ChatShell.cs:387-391.
- QUIRK-6.14: Alternatives never receive an alternatives list of their own while chosen tokens always do, producing inconsistent shapes in persisted history. Evidence: AzureOpenAIService.cs:300-305 vs :315-319.
- QUIRK-6.15: The response object's elapsed-time and error-message slots are never populated by this backend — always 0 and null. Evidence: AIResponse.cs:22-32; AzureOpenAIService.cs:104,207,211.
- QUIRK-6.16: Settings loaded from disk bypass every range check; validation lives only in the interactive command handlers. Evidence: SettingsService.cs:43-58; SetCommand.cs:79-85,173-181; AzureOpenAIService.cs:155,158,459.
- QUIRK-6.17: The full-screen shell never pre-checks configuration, so the backend's internal message naming internal settings identifiers reaches the user in a modal. Evidence: ChatDbg.Shell.Gui/UI/ChatWindow.cs:426-437,494-497 vs src/ChatDbg/ChatShell.cs:356-361.
- QUIRK-6.18: Provider lookup is case-sensitive in the console shell and case-insensitive in the full-screen shell, so one settings file yields two behaviours. Evidence: src/ChatDbg/ChatShell.cs:251,349 vs ChatDbg.Shell.Gui/UI/ChatWindow.cs:425-433.
- QUIRK-6.19: A second full-screen shell class registers only two of the three backends and is never instantiated — dead code that would break local-model selection if wired up. Evidence: ChatDbg.Shell.Gui/ChatShell.cs:33-37 vs ChatDbg.Shell.Gui/Program.cs:22-27.
- QUIRK-6.20: Four different 'text was null' fallback strings exist for the same condition, one misspelled, plus a fifth outcome where the full-screen shell substitutes an empty string. Evidence: AzureOpenAIService.cs:50; BedrockService.cs:33; LLamaSharpService.cs:58; src/ChatDbg/ChatShell.cs:377; ChatDbg.Shell.Gui/UI/ChatWindow.cs:444.
- QUIRK-6.21: A pseudo-random generator is constructed once per fabricated token and never used; fabricated output is fully deterministic. Evidence: AzureOpenAIService.cs:451.
- QUIRK-6.22: The garbage-collection cleanup hook never releases the self-created transport, leaking it while keeping every instance alive an extra collection cycle. Evidence: AzureOpenAIService.cs:480-496.
- QUIRK-6.23: Security documentation instructs non-Windows users to enable the Windows-only credential vault; off Windows the vault yields nothing, the enable command is refused, and the failure is silent. Evidence: docs/SECURITY-IMPLEMENTATION.md:226,233,236; WindowsCredentialManager.cs:59-62,169-172; ChatSettings.cs:137-141; SetCommand.cs:219-224.
- QUIRK-6.24: The only test exercising real confidence extraction feeds a response shape the hosted service does not emit, so the branch that runs in production has no coverage at all. Evidence: AzureOpenAIServiceTests.cs:40-59 vs AzureOpenAIService.cs:260-270.
- QUIRK-1.1 Three complete command implementations (export-logs, export-analysis, show-analysis) are never registered, so they are unreachable from every surface and appear in no help; one is the only command with flag-style arguments (--top N, --state, --range START END, default top 3), which the parser cannot support.
- QUIRK-1.2 Two entirely different help renderers exist with different content and ordering: the curated grouped document from /help versus the alphabetical name-and-description list from F1 / View Commands.
- QUIRK-1.3 The command listing dialog checks that a 'help' command exists but never uses it; if help were unregistered, F1 would silently do nothing.
- QUIRK-1.4 An entire second dispatcher (registry + read-eval loop + parser) is dead code, never instantiated, and registers only 13 commands and 2 providers instead of 15 and 3.
- QUIRK-1.5 General help is a hand-maintained script that can drift from the registry in both directions - a registered command missing from the script is invisible, and a scripted name that is not registered is silently dropped.
- QUIRK-1.6 /help with several arguments ignores all but the first, so /help set provider silently becomes /help set.
- QUIRK-1.7 /help /set fails with 'Unknown command: /set' because the argument is looked up verbatim after lower-casing.
- QUIRK-1.8 The help command reports an unknown name as a failure while listing help is a success, so a typo inside /help produces a blocking modal error dialog in the full-screen shell.
- QUIRK-1.9 F10 Quit and File > Exit bypass the command system entirely, stopping the window directly, so exit-time behaviour attached to the exit/quit commands would not run.
- QUIRK-1.10 Several commands write directly to the terminal (37 and 10 direct writes) and the system-prompt editor reads console lines until a line equal to END - corrupting the full-screen shell's layout, in the very host that injects the rich renderer.
- QUIRK-1.11 A plain-text formatter implementation ships in the core library but is wired into no host; only tests construct it.
- QUIRK-1.12 The About dialog names two providers while general help names three.
- QUIRK-1.13 In the full-screen shell every configuration-bearing command is wired to a configuration object nobody else uses: /set reports built-in defaults instead of saved values, /model persists a change the window never uses, and /set provider does not switch the provider chat turns actually use.
- QUIRK-1.14 The console shell spins forever at end of input - a null read is treated exactly like a blank line, so an exhausted redirected input re-prompts indefinitely with no exit path but an exit command or killing the process.
- QUIRK-1.15 Overlapping transient status messages truncate each other: each starts an independent 3000 ms timer that unconditionally overwrites the label and no earlier timer is cancelled.
- QUIRK-1.16 The empty-message-means-silence rule is violated on exactly two paths: the inject and change-model dialogs substitute canned text and title the failure modal 'Error' instead of 'Command Error'.
- QUIRK-1.17 The failure factory accepts an empty message without validation, producing a failure that renders as total silence and is indistinguishable from a silent success.
- QUIRK-1.18 The inject dialog silently discards a non-numeric position with no warning and reports a success that does not mention it.
- QUIRK-1.19 Both dialog paths become silent no-ops if their command is missing from the registry - the lookup is guarded but there is no alternative branch.
- QUIRK-1.20 Faults raised on the two dialog-driven paths are unhandled, unlike the menu and typed paths which both catch and show a modal; nothing waits on those handlers.
- QUIRK-1.21 One command publishes its generated data on a separately readable property alongside its result - a second output channel no host reads and only tests use.
- QUIRK-1.22 General help renders its whole skeleton (7 headings, 5 configuration lines, 3 provider lines, 2 closing lines) against an empty registry with zero command entries and still reports success; the shipped test does exactly this and passes.
- QUIRK-1.23 A View-menu item is a stub that only shows 'System messages toggle not yet implemented', although the user manual lists it as a working feature.
- QUIRK-1.24 Detailed help for the configuration command renders 30 lines whose leading bullet is a replacement character, because that one source file is stored in a legacy single-byte encoding with no byte-order mark.
- QUIRK-1.25 The command listing dialog sorts with the platform default (culture-sensitive) ordering and the slash-prefix test uses a culture-sensitive comparison, while two shipped packaging configurations enable invariant-globalisation mode - so behaviour depends on the build configuration.
- QUIRK-7.1: The probability-request members are not part of any real marketplace contract; both body shapes always send logprobs and top_logprobs, so the entire response-side probability parser is dead code against genuine replies. INFERRED upstream half. Evidence: BedrockService.cs:76-77, :101-102, :67, :100, :144-187, :225-324; BedrockServiceTests.cs:28-41.
- QUIRK-7.2: The probability members are emitted even when the feature is off, as false and 0 rather than omitted. Evidence: BedrockService.cs:76-77, :101-102.
- QUIRK-7.3: Double exponentiation of alternatives — an alternative's stored value is already a probability and the display layer exponentiates again, yielding derived percentages always at or above 100 % and up to about 271 %. Evidence: BedrockService.cs:162, :178, :245, :268; TokenLogProbabilities.cs:26.
- QUIRK-7.4: Malformed probability data is fatal in the structured-content branch and harmless in the completion branch — identical input, opposite outcomes. Evidence: BedrockService.cs:151-182 vs :227, :319-323.
- QUIRK-7.5: A non-string content[0].text destroys the whole turn rather than degrading to the 'No response received' fallback an explicit null gets. Evidence: BedrockService.cs:140, :215-219.
- QUIRK-7.6: Top-K is requested but never applied — the tolerant parser accepts a top-K argument and never reads it, so nothing truncates the response. Evidence: BedrockService.cs:199, :225.
- QUIRK-7.7: The object-shaped probability path silently drops all alternatives, always assigning an empty collection with no diagnostic. Evidence: BedrockService.cs:310.
- QUIRK-7.8: A missing log-probability in the object-shaped path silently defaults to 0, deriving to exactly 100 % confidence, while the array-shaped path drops such entries. Evidence: BedrockService.cs:292, :299-301 vs :236-237.
- QUIRK-7.9: An unparseable reply is reported as success — the literal 'Unable to parse model response' becomes the assistant's words in the transcript. Silent data loss. Evidence: BedrockService.cs:202-204, :213.
- QUIRK-7.10: An empty content array falls through to the fallback branch; presence of content is not enough, only a non-empty array selects the structured branch. Evidence: BedrockService.cs:133-135.
- QUIRK-7.11: A system message injected into the history is silently downgraded to user, because role mapping is binary. The sibling cloud back end preserves three roles. Evidence: BedrockService.cs:55 vs AzureOpenAIService.cs:84-95.
- QUIRK-7.12: Cross-region inference-profile identifiers such as us.anthropic.claude-… fail the bare 'anthropic.' prefix test and take the generic body path, producing a body the model rejects. INFERRED consequence. Evidence: BedrockService.cs:61.
- QUIRK-7.13: The non-Claude branch is a stub — one invented messages shape is sent to every other model family, none of which accept it. INFERRED upstream half. Evidence: BedrockService.cs:82-103.
- QUIRK-7.14: Temperature range mismatch — the product accepts and forwards up to 2.0 while Claude models on the marketplace cap at 1.0. INFERRED upstream limit. Evidence: SetCommand.cs:72-75; BedrockService.cs:72.
- QUIRK-7.15: Readiness ignores the secret key entirely, so readiness and the client-construction precondition disagree — readiness needs one key, construction needs both. Evidence: BedrockService.cs:25-27 vs DefaultBedrockRuntimeClientFactory.cs:11.
- QUIRK-7.16: Readiness ignores the region completely — never validated, never required. Evidence: BedrockService.cs:23-28.
- QUIRK-7.17: Readiness reads AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID a second time, redundantly; the resolved access key already consults it. Evidence: BedrockService.cs:27 vs ChatSettings.cs:73.
- QUIRK-7.18: The default model identifier is 'gpt-4' — non-empty and not a marketplace identifier — so the model half of readiness passes out of the box and 'gpt-4' is sent down the generic body path. Evidence: ChatSettings.cs:11.
- QUIRK-7.19: Temporary and single-sign-on credentials are silently broken — standard environment variables are hoisted into a static key pair and re-injected without the session token. No session-token support exists anywhere. Evidence: ChatSettings.cs:73, :76 + DefaultBedrockRuntimeClientFactory.cs:11-18.
- QUIRK-7.20: An unrecognised region name does not fail fast — no allow-list, format check or normalisation at any layer; the windowed dialog substitutes us-east-1 only when the field yields nothing. INFERRED that the endpoint is fabricated. Evidence: SetCommand.cs:92-94; SettingsDialog.cs:411, :417; DefaultBedrockRuntimeClientFactory.cs:16.
- QUIRK-7.21: awsAccessKey and awsSecretKey are recognised settings keys but are missing from the 'Valid keys' list shown on a mistyped key. Evidence: SetCommand.cs:264-270 vs :280.
- QUIRK-7.22: The credential vault degrades silently off its native platform — every failure, including 'wrong platform', is swallowed and reported as 'not found' with no indication the source was skipped. Evidence: WindowsCredentialManager.cs:59-60; ChatSettings.cs:138-141.
- QUIRK-7.23: The windowed host performs no readiness check before sending, so an unconfigured user sees the internal developer-worded guard message in a modal instead of remediation text. Evidence: ChatWindow.cs:420-441 vs ChatShell.cs:356-359; message at BedrockService.cs:40.
- QUIRK-7.24: The windowed host never releases its back ends — its main window declares no teardown at all. Harmless only because release is a no-op. Evidence: no release member in ChatWindow.cs; contrast src/ChatDbg/Program.cs:5 + src/ChatDbg/ChatShell.cs:698-713.
- QUIRK-7.25: The windowed project contains a whole second, unreachable host-wiring file that registers only two of the three back ends and carries its own readiness checks and teardown. Copying the wrong file would ship a two-provider product. Evidence: src/ChatDbg.Shell.Gui/ChatShell.cs:33-37, :223, :309, :651-666 vs src/ChatDbg.Shell.Gui/Program.cs:22-26, :79-87.
- QUIRK-7.26: The windowed host's 'provider is not set' modal is unreachable because the provider setting is non-nullable and defaults to azure. Evidence: ChatWindow.cs:426-430 vs ChatSettings.cs:8.
- QUIRK-7.27: Three different fallback strings exist for the same 'answer text absent' condition — 'Error: Response text expected, none given.', the misspelled 'Error: Response text expected, none recieved.', and an empty string — with two more variants in the sibling back ends. Evidence: BedrockService.cs:33; ChatShell.cs:377; ChatWindow.cs:444; AzureOpenAIService.cs:50; LLamaSharpService.cs:58.
- QUIRK-7.28: A fresh remote client is constructed per chat turn, paired with a release operation that does nothing and a cleanup hook with nothing to clean; connection pooling and credential-discovery caching are forfeited. Evidence: BedrockService.cs:45, :326-344.
- QUIRK-7.29: The request body is serialised twice per turn — once to a string purely for the diagnostic trace, once to bytes for the request — even when nothing is listening. Evidence: BedrockService.cs:107, :115.
- QUIRK-7.30: The diagnostic trace records the entire conversation and reply verbatim with no redaction or truncation; no credentials appear but every prompt and answer does. Evidence: BedrockService.cs:108, :124.
- QUIRK-7.31: The README names no cloud credential environment variable and the settings command refuses to accept keys, so a README-only user cannot reach a configured state. Evidence: README.md:123-129; SetCommand.cs:264-270.
- QUIRK-7.32: The README overclaims token probability support for this marketplace. Code wins — treat it as aspirational. Evidence: README.md:206 vs BedrockService.cs:76-77, ChatShell.cs:389-390.
- QUIRK-7.33: The settings help suggests the model identifier 'claude-3', which fails the 'anthropic.' prefix test, while the README example passes it. Code wins. Evidence: SetCommand.cs:310 vs README.md:127 vs BedrockService.cs:61.
- QUIRK-7.34: 'Standard cloud credential chain' is claimed in the project's assistant instructions but inverted in practice — the default chain is only the fallback. Code wins. Evidence: .github/copilot-instructions.md:162 vs ChatSettings.cs:73, :76 + DefaultBedrockRuntimeClientFactory.cs:11-20.
- QUIRK-7.35: 'Role-based authentication support' is claimed but not designed for; roles work only through the fallback branch and, combined with QUIRK-7.19, any role issuing temporary credentials is broken. Code wins. Evidence: .github/copilot-instructions.md:163.
- QUIRK-7.36: A design document claims the local-model introspection format matches this marketplace's format; no such established format exists. Code wins. Evidence: docs/LLamaSharp-Token-Introspection.md:320.
- QUIRK-7.37: The project's assistant instructions name a different runtime major version than every project file targets. No behavioural impact. Evidence: .github/copilot-instructions.md:138, :240 vs the four project files.
- QUIRK-7.38: Two stale duplicated specification files describe a two-provider product and pin an older client-library version than the one referenced. Evidence: src/ChatDbg/prd.md:53, :114 and the identical src/ChatDbg.Shell.Gui/prd.md vs SetCommand.cs:50, Xcaciv.ChatDbg.Core.csproj:11.
- QUIRK-7.39: The readiness test is misnamed and environment-dependent — it builds a record whose model identifier is the non-empty default and passes only because no key resolves, so it fails on any machine exporting CHATDBG_AWS_ACCESS_KEY or AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID. Evidence: BedrockServiceTests.cs:17-23; ChatSettings.cs:11, :73.
- QUIRK-7.40: The only round-trip test pairs a Claude-family request with a completion-shaped reply — a combination no real service produces — leaving the structured-content branch with zero coverage. Evidence: BedrockServiceTests.cs:58 vs :30; BedrockService.cs:133-188.
- QUIRK-7.41: Both tests supply credentials through the deprecated settings-document fields — the exact storage the product refuses to let a user populate. Evidence: BedrockServiceTests.cs:59-60, DefaultFactoriesTests.cs:28-29 vs SetCommand.cs:264-270.
- QUIRK-7.42: The client-construction test asserts only non-nullness with dummy credentials, proving nothing about which construction branch was taken; the region-only branch is never exercised. Evidence: DefaultFactoriesTests.cs:19-33 vs DefaultBedrockRuntimeClientFactory.cs:20.
- QUIRK-3.1: Documentation claims 'no plaintext credential storage on disk', but the three legacy credential fields are still serialized on every save and a freshly created settings file is written containing all three as empty strings; the storage slot and the read path both remain fully live. Evidence: docs/SECURITY-IMPLEMENTATION.md:18, docs/WINCRED-IMPLEMENTATION.md:173, Models/ChatSettings.cs:79-86, Services/SettingsService.cs:47-53.
- QUIRK-3.2: The user manual names the vault commands and the vault-enabled setting but documents no environment variable names anywhere, so a reimplementer working from it alone would miss the primary credential channel. Evidence: README.md:58-60,106.
- QUIRK-3.3: The migration instructions interpolate the actual stored secrets into 'set CHATDBG_...=<value>' lines on standard output, and that block prints automatically on every settings load while plaintext credentials exist, including the extra load performed mid-way through the store-a-secret operation. Evidence: Services/SettingsService.cs:335,341,346 and :60-64.
- QUIRK-3.4: Declining the consent prompt is reported as a failure ('Failed to enable Windows Credential Manager integration.'), conflating 'the user said no' with 'something broke'. Evidence: Commands/SetCommand.cs:255-257.
- QUIRK-3.5: The migration command always returns success whatever happened, while the wizard returns 'not migrated' whenever the cleanup prompt is declined, so a fully successful vault migration is reported as 'No credentials found to migrate or migration cancelled.' Evidence: Commands/SetCommand.cs:275-277, Services/SettingsService.cs:261.
- QUIRK-3.6: The status builder substitutes a string that does not occur anywhere in the block being built, so the substitution is a permanent no-op and the status output never adapts to platform. Evidence: Commands/SetCommand.cs:451-455.
- QUIRK-3.7: The store-a-secret operation re-reads settings from disk and ignores the caller's record, so the plaintext warning and secret-bearing instruction block may print again mid-command, unsaved in-memory changes are silently dropped, and the record the shell continues to use is not updated. Evidence: Services/SettingsService.cs:155.
- QUIRK-3.8: The instructions printer accepts an 'environment variables only' mode flag that both wizard branches pass explicitly and the body never consults; output is byte-identical in all cases. Evidence: Services/SettingsService.cs:328 versus :216,232,330-357.
- QUIRK-3.9: A corrupt settings file silently reverts the vault-enabled flag to false for the session, so the encrypted tier disappears with only a generic 'Error loading settings:' line. Evidence: Services/SettingsService.cs:78-82.
- QUIRK-3.10: Save failures are invisible to every caller: the save prints an error and returns normally with no failure signal, so the settings command, the enable flow, the store flow, bulk migration, the cleanup step and the graphical confirm button all report success while nothing reached disk. Evidence: Services/SettingsService.cs:100-103.
- QUIRK-3.11: The graphical manage-credentials dialog calls the store operation without checking the vault-enabled flag (unlike the console command, which refuses) and reports 'Credential saved successfully' regardless of the returned boolean. Evidence: UI/SettingsDialog.cs:543-546.
- QUIRK-3.12: The graphical migrate-credentials radio selection is read into a local value and never used; the same console-driven wizard runs regardless, writing prompts beneath a full-screen interface and blocking on input that cannot be typed, and the dialog reports 'Credentials migrated successfully' unconditionally. Evidence: UI/SettingsDialog.cs:587-589.
- QUIRK-3.13: In the graphical shell the settings command is bound to a record later replaced by the loaded one, so its enable gate always sees false and its plaintext check always sees empty fields, making '/set wincred' unable to succeed and '/set migrate' a guaranteed no-op from the chat box while the dialog-driven button works on the real data. The same project also contains an entire alternative shell class with a duplicate copy of the startup credential diagnostics that is never instantiated. Evidence: ChatDbg.Shell.Gui/Program.cs:14,38,58; UI/ChatWindow.cs:1037; UI/SettingsDialog.cs:17-22; ChatDbg.Shell.Gui/ChatShell.cs:213-295.
- QUIRK-3.14: A delete-a-vault-entry primitive is fully implemented and has zero call sites anywhere; no command, menu item, or wizard step removes a stored secret, though the documentation states delete is implemented. Rotation and revocation must be done in the operating system's own credential interface. Evidence: Models/WindowsCredentialManager.cs:148-163; docs/WINCRED-IMPLEMENTATION.md:26.
- QUIRK-3.15: Two separate text-corruption defects, not one. (a) The settings-service file is pure ASCII with status glyphs committed as genuine question-mark characters, so the program really prints '??  WARNING: Credentials found in settings file...' and '? Credential stored securely...'. (b) The settings-command help text and the graphical settings dialog are stored with a raw 0x95 byte (a Windows-1252 bullet) that is not valid UTF-8, so every help bullet decodes to a replacement glyph. Evidence: Services/SettingsService.cs:62,110,180,257; Commands/SetCommand.cs:307-352; UI/SettingsDialog.cs.
- QUIRK-3.16: The documentation names a specific platform encryption facility for the vault; the code only calls the generic credential-store API and encryption at rest is whatever the operating system provides. Evidence: docs/SECURITY-IMPLEMENTATION.md:66,100.
- QUIRK-3.17: The two credential test scripts are manual, assert nothing, exit zero regardless, and end by launching the application, so neither can run unattended. One sets scratch variables (TEMP_AZURE_KEY, TEMP_AWS_ACCESS, TEMP_AWS_SECRET) that are not credential variable names and are never read by the product; they exist only to echo example values into printed instructions. Evidence: tmp/test-wincred.cmd:11-13,26-28; tmp/test-env-vars.cmd:48-50.
- QUIRK-3.18: The documentation shows a 'Credential Storage Priority' footer inside the status output that the code never prints. Evidence: docs/SECURITY-IMPLEMENTATION.md:150-154.
- QUIRK-3.19: The graphical settings dialog can enable the vault on a platform that has none: the checkbox is copied straight into the flag and saved with no availability check, while the equivalent console command refuses. Every subsequent load then prints the 'enabled but not available' warning forever and every resolution wastes a probe that can only return nothing. Evidence: UI/SettingsDialog.cs:431-437,501 versus Commands/SetCommand.cs:219-222.
- QUIRK-3.20: The cloud-inference provider reports itself configured on the strength of a model identifier and the access key alone, never inspecting the secret key, so the provenance line prints, remediation is suppressed, and the client silently falls back to the vendor SDK's own credential chain because it requires both halves. Evidence: Services/BedrockService.cs:23-27; Services/DefaultBedrockRuntimeClientFactory.cs:11-20.
- QUIRK-3.21: The same configured-check ORs the resolved access key with a direct read of AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID, but the resolution layer already consults that variable as the access key's second name, so the second half of the OR can never decide anything, and it bypasses the very resolution layer this feature exists to provide. Evidence: Services/BedrockService.cs:26-27; Models/ChatSettings.cs:73.
- QUIRK-3.22: The startup provenance line for the cloud-inference provider is emitted when either half of the key pair is non-empty but always reports the source of the access key, so an operator whose secret key came from the vault is told the credentials came from the environment. Evidence: ChatDbg/ChatShell.cs:310-312.
- QUIRK-3.23: No validation of a secret before storage: no length cap, no character filter, no emptiness check between reading the typed value and handing it to the operating system. An oversized value produces only a generic failure with no hint of the cause. Evidence: Models/WindowsCredentialManager.cs:110-127. (INFERRED: the platform's documented generic-credential blob limit is 2560 bytes / 1280 UTF-16 characters; the limit is platform documentation, not code.)
- QUIRK-3.24: Entries are written with local-machine persistence, which explicitly does not roam with a domain profile, while the documentation promises central policy management, domain security, enterprise backup/restore and enterprise integration, all of which describe the roaming persistence scope that is not used. Evidence: Models/WindowsCredentialManager.cs:120; docs/WINCRED-IMPLEMENTATION.md:179-181; docs/SECURITY-IMPLEMENTATION.md:103,242-245.
- QUIRK-3.25: A doubly-swallowed fault: the resolution path wraps its vault lookup in a catch-everything around an adapter that already catches everything and returns 'no value'. The outer handler is unreachable in practice, and together they guarantee a genuine vault fault (policy denial, corrupted store, exhausted handles) is indistinguishable from 'the entry is not there', at every layer, forever, with nothing logged. Evidence: Models/ChatSettings.cs:137-141; Models/WindowsCredentialManager.cs:88-91.
- QUIRK-3.26: The store operation lower-cases the type for lookup but echoes the operator's original casing in both success and failure messages, so '/set wincred AZURE hunter2' reports storing a credential type named 'AZURE' that appears in no documentation and no valid-types list. Evidence: Services/SettingsService.cs:157,180,185; Commands/SetCommand.cs:243,249-250.
- QUIRK-3.27: The status display and the startup line each resolve every credential twice through two independent passes, so one status command on an enabled vault-capable host performs up to six native vault round-trips and the two passes can in principle disagree. Evidence: Commands/SetCommand.cs:420-422.
- QUIRK-3.28: In the graphical shell every credential message (load-time plaintext warning, secret-bearing migration instructions, wizard menus, both consent prompts, unknown-type errors, enable banner) is written directly to standard output and read from standard input inside the service layer, beneath a full-screen interface painted over the same terminal. The secrets still reach the scrollback; they are merely invisible while the interface is up, and the prompts block the interface thread. Evidence: Services/SettingsService.cs throughout; ChatDbg.Shell.Gui/Program.cs:71-90.
- QUIRK-3.29: The documented consent prompt text does not match the shipped one: documentation shows 'Do you want to enable Windows Credential Manager? (y/N):' while the code asks 'Do you want to enable Windows Credential Manager for secure credential storage? (y/N): '. Evidence: docs/SECURITY-IMPLEMENTATION.md:121; Services/SettingsService.cs:120.
- QUIRK-3.30: A fourth storage tier ('encrypted credential files') is listed inside a defense-in-depth list presented as implemented and does not exist in the code; there are exactly three tiers. Evidence: docs/SECURITY-IMPLEMENTATION.md:183.
- QUIRK-3.31: No settings-file permission hardening exists anywhere: no mode or access-control-list call, no ownership check, and the repository ignore list does not name the settings file, so an operator who relocates the settings directory into a working tree gets no protection from the tooling. Evidence: Services/SettingsService.cs:89-93; .gitignore.
- QUIRK-4.1: Inject reports a position it did not use — an out-of-range or negative position appends at the end while the confirmation still names the requested position (ChatHistory.cs:43-50; InjectCommand.cs:42-45).
- QUIRK-4.2: Pop's confirmation always ends in three dots, even when nothing was truncated; an empty-content message pops as 'Removed last message: [user] ...' (PopCommand.cs:29).
- QUIRK-4.3: Pop's 50-character preview slices by raw UTF-16 code unit, so a surrogate pair or combining sequence straddling offset 50 is split (PopCommand.cs:29).
- QUIRK-4.4: The full-screen inject dialog silently discards an unparseable position while the console keeps it as message text — two paths, two incompatible rules (ChatWindow.cs:967-970; InjectCommand.cs:36).
- QUIRK-4.5: The real failure reason never reaches the user — export/import failures are printed to stdout and reduced to a generic 'Failed to ...: {path}' with no cause (ChatHistoryService.cs:39-43, :60-64).
- QUIRK-4.6: Export is not atomic and destroys the previous file first — whole-file overwrite, no temp file, no rename, no overwrite warning (ChatHistoryService.cs:36).
- QUIRK-4.7: Import discards the imported created-at timestamp while copying the session identifier on the adjacent line (ImportCommand.cs:43-45).
- QUIRK-4.8: Import performs zero validation — roles bypass the three-value whitelist that inject enforces, content is unbounded, timestamps unchecked, hidden-from-model flag honored as read (ImportCommand.cs:43-44).
- QUIRK-4.9: No confirmation and no undo on either destructive operation, and the full-screen File menu places Clear History directly below Pop Last Message (ClearCommand.cs:18-22; PopCommand.cs:19-30; ChatWindow.cs:267-268).
- QUIRK-4.10: Import reads the whole file into memory with no size guard, no streaming and no cap (ChatHistoryService.cs:56).
- QUIRK-4.11: No path restrictions whatsoever — '/export /etc/passwd.json' and '/import ../../secrets.json' are attempted verbatim (ExportCommand.cs:28-42; ImportCommand.cs:28-36).
- QUIRK-4.12: Export defaults the file extension but import does not, so '/export mychats' followed by '/import mychats' fails (ExportCommand.cs:37-40 vs ImportCommand.cs:28-34).
- QUIRK-4.13: The '.json' default is keyed on 'has any extension', not 'is the conversation format' — '/export notes.txt' writes the format into a .txt file, and '.history' gets no suffix (ExportCommand.cs:37-40).
- QUIRK-4.14: Home-directory expansion handles exactly one form; a bare '~', a backslash form and '~someuser/' produce a literal directory named '~' on disk (ExportCommand.cs:31-34; ImportCommand.cs:31-34).
- QUIRK-4.15: Runs of spaces collapse with no quoting mechanism, and the file dialogs route browser-chosen paths back through the same tokenizer (ChatShell.cs:326; ChatWindow.cs:384, :1014, :1031).
- QUIRK-4.16: The two shells disagree about trimming user input — the full-screen shell trims, the plain-console shell stores verbatim (ChatWindow.cs:334 vs ChatShell.cs:83, :346).
- QUIRK-4.17: Role matching is case-insensitive when rendering but case-sensitive when selecting for the token-probability panel, so an imported 'Assistant' renders but is invisible to the panel (ChatWindow.cs:513, :523, :554, :570 vs :219, :233, :364, :447, :837, :873).
- QUIRK-4.18: Whitespace-only wrapped lines are dropped from the transcript, so blank lines vanish and empty-content messages render as a bare role header while still being sent to the provider (ChatWindow.cs:543).
- QUIRK-4.19: The local on-device provider ignores the hidden-from-model exclusion and reads only the last user-role message, so the same record produces materially different context depending on provider (LLamaSharpService.cs:131, :361).
- QUIRK-4.20: The hidden-from-model flag is a dormant hook — persisted, imported, filtered on by three provider paths, but never set true by any shipping code (ChatHistory.cs:14, :20; ChatMessage.cs:13-14).
- QUIRK-4.21: The session identifier is write-only data — generated, persisted, imported, overwritten, and never read by anything else (ChatHistory.cs:9-10; ImportCommand.cs:45).
- QUIRK-4.22: Pop and clear silently ignore all arguments — '/pop 3' removes one message, '/clear everything' succeeds (PopCommand.cs:19-24; ClearCommand.cs:18-22).
- QUIRK-4.23: Persistence diagnostics written to standard output paint over the full-screen layout on any export/import failure; there is no logger abstraction and no suppression (ChatHistoryService.cs:41, :52, :62).
- QUIRK-4.24: A dead, never-instantiated duplicate of the console shell lives in the full-screen project and wires the same five history operations but drops token log probabilities when appending replies — porting the wrong copy loses the payload (ChatDbg.Shell.Gui/ChatShell.cs:330; ChatDbg.Shell.Gui/Program.cs:77-90).
- QUIRK-4.25: The 'Toggle System Messages' View-menu item is a stub reporting 'System messages toggle not yet implemented' (ChatWindow.cs:1138-1142).
- QUIRK-4.26: The status-bar revert timer is unconditional, so two commands within 3 seconds leave the second message wiped early by the first timer. INFERRED — no test (ChatWindow.cs:903-916).
- QUIRK-4.27: 'Persistent chat history' is promised by the project's own requirements document and README but there is no autosave and no autoload; the dispose path saves nothing. CODE WINS (prd.md:17, README.md:11 vs ChatShell.cs:23, :692-713).
- QUIRK-4.28: Pop is documented as removing 'messages' (plural) but removes exactly one — the last (prd.md:103 vs ChatHistory.cs:26-32).
- QUIRK-4.29: The project documents disagree about the target platform (Windows-only vs cross-platform); this feature's code is genuinely cross-platform and the Windows framing is a packaging default (README.md:3 vs prd.md:34).
- QUIRK-4.30: Timestamps stop being monotonic the moment anything is injected — an injected message is stamped with the current time and placed at an arbitrary index, so consumers of an exported file must not sort by timestamp (ChatHistory.cs:34-41 vs :43-50).
- QUIRK-2.1: /model bypasses every rule /set modelId enforces - no local-model file-existence check, no emptiness check - though both write the same field and both persist. Evidence: Commands/ModelCommand.cs:28-34 vs Commands/SetCommand.cs:55-68.
- QUIRK-2.2: The plain-console shell never restores showAllTokens, gridViewForTokens or gridViewMaxAlternatives, so every console session starts with list layout / samples-only / 5 alternatives regardless of the document. Evidence: ChatDbg/ChatShell.cs:130-151.
- QUIRK-2.3: In the windowed Settings dialog, changing the provider selection takes effect even on Cancel; Cancel only skips the disk write, so the session runs on the new provider while the document names the old one. Evidence: ChatDbg.Shell.Gui/UI/SettingsDialog.cs:139-153, :59-64.
- QUIRK-2.4: In the windowed shell the text commands and the UI operate on two different records; commands mutate and persist a stale pre-load defaults record the window never reads, overwriting the document with mostly-default values. Evidence: ChatDbg.Shell.Gui/Program.cs:14, :37-41, :58, :79-81.
- QUIRK-2.5: A failed save still reports success - the save swallows its exception after printing 'Error saving settings: <message>' and the command returns 'Set <key> = <value>' with a success flag. Evidence: Services/SettingsService.cs:100-103 with Commands/SetCommand.cs:284-289.
- QUIRK-2.6: llamaGpuDevice, llamaThreads and llamaBatchSize are validated, clamped, persisted and displayed but never reach the local model runtime; only context size and GPU layer count do. Evidence: Services/LLamaSharpService.cs:550-551; README:87-91, 138-159 claim otherwise.
- QUIRK-2.7: The unknown-setting error lists only 13 of roughly 26 accepted keys, omitting the three display keys, all five local-model keys and every short alias. Evidence: Commands/SetCommand.cs:279-280 vs :303-375.
- QUIRK-2.8: Dead find-and-replace in the settings dump - where no keystore exists, the text is post-processed to replace a sentence that does not exist in it, so the substitution is always a no-op. Evidence: Commands/SetCommand.cs:451-455.
- QUIRK-2.9: Startup migration instructions echo plaintext secrets to the console as 'set CHATDBG_AZURE_API_KEY=<the actual key>'. Evidence: Services/SettingsService.cs:332-347 reached from :60-64.
- QUIRK-2.10: /set wincred persists the on-disk record, not the live one - the delegate re-loads from disk, flips the toggle on that copy and saves it. Evidence: Services/SettingsService.cs:155, :176-178.
- QUIRK-2.11: Storing a credential silently turns the keystore toggle on, even though the command reaching it already refuses to run unless the toggle was on. Evidence: Services/SettingsService.cs:176-178.
- QUIRK-2.12: Mojibake in user-facing strings - console messages contain literal '?'/'??' where emoji were intended, and the /set help text is stored in a non-UTF-8 encoding whose bullet is a lone 0x95 byte. Evidence: Services/SettingsService.cs:62; Commands/SetCommand.cs:308.
- QUIRK-2.13: An entire duplicated shell component in the windowed project is dead code, carrying its own hydration routine that omits even more fields than the console shell's; nothing constructs it. Evidence: ChatDbg.Shell.Gui/ChatShell.cs:14, :123-152.
- QUIRK-2.14: The documentation's General Settings list omits showAllTokens, gridViewForTokens and gridViewMaxAlternatives though they are persisted fields documented elsewhere in the same file. Evidence: README:95-111 vs 226-254.
- QUIRK-2.15: Decimal parsing is culture-sensitive but only in some builds - invariant globalization is forced only under the Compact and SingleFile configurations, so /set temperature 0.7 is rejected by an ordinary build and accepted by the shipped compact binary on a comma-decimal locale. Evidence: Commands/SetCommand.cs:72; ChatDbg.Shell.Gui/UI/SettingsDialog.cs:420-422, :121; src/ChatDbg/Xcaciv.ChatDbg.Shell.csproj:23, :52, :63, :95; src/ChatDbg.Shell.Gui/Xcaciv.ChatDbg.Shell.Gui.csproj:52, :95.
- QUIRK-2.16: The success echo reports what the user typed, not what was stored - /set provider AZURE stores 'azure' but answers 'Set provider = AZURE', while the key is echoed lowercased. Evidence: Commands/SetCommand.cs:47, :52, :289.
- QUIRK-2.17: /set provider reads only the first value token but echoes all of them, so '/set provider azure and then some' succeeds, stores 'azure' and reports the whole string. Evidence: Commands/SetCommand.cs:47 vs :56, :71, :89.
- QUIRK-2.18: The windowed Credentials tab turns the OS-keystore toggle on with no platform check, persisting a toggle the text command refuses, which then only produces a startup warning. Evidence: ChatDbg.Shell.Gui/UI/SettingsDialog.cs:436-437 vs Commands/SetCommand.cs:219-222.
- QUIRK-2.19: The windowed Manage Credentials dialog discards the store result and reports 'Credential saved successfully' unconditionally, never checks the keystore toggle, and silently does nothing on an empty type or value. Evidence: ChatDbg.Shell.Gui/UI/SettingsDialog.cs:534-552, esp. :543-545, :539.
- QUIRK-2.20: The windowed Migrate Credentials dialog's three-way choice is read into an unused variable, the console-driven flow is called regardless, 'Credentials migrated successfully' is shown for every outcome, and the flow's own prompts are invisible because it blocks on standard input while the windowed UI owns the screen. Evidence: ChatDbg.Shell.Gui/UI/SettingsDialog.cs:586-601, esp. :588, :593-595.
- QUIRK-2.21: /set wincred re-triggers the plaintext-secret echo, because storing a credential re-loads the record and the load path re-runs the deprecated-secret check. Evidence: Services/SettingsService.cs:155 -> :43-64 -> :328-357.
- QUIRK-2.22: The migration-instructions routine declares an 'environment variables only' flag it never reads, so options 1 and 3 emit exactly the same text as the unsolicited startup warning. Evidence: Services/SettingsService.cs:328 declares it; nothing in :330-359 reads it; callers at :216, :232.
- QUIRK-2.23: Migration instructions can print a secret with no heading - the 'For AWS Bedrock:' heading is emitted only inside the AWS access-key branch, so a document holding only an AWS secret key yields a bare 'set CHATDBG_AWS_SECRET_KEY=<secret>' line. Evidence: Services/SettingsService.cs:338-347.
- QUIRK-2.24: In the shipped compact and single-file builds every error message interpolating a platform exception degrades to bare resource keys. Evidence: src/ChatDbg/Xcaciv.ChatDbg.Shell.csproj:53, :96 and the same lines in the windowed project. INFERRED - the property is observed; the resulting text was not reproduced.
- QUIRK-2.25: A dead default on the AWS region field - the save path guards it with a fallback to us-east-1, but the widget yields an empty string rather than nothing when cleared, so clearing the box stores '' and the fallback never fires; the Model ID field has the same dead guard. Evidence: ChatDbg.Shell.Gui/UI/SettingsDialog.cs:417-418. INFERRED - rests on widget-toolkit semantics; not reproduced.
- QUIRK-2.26: In the windowed shell the Change Model dialog appears to do nothing - it routes through /model, which holds the stale record, then refreshes the status bar from the live record that was never touched. Evidence: ChatDbg.Shell.Gui/UI/ChatWindow.cs:1053-1075, esp. :1066, :1071, against ChatDbg.Shell.Gui/Program.cs:14, :37, :58.
- QUIRK-2.27: The provider selector pre-selects Azure OpenAI for any unrecognised provider (including a capitalised 'Azure'), claiming a provider that is not in effect, and opening and OK-ing the dialog does not correct the stored value. Evidence: ChatDbg.Shell.Gui/UI/SettingsDialog.cs:83-89, :139-153. INFERRED - depends on the toolkit's construction-time event semantics; not reproduced.
- QUIRK-2.28: Trailing whitespace in the settings dump - a non-zero GPU layer count renders as '- GPU Layer Count: 32 ' with a trailing space. Evidence: Commands/SetCommand.cs:426.
- QUIRK-2.29: The documentation never names the credential environment variables, though it advertises multi-level secure credential management and has no security or credentials section at all. Evidence: verified by search over README.md; names appear only at Commands/SetCommand.cs:262, :266, :270, :348-352 and ChatDbg/ChatShell.cs:265, :282-283.
- QUIRK-2.30: The documentation's persisted-field list omits the three deprecated secret slots that every written document always contains. Evidence: README:95-111 vs Models/ChatSettings.cs:79-86.
- QUIRK-2.31: The system-prompt placeholder reads '<n>' rather than '<name>' in the long help, the settings dump, the documentation and the /prompt use error text. Evidence: Commands/SetCommand.cs:327, :438; README:53; Commands/PromptCommand.cs:150.
- QUIRK-2.32: maxTokens has no effect on the default provider in the default configuration - the Azure non-log-probabilities path never sends a cap, while the log-probabilities path does, and Bedrock and the local provider always honour it. Evidence: Services/AzureOpenAIService.cs:61-64, :97-100, :152-159; Services/BedrockService.cs:71, :97; Services/LLamaSharpService.cs:143, :373.
- QUIRK-2.33: The Azure API version is hard-coded to 2023-12-01-preview with no setting, environment variable or override, while /logprobs debug advises the user to ensure a recent API version as if it were theirs to choose. Evidence: Services/AzureOpenAIService.cs:120; Commands/LogProbsCommand.cs:185.
- QUIRK-2.34: The two local-model context-size fallbacks disagree - 4096 in one path, 2048 in the model-loading path, for the same 'value not greater than zero' condition. Evidence: Services/LLamaSharpService.cs:291-292 vs :550.
- QUIRK-5.1: The library's serializer is configured for indented output AFTER the constructor seeds the built-ins, so the four seeded files are written compact on one line while every later save is written indented. Evidence: Services/SystemPromptService.cs:32 runs before :34-37; writer used at :106.
- QUIRK-5.2: /prompt list intends to print the library's directory but instead re-queries the whole prompt collection and prints the collection object's type name, so users literally see 'System prompts directory: System.Collections.Generic.List`1[Xcaciv.ChatDbg.Core.Models.SystemPrompt]'. Root cause: the 'report the store directory' capability exists only on the concrete store, not on the contract the command holds. Evidence: Commands/PromptCommand.cs:102; Services/SystemPromptService.cs:43; Services/ISystemPromptService.cs:5-12. Exact rendered characters INFERRED; the defect is certain.
- QUIRK-5.3: As a consequence of QUIRK-5.2, /prompt list reads the entire library from disk twice per invocation. Evidence: Commands/PromptCommand.cs:78 and :102.
- QUIRK-5.4: The interactive content editor's read loop compares each line against 'END' but never checks for end-of-input; with standard input redirected and exhausted it never terminates and appends blank lines without bound - an unbounded-memory hang. Evidence: Commands/PromptCommand.cs:267-270. INFERRED from code shape.
- QUIRK-5.5: /prompt edit writes to and reads from the raw console, so inside the full-screen terminal shell (where commands run in the UI event loop) its output never reaches the chat pane and its input never arrives - the subcommand is effectively unusable there. Evidence: Commands/PromptCommand.cs:258-270 vs UI/ChatWindow.cs:382-399.
- QUIRK-5.6: The user manual nowhere records that the graphical path skips the active-prompt delete guard, skips duplicate-name protection on create and import, and skips the last-used stamp on Use. Evidence: README.md:15 vs UI/SystemPromptsDialog.cs:433-459, :296-332, :583-620, :205-226.
- QUIRK-5.7: The user manual's create example implies quoted descriptions are supported, but the parser has no quoting and the double-quote characters are stored verbatim in the description. Evidence: README.md:183 vs src/ChatDbg/ChatShell.cs:326 plus Commands/PromptCommand.cs:192.
- QUIRK-5.8: In the full-screen shell's entry point the command objects are constructed against one settings instance, and the settings variable is then reassigned to the instance loaded from disk (the one given to the main window and the providers), so commands mutate an orphaned settings object. Evidence: src/ChatDbg.Shell.Gui/Program.cs:30-46, :57, :76-84. INFERRED from construction ordering.
- QUIRK-5.9: The full-screen project contains a second, complete console-style shell class that is never instantiated but still carries its own copy of the startup prompt-resolution logic. Evidence: src/ChatDbg.Shell.Gui/ChatShell.cs - no construction site anywhere in src/; the type is present in the committed build output. INFERRED.
- QUIRK-5.10: The graphical dialog's Close button is centred on the same bottom row already occupied by the seven action buttons, so it visually overlaps them. Evidence: UI/SystemPromptsDialog.cs:60, :111-115.
- QUIRK-5.11: The 'Browse...' button does not browse - it opens a plain 60x8 text-entry box titled 'Enter File Path' where the user must type the whole path by hand. In-code comments blame a limitation of a toolkit major version the project does not actually use. Evidence: UI/SystemPromptsDialog.cs:522-567.
- QUIRK-5.12: Export and import are asymmetric with the stored record - export writes only the text as plain .txt so description, created-at and last-used are lost on a round trip, and importing a stored record file yields a prompt whose text is that file's raw markup. Evidence: Commands/PromptCommand.cs:320 vs :347-361.
- QUIRK-5.13: The default export directory differs between the two surfaces (user Documents for the command path, user home for the graphical path) and the graphical default does not sanitize the name into the file name. Evidence: Commands/PromptCommand.cs:313-314 vs UI/SystemPromptsDialog.cs:645-647.
- QUIRK-5.14: Three sources disagree about the terminal-interface toolkit version - in-code comments say major version 2, the project file pins 1.19.0, and docs say 1.17.1 and claim the dialog is 402 lines when it is 697. Resolved from the committed build output: the shipped shell carries 1.19.0 plus its 1.x-only string dependency. Evidence: src/ChatDbg.Shell.Gui/Xcaciv.ChatDbg.Shell.Gui.csproj; docs/PHASE1-SUMMARY.md:41-48, :60; docs/TERMINAL-GUI-IMPLEMENTATION.md:128-133; bin/Debug/net10.0/Xcaciv.ChatDbg.Shell.Gui.deps.json.
- QUIRK-5.15: The graphical Create/Edit/Import handlers close the form window, start a background list reload, then immediately raise the success box without waiting - so the refreshed list and the success box are unordered, the reload's own failure has nowhere to surface, and the box is shown against an already-dismissed form. Evidence: UI/SystemPromptsDialog.cs:324-326, :417-419, :612-614.
- QUIRK-5.16: The library never guards against concurrent writers - no lock, no temp-file-and-rename, no version token - so two shells interleave whole-file overwrites, a last-used stamp can clobber a concurrent content edit, and a crash mid-write leaves a truncated file that is thereafter silently skipped. Evidence: Services/SystemPromptService.cs:100-113, :141-152.
- QUIRK-5.17: The user manual's export and import examples cannot work as written - both use a '~/'-prefixed path but no home-directory expansion exists anywhere, so export tries to write into a literal relative directory named '~' and fails, and import reports 'File not found: ~/Desktop/my-prompt.txt'. Evidence: README.md:189-190 vs Commands/PromptCommand.cs:307, :320, :337, :339. INFERRED for the exact failure text.
- QUIRK-5.18: The command-path export default silently loses its directory on Linux - the Documents well-known folder resolves to the empty string on a host with no XDG user-directory configuration, so the destination collapses to a bare relative name in the working directory and the success message shows no directory at all. Evidence: Commands/PromptCommand.cs:313-314; measured on both platforms.
- QUIRK-5.19: The graphical 'Please select a prompt first' guard is nearly dead code - the list's selected index starts at 0 and is still 0 after the list is populated, so whenever the library is non-empty the alphabetically first prompt counts as selected from the instant the dialog opens; pressing Delete immediately after opening targets that prompt. Evidence: UI/SystemPromptsDialog.cs:150-157, :162-166, :208-212, :342-347, :435-440, :630-635; measured against toolkit 1.19.0.
- QUIRK-5.20: Consequences of QUIRK-5.8 inside the full-screen shell - (a) /prompt list marks whichever prompt is named 'default' as ' (current)' regardless of the real active prompt; (b) /prompt delete refuses to delete 'default' and happily deletes the actually-active prompt; (c) /prompt use and /set systemPrompt write the orphaned settings object to the settings file, overwriting the user's stored provider, model, temperature, token limit, endpoint, region and every other persisted setting with construction-time defaults - silent data loss. Evidence: src/ChatDbg.Shell.Gui/Program.cs:14, :38, :40, :58, :79-87; Commands/PromptCommand.cs:91, :231, :166. INFERRED.
- QUIRK-5.21: Prompt libraries are not portable between operating systems - the file name replaces every character the HOST OS forbids, and that set is 41 characters on Windows versus 2 on Linux, so 'a:b' is stored as a_b.json on Windows but a:b.json on Linux; copying a library across platforms makes names unreachable one way and silently collide the other. Evidence: Services/SystemPromptService.cs:209-214; measured on both platforms.
- QUIRK-5.22: The command's own usage line advertises 'import <file>' when import actually requires two operands, <n> then <file_path> - the form the error message and the manual both give correctly. The same line renders every name placeholder as the bare token '<n>', which reads as 'a number', but that spelling is consistent across code, errors and the manual so 'correcting' it is a deviation. Evidence: Commands/PromptCommand.cs:25 vs :333; README.md:73.
- QUIRK-5.23: The graphical settings dialog is handed the prompt library at construction and never uses it - no field, no read, no write. Evidence: UI/ChatWindow.cs:1037 passes the store into UI/SettingsDialog.cs, which contains no reference to prompts at all.
- QUIRK-5.24: An unset last-used timestamp is written to disk as an explicit null rather than omitted, so every record file always carries all five keys; a reimplementation that omits absent fields produces byte-different files. Evidence: Models/SystemPrompt.cs:15-16; measured serialization.
- QUIRK-5.25: The repository as committed cannot be built - its global.json ends with a stray extra brace, making it invalid, and the build tool aborts with a parse error at line 5 from any directory inside the repository. This is why no dynamic verification of this feature was possible. Evidence: global.json (5 lines, trailing '}'); reproduced.
- QUIRK-5.26: Per-file read/parse failures during a listing are caught and skipped, but failure to ENUMERATE the library directory is not caught in the store at all - /prompt list surfaces the generic 'Error processing prompt command: <message>' and the graphical list reload, which runs on a background path with no handler, has nowhere to report it. Evidence: Services/SystemPromptService.cs:52 vs :54-66; Commands/PromptCommand.cs:52-55; UI/SystemPromptsDialog.cs:131-143.
- QUIRK-5.27: The graphical delete confirmation lists Yes first, so the affirmative is the focused/default choice and a stray Enter deletes; combined with QUIRK-5.19 and the missing active-prompt guard, two keystrokes from opening the dialog can delete the prompt the assistant is currently using. Evidence: UI/SystemPromptsDialog.cs:442-446. The index-0 mapping is in the code; the focus half is INFERRED.
- QUIRK-11.1: The export-logs command is unreachable - registered in neither shell's command table and absent from help output, so typing /export-logs anything yields the unknown-command response, while five product documents show it as working. Evidence: src/Xcaciv.ChatDbg.Core/Commands/ExportLogsCommand.cs; src/ChatDbg/ChatShell.cs:42-57; src/ChatDbg.Shell.Gui/ChatShell.cs:45-58; src/ChatDbg.Shell.Gui/Program.cs:31-47.
- QUIRK-11.2: Even if registered, the command exports nothing - it returns a canned advisory string, writes no file, and ignores the supplied path entirely; the documented example output 'Success: System logs exported to paris_debug.log' exists nowhere in code. Evidence: src/Xcaciv.ChatDbg.Core/Commands/ExportLogsCommand.cs:23; docs/LLamaSharp-Token-Introspection.md:301-305.
- QUIRK-11.3: A zero-argument invocation is reported as a failure but an invocation with a path is reported as a SUCCESS despite doing nothing, and the test suite locks that wrongness in by asserting success plus the literal substring 'Note'. Evidence: src/Xcaciv.ChatDbg.Core/Commands/ExportLogsCommand.cs:18-23; src/Xcaciv.ChatDbg.Core.Tests/Commands/ExportLogsAndAnalysisCommandTests.cs:10-28.
- QUIRK-11.4: Flushing defeats exporting - generation force-flushes and drains at the end of every run and after every error, while the snapshot export writes only what is currently buffered, so the documented 'run a generation then export' workflow produces a file containing none of the generation's diagnostics. Evidence: src/Xcaciv.ChatDbg.Core/Services/LLamaSharpService.cs:101,255,405,587,601 vs src/Xcaciv.ChatDbg.Core/Services/TokenInspection/LLamaSharpLogConfig.cs:182.
- QUIRK-11.5: The size-triggered auto-flush fires only for engine-sourced lines, never for application entries, so a run producing thousands of per-token DEBUG entries accumulates every one with no size-triggered flush. Evidence: LLamaSharpLogConfig.cs:90 present vs :123-126 absent; producer at LLamaSharpService.cs:231.
- QUIRK-11.6: The documented inference-engine dependency version (0.11.2) is stale; the build pins 0.25.0. Evidence: docs/LLamaSharp-Implementation-Notes.md package section and IMPLEMENTATION_SUMMARY.md vs src/Xcaciv.ChatDbg.Core/Xcaciv.ChatDbg.Core.csproj.
- QUIRK-11.7: The recorder is filed under a folder named for a different feature (token inspection) and its tests mirror that folder, yet it has no relationship to token inspection, which uses the raw platform debug stream directly. - QUIRK-11.8: Three kinds of per-user state go to three different per-user roots - logs to roaming application data, system prompts to local application data, settings to the user-profile root. - QUIRK-11.9: A unit test writes a real dated log file into the system temporary directory and never cleans it up, while a sibling test in the same file does clean up in a finally block. Evidence: src/Xcaciv.ChatDbg.Core.Tests/Services/TokenInspection/LLamaSharpLogConfigTests.cs:119-137 vs :85-90.
- QUIRK-11.10: The shipped builds are 64-bit-Windows-only by default in a product whose own assembly description calls it cross-platform; the condition supplies only a default that an explicit publish-time runtime identifier would override, and nothing in the repository ever does. - QUIRK-11.11: The feature's only internal-failure channel is compiled out of every build lacking the DEBUG symbol, and no project or shared property file defines that symbol outside stock Debug - so in Release, Compact and SingleFile builds the default-on debug-output switch does nothing and a completely dead logging subsystem reports nothing on any channel. Evidence: LLamaSharpLogConfig.cs:98,112,130,163,189; Directory.Build.props:1-5; src/Xcaciv.ChatDbg.Core/Xcaciv.ChatDbg.Core.csproj:1-19.
- QUIRK-11.12: The shipped builds gut the exception detail the logs exist to capture - Compact and SingleFile substitute bare framework resource keys for exception messages, strip symbols and disable stack-trace metadata, so every ERROR entry embedding an exception, and the chat-visible 'Error running local LLM: {message}' text, degrade to an opaque key. - QUIRK-11.13: The same source emits two different timestamp shapes depending on build configuration - timestamps and the daily file-name date render through the current culture, and only Compact and SingleFile force invariant globalization. Evidence: LLamaSharpLogConfig.cs:83,121,149; src/ChatDbg/Xcaciv.ChatDbg.Shell.csproj:52,95.
- QUIRK-11.14: The five configuration knobs the documentation tells users to set 'in config' have no configuration surface at all - absent from the settings model and the settings dialog - and the documentation's only example uses a Windows-only literal directory. Evidence: LLamaSharpService.cs:25; src/Xcaciv.ChatDbg.Core/Models/ChatSettings.cs and src/ChatDbg.Shell.Gui/UI/SettingsDialog.cs (no matches); docs/LLamaSharp-Implementation-Notes.md:199-208.
- QUIRK-11.15: Two of the seven recorder unit tests assert nothing - their entire assert step is a source comment - so both would still pass if flush-on-shutdown and arm-idempotency were deleted outright. Evidence: src/Xcaciv.ChatDbg.Core.Tests/Services/TokenInspection/LLamaSharpLogConfigTests.cs:118-137 (:134-135) and :139-153 (:152).
- QUIRK-11.16: The arm-idempotency test probably never exercises the path it names - installing the engine destination requires the native library, and on a host where it will not load the attempt throws, is swallowed, the armed latch is never set, and both calls take the un-armed path. INFERRED. Evidence: LLamaSharpLogConfigTests.cs:139-153; LLamaSharpLogConfig.cs:69-70,81,107,110-113.
- QUIRK-11.17: The documented promise 'logs flushed every 10,000 characters or on disposal' is half true - the threshold check exists only on the engine path, and the documentation omits the five forced flushes entirely. Evidence: docs/LLamaSharp-Quick-Start.md:206; LLamaSharpLogConfig.cs:90 vs :123-126.
- QUIRK-11.18: Exporting an empty buffer silently produces a 0-byte file - the snapshot write is unconditional, unlike the daily flush which skips an empty buffer - and the caller is told neither that it succeeded nor that it wrote nothing. Evidence: LLamaSharpLogConfig.cs:180-183 vs :154.
- QUIRK-11.19: A recorder armed with file capture off can never write a daily file afterwards, because directory creation is gated on the file-capture switch at arm time and arming is one-shot - which is exactly the state the product's own 'verify file logging is enabled' troubleshooting advice would leave a user in. Evidence: LLamaSharpLogConfig.cs:69-70,75,161-164; docs/LLamaSharp-Token-Introspection.md:258-261.
- QUIRK-11.20: The export command imports the console-rendering library and the services namespace and uses neither - vestigial evidence it was meant to reach a live service instance and never got wired to one. Evidence: src/Xcaciv.ChatDbg.Core/Commands/ExportLogsCommand.cs:1,3.
- QUIRK-11.21: Shutdown does not uninstall the process-global engine destination, and the installed callback holds a reference to the recorder's buffer, so after shutdown engine diagnostics keep accumulating into a buffer that will never be flushed. Evidence: LLamaSharpLogConfig.cs:81 vs :193-200.
- QUIRK-11.22: The owning subsystem's finalizer routes to shared teardown with the explicit-disposal flag false, and recorder teardown lives only on the true branch - so a subsystem reclaimed by the garbage collector loses its entire unflushed buffer with no final flush and no report. Evidence: src/Xcaciv.ChatDbg.Core/Services/LLamaSharpService.cs:693-696 vs :678-681.
- QUIRK-11.23: The documentation claims the per-token analysis record's system-debug-information field is populated from the log buffer; in code it is filled with a synthesized one-liner and no log content ever reaches it. Evidence: LLamaSharpService.cs:286; src/Xcaciv.ChatDbg.Core/Services/TokenInspection/TokenAnalysis.cs:52-56; docs/LLamaSharp-Implementation-Notes.md:266.
- QUIRK-11.24: On a Unix-like host with no home directory set, the per-user application-data lookup returns the empty string and the two path segments compose into a working-directory-relative path, so logs land wherever the process happens to be running; nothing guards the empty root. INFERRED from platform behaviour. Evidence: LLamaSharpLogConfig.cs:20.
- QUIRK-11.25: The maximum buffer size is a plain unvalidated setting compared with a bare strictly-greater-than test, so zero or a negative value is accepted and makes every single engine diagnostic line trigger its own file append, with no validation error at any point. Evidence: LLamaSharpLogConfig.cs:40 and :90.
- QUIRK-13.1: The three display-mode settings (show-all-tokens, grid-layout, grid-maximum-alternatives) are persisted and settable at runtime but never read back at startup, so every session begins in sample mode, list layout, cap 5. Evidence: src/ChatDbg/ChatShell.cs:130-151 vs Models/ChatSettings.cs:39-46, Commands/LogProbsCommand.cs:67-97, README.md:230-246.
- QUIRK-13.2: End-of-stream on standard input returns nothing, which the blank-input guard treats as 'keep going', producing an infinite busy loop that re-prints the prompt forever. Evidence: src/ChatDbg/ChatShell.cs:83-88 (measured).
- QUIRK-13.3: The user's turn is appended to the transcript before any validation, so failed turns accumulate orphan user messages that are re-sent as context on the next successful turn. Evidence: src/ChatDbg/ChatShell.cs:346 vs :349,356.
- QUIRK-13.4: Probability is a 0-1 value but the colour thresholds compare it against 90/70/50/30, so every genuine token renders red; the displayed number is nonetheless correct. - QUIRK-8.9: Prompt-offset attribution is a copy of the step index; nothing is computed. (LLamaSharpService.cs:284; Services/TokenInspection/TokenAnalysis.cs:41-44)
- QUIRK-8.10: A 'token' is a decoder text piece, not a model token, so counts can diverge for multi-byte characters. (LLamaSharpService.cs:170-175)
- QUIRK-8.11: The analysis export cannot fail visibly — errors go into a buffer nothing displays and the operation returns normally. (LLamaSharpService.cs:644-647)
- QUIRK-8.12: Absorbed failures are returned as success-shaped responses and appended to the transcript as assistant turns; the dedicated error field is read by nobody. (LLamaSharpService.cs:103-109; src/ChatDbg/ChatShell.cs:377-381)
- QUIRK-8.13: Instance shutdown disposes a process-wide lock shared by every instance. (LLamaSharpService.cs:24, :681)
- QUIRK-8.14: Asymmetric lock disposal — the equally process-wide model-load lock is never released or disposed. (LLamaSharpService.cs:23, :670-685)
- QUIRK-8.15: The reclamation path releases no native memory; an object collected without explicit shutdown leaks the model and context for the process lifetime. (LLamaSharpService.cs:670-696)
- QUIRK-8.16: Shutdown does not unregister the engine log hook, and the log component keeps buffering and writing after shutdown because neither logging nor flushing checks the shut-down flag. (LLamaSharpLogConfig.cs:81-105, :119-137, :142-165, :193-200)
- QUIRK-8.17: The engine log hook is process-global while the arm-once flag is per instance, so a second instance silently steals it. (LLamaSharpLogConfig.cs:15, :69-70, :81)
- QUIRK-8.18: The buffer flush threshold is checked only on engine-origin lines, so a debug-heavy run with no engine output grows the buffer unbounded. (LLamaSharpLogConfig.cs:90-93 vs :123-126)
- QUIRK-8.19: Saving the buffer to a path does not clear it while flushing does, duplicating content into the daily file; and the confirmation line is appended after the save, so the saved file never contains it. (LLamaSharpLogConfig.cs:152-158 vs :180-185)
- QUIRK-8.20: The 'not configured' message for this back end instructs the user to configure environment variables or the operating-system credential store, although it reads no credential. (src/ChatDbg/ChatShell.cs:358)
- QUIRK-8.21: Shutdown is not synchronised against an in-flight generation; context and weights can be released while streaming. (LLamaSharpService.cs:670-685)
- QUIRK-8.22: Three settings — graphics-device selector, thread count, batch size — are validated, persisted and printed by two user interfaces and read by nothing. (Models/ChatSettings.cs:59-66; Commands/SetCommand.cs:116-136, :424-429; absent from LLamaSharpService.cs:548-554)
- QUIRK-8.23: The introspection alternatives count silently sets the sampler's selection limit on both paths, so the default of 5 makes all local generation top-5-limited and a value of 1 makes it deterministic. (LLamaSharpService.cs:148, :378; Models/ChatSettings.cs:37)
- QUIRK-8.24: Hardware settings changed mid-session silently do nothing because reload is keyed on the model path only, while the settings surfaces report every change as applied. (LLamaSharpService.cs:525)
- QUIRK-8.25: Two context-size fallbacks disagree inside one file — the loader uses 2048, the state snapshot reports 4096. (LLamaSharpService.cs:550 vs :291-292)
- QUIRK-8.26: Stop sequences are hard-coded to 'User:' and 'USER:' — not configurable, not documented, and wrong for any other chat template. (LLamaSharpService.cs:144, :374)
- QUIRK-8.27: The default model identifier is a remote model name ('gpt-4'), which can never satisfy this back end's file-exists check. (Models/ChatSettings.cs:11)
- QUIRK-8.28: Only the last user message is ever sent; system messages and all prior turns are discarded. (LLamaSharpService.cs:131-136, :361-366)
- QUIRK-8.29: Clearing the conversation does not clear the model's memory; the model answers from turns the engine-side session still holds. (Commands/ClearCommand.cs:18-23; Commands/ImportCommand.cs:43)
- QUIRK-8.30: An empty user message is reported as a missing one. (LLamaSharpService.cs:131-136)
- QUIRK-8.31: The fast path's readiness check omits the context while the introspection path checks all three handles. (LLamaSharpService.cs:125-128 vs :355-358)
- QUIRK-8.32: Stale analyses survive a non-introspected run and there is no operation to clear them, although the documentation advises exporting and clearing beyond ~1000 tokens. (LLamaSharpService.cs:156, no counterpart at :353-414)
- QUIRK-8.33: The null-reply substitute is misspelled and differs between layers; the shells' misspelled version is the one users see. (LLamaSharpService.cs:58; src/ChatDbg/ChatShell.cs:377; src/ChatDbg.Shell.Gui/ChatShell.cs:330)
- QUIRK-8.34: Load progress is written to standard output rather than the log component, including inside the full-screen terminal interface. (LLamaSharpService.cs:557)
- QUIRK-8.35: Model size is logged with truncating integer division, so any model under one mebibyte logs as '0 MB'. (LLamaSharpService.cs:544-545)
- QUIRK-8.36: Numeric and date formatting follow the ambient locale, so a comma decimal separator can appear inside exported documents; timestamps carry no zone offset. (LLamaSharpService.cs:285, :295-296; LLamaSharpLogConfig.cs:83, :121)
- QUIRK-8.37: The candidate computation fully sorts the entire vocabulary once per generated piece instead of using a partial selection. (LLamaSharpService.cs:459-463)
- QUIRK-8.38: Nothing bounds the accumulated analyses — one record per piece with candidates, a snapshot and a map, retained until the next introspected run, with a token budget up to 8192. (LLamaSharpService.cs:31, :230)
- QUIRK-8.39: Three implemented diagnostic operations have no caller, and the three matching commands are placeholders registered in neither shell, so their notice is unreachable. (LLamaSharpService.cs:628, :633-648, :653-656; Commands/ShowTokenAnalysisCommand.cs:46-48, ExportTokenAnalysisCommand.cs:23, ExportLogsCommand.cs:23)
- QUIRK-8.40: The introspection diagnostics command that users are told to run when probabilities do not appear is written entirely for the cloud back ends, and the enable message warns about 'a compatible model and API version' for a back end with no interface version. (Commands/LogProbsCommand.cs:39-44, :159-207)
- QUIRK-8.41: The adjacent tokenise and inspect commands load their own separate copies of the model at context sizes 512 and 2048 rather than reusing the resident one. (Services/TokenInspectionService.cs:30-41, :104-118)
- QUIRK-8.42: The processor and accelerator backend packages are both referenced unconditionally, so every published output carries both sets of native binaries — which the source's own troubleshooting document names as a cause of native load failures. (src/Xcaciv.ChatDbg.Core/Xcaciv.ChatDbg.Core.csproj:14-15)
